- The E102 row of `skills/notebook/reference/errors.md` and `docs/troubleshooting.md` name the missed-message case.
- **Allowed as before:**
  - no record at all: the `/nh:init` message, whose hook runs before `.nh/` exists (`test_skill_files.py` `test_init_skill_expects_no_turn_record_in_its_own_message`);
  - an orphan record (no human turn): an unknown id newer than it keeps v0.1's behaviour, since a background task can finish during the `/nh:init` message;
  - a known id: the turn, an alias, an earlier alias, or `prev_turn_id`;
  - writer calls: their turn comes from the run (`_writer_turn`, E107).
- V16 found no false D1 hit: an absorbed message keeps the turn's prompt_id, and a new message's record is written before its first tool call. V16 typed into a turn a human message started; a turn a notification started is absorbed by the same rule (its prompt id is an alias, which `known()` accepts).

**Tests:** `tests/unit/test_intent.py` (the classifier table, 60+ phrases), `tests/unit/test_turn_record.py`, `tests/unit/test_ledger.py` (v1 load, v2 save, pending, the `grant()` table), `tests/hooks/test_hook_prompt.py` (fields, `classify_failed`, mid-turn absorption; `RULE` and `QA_REPORT` pins unchanged), `tests/gateway/test_gateway.py` (D1), `tests/nhctl/test_metrics.py` (`turn_absorbed` is not a turn). `tests/fakes/turns.py` gains `Turns.prompt(prompt_id, session_id=None, text="…")`.

### 6.2 Explain-only (FR-9)

Chunk C2 (plan D2, D0 c, D0 e). Files: `policy/errors.py`, `app.py`, `_shared/turn_record.py`, `hooks/nh_hooks/pre_tool.py`, `hooks/nh_hooks/prompt_submit.py`, `skills/explain/SKILL.md` (new), `skills/notebook/SKILL.md`, `skills/notebook/reference/errors.md`, `skills/notebook/reference/replies.md`, `docs/troubleshooting.md`, both READMEs, and the eval cases `explain-only` and `slash-explain`. It is the first reader of the record's `mode` (6.1). The mode that 6.1's classifier sets, `explain`, `plan` or `ask`, means the same here for all three. So a `/nh:plan` message (C6's skill) and a "run next 3" message (C6's batch ask) are no-write turns from C2 on.

**`no_write_mode(record, turn_id)`** (`_shared/turn_record.py`)
- Returns the record's mode (`explain`, `plan` or `ask`) when `turn_id` is the record's `turn_id`.
- Else None: no record, no turn, another turn, an orphan's None turn, a v1 record, or no mode.
- The gateway and the workflow guard both pass the call's canonical turn (`canonical()`), so a notification's alias counts as its human turn.

**E109** (`policy/errors.py`)
- First line: "Not {verb}: no notebook change in an explain, plan or ask message." (`{verb}` as for E107/E108: written, run or undone). §6.0 d's "Meaning" becomes this line in the `Not {verb}:` shape of E107 and E108, and says "message" as the other user-facing refusals do.
- Next: `E109_NEXT[mode]`, the turn's mode:

| Mode | Next |
|---|---|
| `explain` | "Answer in chat with a numbered walkthrough; write nothing this message." |
| `plan` | "Reply with the numbered plan; write nothing this message." |
| `ask` | "Ask the user the one question; write nothing until they reply." |

- A writer's E109 (below) takes `RETURN_TO_WORKFLOW` as its Next instead.

**Main conversation** (`TurnGate.on_call_tool`)
- A call is refused with E109 when all of these hold:
  - no `agent_id` (the main conversation);
  - `_is_write(name, args)`: `nh_add_cell`, `nh_edit_cell`, `nh_undo`, or `nh_run` in mode `run` (the default);
  - a turn record exists, and the call's canonical turn is the record's `turn_id`;
  - the record's `mode` is `explain`, `plan` or `ask`.
- Allowed as before:
  - `nh_inspect` (never gated);
  - `nh_run` in mode `wait` or `interrupt`: they only follow a running cell;
  - any call with no turn record (the `/nh:init` message);
  - a turn with no mode, a "yes" or "go" turn included: E109 never outlives its message;
  - a call of an earlier turn that got past E102 (a known id, 6.1): the record's mode belongs to the newer message.
- **Gate order:** E105, E101, E106, `_writer_turn` (E103, E107, the writer's E109), E104, E102 for an orphan, D1's E102, v0.1's E102, **E109**, E108, then the tool.
  - After E102: a missed or older message still gets "send it again" or "wait", which is the stronger answer.
  - Before E108: an explain message typed while nh:qa-cell writes is told to answer in chat, not to wait for a report.
- **Tighten-only.** The gate reads the mode at call time. A mode absorbed mid-turn (6.1) therefore refuses every later write of that turn, including a retry of a cell written before the message arrived. The cell itself stays: E109 only stops changes.

**nh:cell-writer** (`_writer_turn`)
- After the run checks (E103, E107): when the record's mode is `explain`, `plan` or `ask`, the writer's `nh_add_cell` and `nh_edit_cell` get E109 with Next `RETURN_TO_WORKFLOW` (so `_for_writer` adds no `WRITER_LINE`).
- `nh_run` in mode `wait` passes, so the writer can still follow a cell it wrote before the mode arrived. Its other calls were already E103.
- A run of an earlier turn still ends with E107.

**Workflow launch** (`pre_tool.workflow_guard`, advisory)
- Deny order: a subagent, `approve_before_run`, **the mode**, then one run per message.
- The mode check: an nh:qa-cell launch is denied with `WORKFLOW_MODE_REASON` when its prompt's canonical turn is the record's `turn_id` (`canonical(record, prompt_id) == record["turn_id"]`) and the record's mode is `explain`, `plan` or `ask`. The reason reads: "nh: this user message only asks to explain, plan or ask, so no cell is written in it. Don't launch nh:qa-cell: answer in chat (a numbered walkthrough, the numbered plan or the one question) and write nothing."
- Other launches are unchanged. The deny is advisory; the gateway's writer E109 is the enforcement.

**Reminder** (`prompt_submit`)
- **Order, settled here** (§6.0 f): `[NO_PROMPT_ID]` + head + `drift_line` + `last_cell_line`, clipped at 400. The clip now cuts the tail of `last_cell_line` first.
- **Head:** `human_head(record, absorbed)` = `[RULE]` for a new turn (nothing for an absorbed message), then `mode_parts(record)`.
- **Notification head:** `notification()` returns its first line (`QA_REPORT`, `QA_EARLIER` or `BACKGROUND`, each byte-identical), then `mode_parts` of the record it joins (`aliased()` keeps the mode). In an explain turn the writer's add got E109, so `QA_REPORT`'s "unless its writer wrote none" must not read as leave to write.
- **`mode_parts(record)`:** `[EXPLAIN]` when the record's mode is `explain`, else nothing. This is the seam for C6: its ask-turn part (mode `ask`) and approved-batch part (`answer == "yes"` with `prev_request`) go here.
- **`EXPLAIN`:** "[nh] Explain only this message: a numbered walkthrough in chat, never in the notebook; change nothing."
- **Cases:**

| Message | Head |
|---|---|
| a new explain message | `RULE EXPLAIN` |
| a message absorbed into a turn whose mode is now `explain` | `EXPLAIN` |
| a background task's notification in a turn whose mode is `explain` | its first line, then `EXPLAIN` |
| any other message | as in 6.1 |

- `RULE`, `QA_REPORT` and `NO_PROMPT_ID` stay byte-identical.

**Skill** `skills/explain/SKILL.md` (`/nh:explain`)
- Frontmatter: `name: explain`, a description, `argument-hint`, `disable-model-invocation: true`, `allowed-tools: [mcp__plugin_nh_nh__nh_inspect]`.
- Body:
  - find the cell with `nh_inspect(view="outline")`, and read it with `view="cell"`. A bare number is `[n]`, the execution count, not the outline's row number (which counts note and markdown rows too). With no cell named: the cell the user's question is about, if any; else the last cell the reminder names (a title it cut with `…` matches by prefix) or, when the clip cut that line (a long drift line comes first), the last code cell nh wrote (author `agent` or `agent*` in the outline); else, in a notebook nh wrote no cell of, the last code cell that ran;
  - give a numbered walkthrough in chat, one line group at a time, quoting the code and the real values from its outputs;
  - never write the explanation into the notebook, and change nothing (the gateway refuses with E109 when the message names no change; the rule holds either way);
  - end by offering the next step;
  - plain, junior-level depth by default. C9 adds the preset's depth line through SessionStart (6.9).
- The skill doesn't enforce anything. `/nh:explain <text>` reaches UserPromptSubmit as its raw text (spike V1), so the classifier sets mode `explain` and E109 applies even if the skill never loads, as long as the text names no change verb (6.1: fix, change, add, update, rewrite, refactor, make, even as a noun).
- With a change verb ("/nh:explain the add step", "/nh:explain how to make it faster") the message has no mode: no E109, and the reminder carries `RULE`, not `EXPLAIN`. Only the skill's "change nothing" guards it, so the explain skill says to change nothing either way, both READMEs say nh blocks changes unless the message names one, and `skills/notebook/SKILL.md` and `reference/replies.md` let an explain reply change something only when the message names a change verb ("explain and fix …"). Making a leading `/nh:explain` always set mode `explain` would be a classifier change (plan D0 a), not C2's.
- `skills/notebook/SKILL.md`: the explain skill is user-only (`disable-model-invocation`), so on the plain "explain …" path the model has only this skill, the `EXPLAIN` reminder and `reference/replies.md`. The turn section's step 1 points an explain-only message to the **explain** reply ("no cell"), and that reply says the whole contract: `nh_inspect` the cell, a numbered walkthrough in chat quoting its code with the real values from its output, one proposed next step not taken, never in the notebook, and no change unless the message names a change verb (E109). `reference/replies.md`'s explain row says the same and lists the seven verbs: a change asked for in other words ("… and drop them") stays refused, so the reply proposes it as the next step. `errors.md` and `docs/troubleshooting.md` give E109's cause the same way. `skills/notebook/SKILL.md` stays at 150 lines.

**INSTRUCTIONS** (`app.py`): 2017 → 2017 chars (cap 2048, 31 to spare).

| Line | Chars before → after | Change |
|---|---|---|
| Rule 1 | 177 → 249 | adds "Only exception: a batch or re-run list the user approved when nh asked." (§0) |
| Rule 5 | 191 → 173 | "named intermediate results" → "named intermediates"; "end with something visible to check" → "end with a visible check" |
| Rule 7 | 305 → 217 | the same statuses and actions, in the reminder's short form: "RUNNING or QUEUED cell: tell the user, add nothing. Interrupted: ask before re-running or changing it. Deleted by the user: a no. Lost (kernel gone) or not run (the user typed into it first): tell the user and ask." |
| Rule 9 | 132 → 166 | adds ", or print env vars or credentials" after ".ipynb files" (C4's L011 enforces it) |
| Others | unchanged | rule 10 stays byte-identical (`test_qa_workflow.py`) |

- Rules 1, 5 and 9 alone would reach 2105, over the cap. Rule 7 is the other line §6.0 f changes, so C2 tightens it to fit.
- No explain clause: it wouldn't fit without cutting more. The reminder's `EXPLAIN` part and E109 carry the rule instead.

**Docs:** an E109 row in `skills/notebook/reference/errors.md` and in `docs/troubleshooting.md`. `/nh:explain` goes in `plugins/nh/README.md` (Commands) and in the root README's review step.

**Evals** (authored in C2 and committed before they ran; the first run, CLI 2.1.284 with CI's flags, scored both cases 1.0 on the graders as first written; C2's review fix changed the graders below, and their re-run with the same CLI and flags scored both cases 1.0 again, every grader passing in all 6 runs):
- `explain-only`: "explain …" about cell [1] of the shared fixture;
- `slash-explain`: the same ask as "/nh:explain …".
- Both expect a numbered walkthrough in chat, with no `nh_add_cell`, `nh_edit_cell`, `nh_run` or `nh_undo` call, no Read of the `.ipynb`, and no `.ipynb` edit. The last holds by construction: `allowed_tools` is `[Read, Glob, Grep, Skill]` and CI grants nothing, so the run withholds Write, Edit and NotebookEdit and a grader on them could never fail (`never-edits-ipynb` covers the case where they are granted). Nothing in the eval refuses a write either: the suite mocks answer `nh_add_cell`, `nh_edit_cell`, `nh_run` and `nh_undo` with success, and the PreToolUse hook only stamps.
- Graders (a run scores passed weight / total weight, a case the mean of its 3 runs, CI's threshold is 0.8):

| Grader | Type | Weight | Checks |
|---|---|---|---|
| `no-writes` | regex on `mock_calls`, `not_contains`, `arm: both` | 15 | no call of the four write tools: `"tool":\s*"mcp__plugin_nh_nh__(?:nh_add_cell\|nh_edit_cell\|nh_run\|nh_undo)"` (each name whole, for the tool-name lint) |
| `walkthrough` | llm on the last message | 5 | a numbered walkthrough of the cell's parts with its real numbers, nothing written or invented. The judge sees only the last message, so the rubric carries the cell's whole output: without it, a run that also cited the 25 order dates failed all three votes as "invented numbers". `slash-explain` also asks for a next step offered and not taken: there the message is the explain skill's command, and the skill asks for one. On the plain path only the notebook skill asks for it, and a run may answer from the `EXPLAIN` reminder alone, which doesn't, so `explain-only`'s judge would fail a run that followed its text |
| `numbered-steps` | regex on the last message, `flags: i` | 1 | a line that is step 3 of a list: `3.`, `3)`, `3:`, `3 —`, `3 - `, `**3**`, `Step 3`, after optional indent, `- `, `#`s and `**`; not `3.5%`, `3 rows`, `2023.` or `Step 30` |
| `uses-real-numbers` | regex on the last message | 1 | `\b43\b`, the row count |
| `no-read-ipynb` | `tool_used` Read, `input_match: '\.ipynb'`, max 0 | 1 | the skill's "never Read the `.ipynb`" (a hook-denied call still counts) |
| `skill-registered` (`slash-explain` only) | regex on the trace, `arm: with-only` | 1 | the init line's skills list names `nh:explain`: `"skills":\[[^\]]*"nh:explain"`. A `-p` trace never holds the expanded command (checked on a kept 2.1.284 trace), so whether the skill's text loaded can't be graded; a heading grader failed every run. CI runs `--ablation none`, which scores `with-only` graders too |

- Why these weights: with W for `no-writes`, J for the judge and 3 or 4 other weight-1 graders (total T), one writing run in three fails the case when W > 0.6 T (15/23 and 15/24); a judge failing every run fails it when J > 0.2 T (5/23, 5/24); one cheap miss passes (22/23); a reply that does nothing but inspect fails (16/23, 17/24), and one judge fail in three runs passes (0.93). One grader per write tool can't do this: failing a single write then needs each of the four to outweigh everything else, which lifts a do-nothing run over 0.8. `tests/unit/test_skill_files.py` replays these scenarios on the case files.

**Tests:**
- `tests/gateway/test_explain_only.py`: E109's first line and the three Next lines as literals; E109 for add, edit, run and undo in an explain turn, each with the right Next; wait and interrupt allowed (no refusal, their own machine line); plan and ask turns refused; the next plain turn allowed; a mid-turn explain refusing a later retry in the same turn; the mode keyed on the call's canonical turn (an explain turn's notification alias gets E109, an earlier turn's alias its own E110); the writer's E109 with its wait still allowed; add, run, edit and undo with no record allowed; the gate order at both ends of E109: a plan-mode call gets E104 and a writer whose run was already reported gets E107, neither E109.
- `tests/unit/test_turn_record.py`: the `no_write_mode` table.
- `tests/unit/test_intent.py`: 6.1's lead marks (quotes, markdown, brackets, a BOM, a zero-width space) before "explain" or `/nh:explain`, and what they don't cover ("1. explain", "_explain_", a change verb, a quoted `/nh:plan`).
- `tests/gateway/test_gateway.py`: `test_known_ids_pass_d1` absorbs a plain message instead of an explain one, since an explain one now blocks the write it checks.
- `tests/hooks/test_hook_workflow.py`: the mode deny (its reason pinned) in explain, plan and ask turns, for an alias and a mode typed mid-message; the mode checked before one run per message, and after the subagent and `approve_before_run` denies (each keeps its own reason); other launches are allowed.
- `tests/hooks/test_hook_prompt.py`: the new order and clip, the `EXPLAIN` pin, the absorbed-explain head, and a notification's head in an explain turn.
- `tests/unit/test_skill_files.py`: the skill set gains `explain`; the explain skill's frontmatter; both cases' `nh_inspect` mocks match the real gateway's cell view byte for byte (their graders read its numbers), and both cases' own `scaffold.sh` builds the 3-cell fixture with no `last_cell.json`; the grader weights replayed on the scoring scenarios above; `numbered-steps` and `no-writes` against sample answers and call logs; `skill-registered` against sample init lines (and not a reply quoting one); both rubrics carry their mock's whole output; `replies.md`, `errors.md` and `troubleshooting.md` list the classifier's seven change verbs, and the notebook skill's explain reply carries the contract; every §6.0 d code the gateway has gets its `errors.md` and `troubleshooting.md` rows.
- INSTRUCTIONS pins (in `test_explain_only.py`): rules 1, 5, 7 and 9 as changed here, rules 1-10 in order, and at most 2048 chars.

**Perf** (50 interleaved runs, d1cc942 vs C2 after its review, p50/p95 in ms; prompt-submit on an explain message, workflow on a plain message's launch): system 3.9 prompt-submit 117.4/125.7 → 117.5/127.4, workflow 121.2/132.1 → 119.9/129.2; server venv 3.13 prompt-submit 83.0/91.1 → 83.9/90.6, workflow 87.4/97.4 → 86.7/102.8. All under the 150 ms budget. The review fix's lead-mark skip adds 4.7 µs per message to `classify()` on system 3.9 (36.7 → 41.4 µs, five messages up to 4000 chars). Its end-to-end run (50 interleaved runs of prompt-submit, system 3.9) had the machine at load average ~7 from another app holding a core: 8882db8 127.6/161.9, the fix 129.7/154.2. Both are over 150 only from that load, and the fix is no slower.

### 6.3 /nh:plan and the approved batch (FR-10)

Chunk C6 (plan D3; D0 a–c and e as they apply to a batch; plan defaults 1, 3, 4, 5, 6 and 13), built as three micro-steps. This section is written for all three in C6a, so C6b and C6c implement against it.

| Step | Builds | Files |
|---|---|---|
| C6a | the gateway: the grant, E110's cap, the stop rules and E123, the batch's machine line and next blocks (the writer's included), `[turn] max_batch` | `_shared/turn_record.py` (`approved_batch`), `policy/turn.py` (`TurnState`, `NotebookLocks.hold_turn`), `policy/errors.py` (E123, `NhError(head=…)`), `tools/batch.py` (new), `tools/write.py`, `tools/run.py`, `tools/undo.py`, `tools/common.py` (`machine_line`, `record_finish`), `render.py` (`next_block`), `config.py`, `defaults.toml`, `skills/notebook/reference/errors.md`, `docs/harness-toml.md`, `docs/troubleshooting.md` |
| C6b | the hooks and the writer: the reminder's ask-turn and approved-batch parts, `workflow_guard`'s N launches, per-slot writer runs, the writer column of the batch matrix | `hooks/nh_hooks/common.py` (`max_batch`, `headless`), `hooks/nh_hooks/prompt_submit.py`, `hooks/nh_hooks/pre_tool.py`, `_shared/turn_record.py` (`valid_max_batch`, `MAX_BATCH_RANGE`), `_shared/tomlread.py`, `config.py`, `meta.py` (`run_metadata`), `policy/turn.py`, `tools/batch.py`, `tools/write.py`, `reference/qa-workflow.md`, `docs/troubleshooting.md`, `docs/harness-toml.md`; `workflows/qa-cell.js` and `agents/cell-writer.md` unchanged (the writer needn't see its slot: below) |
| C6c | the plan skill, `planning.md`'s format and batch path, the docs, the three evals; from its review and eval rounds, the plan reminder part, `ASK_BATCH`'s words and the going next block's words | `skills/plan/SKILL.md` (new), `skills/notebook/SKILL.md` (its planning.md pointer line and the big-ask rule's wording), `reference/planning.md`, both READMEs, `docs/troubleshooting.md`, `.gitignore`, `evals/plan-no-code`, `evals/batch-asks-once`, `evals/batch-stops-on-check-this` (new), `evals/big-ask-plans/graders/no-load-step.md` (new), `hooks/nh_hooks/prompt_submit.py` (`PLAN`, `ASK_BATCH`, `ASK_BATCH_CAP`), `render.py` (`_batch_next`), `tests/unit/test_skill_files.py`, `tests/hooks/test_hook_prompt.py`, `tests/unit/test_r2_render.py`, `tests/gateway/test_r3_gateway.py` |

**The flow** (user decisions for FR-10, 2026-09-28; any preset)

| Message | Classifier (6.1) | nh | Claude |
|---|---|---|---|
| `/nh:plan <goal>` | mode `plan` | E109 for every write (6.2) | the plan skill: 5–12 numbered cell-sized steps, in chat only (plan default 5) |
| an edit of the plan ("drop step 4") | nothing | as any message | re-prints the list; advisory, nh doesn't check it (plan default 6) |
| "go" | answer yes, no request | a normal message: one cell | step 1 (or the step the last reply proposed) |
| "run the next 3", "run steps 3-5" | mode `ask`, request `{batch, n}` | E109 for every write | asks one question in chat, "Run steps 3–5 in one reply?", for at most `max_batch` steps; writes nothing |
| a whole-message yes ("go" alone too, plan default 1) | answer yes, `prev_request` the batch | the grant: up to `min(n, max_batch)` new cells this message | writes the steps in order, one cell each, a short report after each; stops at the first error or "check this" |
| "no" after the question | answer no | no grant | writes nothing; asks what to do instead (C6c, `planning.md`) |
| anything else after the question ("go on", "yes, but …") | not yes | no grant: one cell, as always | |
| a new ask after the question ("yes, but only run steps 1-2", "no, run the next 2") | mode `ask`, a new request | E109 for every write | asks again, once |

- **Plan mode writes nothing (confirmed, C2).** `turn_record.no_write_mode` returns `plan` and `ask` as well as `explain`, so the main conversation's add, edit, re-run and undo get E109 with `E109_NEXT["plan"]` or `["ask"]`, a writer's add and edit get E109 (`_writer_turn`), and `workflow_guard` denies an nh:qa-cell launch (`test_explain_only.py` and `test_hook_workflow.py` pin plan and ask turns). C6 adds no check for them.
- **INSTRUCTIONS** stay as C2 left them: rule 1 already ends "Only exception: a batch or re-run list the user approved when nh asked." (6.2's per-line counts). No tool description changes ("Once per user message" stays in `nh_add_cell`'s): the batch's own next block and reminder part say what to do, so no tool snapshot changes (no Gs).

**No pending question for a batch ask** (decided in C6a)
- The gateway records nothing in the ledger for "run the next 3". The plan's grant rule is "answer == yes and prev_request is set", and the record already holds what was asked:

| Why | |
|---|---|
| nothing to record it from | the ask turn is mode `ask`, so every write is refused by E109 in `TurnGate`, before any tool runs; no gated call reaches the ledger |
| the record is the question's identity | the hook writes `request` into the ask turn's record, and the next message's record carries it as `prev_request` (6.1), so it lives exactly one message, like a pending question whose `turn_id` must be `prev_turn_id` |
| one shape for questions nh asks itself | `pending` stays `cell` \| `rerun` (6.1): hashes of calls nh refused. A batch question is the user's own request echoed back; there is no call to hash |

- So a batch question can't be "already waiting" (E122's waiting row) and never drops a cell question: they are separate records. How the two meet is under "Cell approvals in a batch" below.
- The gateway can't see whether Claude actually asked. A "yes" after a reply that asked something else still grants the batch the user's own previous message requested (Known gaps).

**The grant** (`tools/batch.py` `decide()`, inside `svc.locks.hold`)
- The turn's batch is decided once, by its first gated call: the first `nh_add_cell`, `nh_edit_cell` or `nh_run(mode="run")` of the turn that reaches the gateway's write path, from the main conversation or a writer of the turn's run. (`nh_undo` neither decides a batch nor counts as a step, but it stops a going one and gets E123 after a stop: below. `nh_run` wait and interrupt are not gated writes.) The decision is stored in `TurnState.batch_total` and never changes in that turn:

| `batch_total` | Means |
|---|---|
| `None` | not decided yet (no gated call yet; a ledger written before C6) |
| `0` | no batch: E110's cap is `[turn] max_code_cells`, as before |
| `N` ≥ 1 | the approved batch: up to N new cells this message |

- `N = min(n, [turn] max_batch)` when all of these hold, else `0`:

| Condition | Why |
|---|---|
| the call's canonical turn is the record's `turn_id` (`turn_record.approved_batch(record, turn_id)`) | a notification's alias counts as its human turn; a call of an earlier turn gets no batch |
| the record's `answer` is `yes` | its opening message: a yes typed mid-turn is never an answer (6.1), so "same turn" never grants |
| the record's `prev_request` is `{batch, n}` | the previous message asked for the batch; two messages later it is gone ("prev" is one message back) |
| no question of the previous message is pending (`ledger.pending(session)` with `turn_id == prev_turn_id`) | first ask wins: one yes answers one question. Both can exist only when the batch request was typed mid-turn into a message whose cell nh had asked about (6.1's absorbed request); the yes then answers that cell's question (6.4) and grants no batch |
| not headless (`config.headless()`) | no yes can arrive (6.0 b): a scripted "yes" grants nothing, as for E122 |

- **Single use.** A grant belongs to its yes message: the next message's `prev_request` is the yes message's own request (None for "yes"), so a second yes later grants nothing. Within the message, the batch is decided once, so a cell grant used later in it (6.4) can't open a batch afterwards.
- **Locking.** `decide()` runs inside `svc.locks.hold(notebook, turn)` for add, edit and re-run, and an undo's stop and E123 inside the undo's own `hold`. E125 is refused before the notebook is resolved (6.8, pinned), so its stop, or its E123 after a stop (below), is decided under `svc.locks.hold_turn(turn)`, the same per-turn lock `hold()` takes first, which needs no notebook. Every grant check and slot use happens under a lock (6.0 b).

**E110's cap** (`tools/write.py` `add_cell`)
- `len(state.claims) >= (state.batch_total or [turn] max_code_cells)` refuses with E110, so a batch of N allows N claimed cells; after the batch's last cell, E110 as usual. With `max_code_cells` raised by hand, a batch still caps at its N: the user approved that many.
- E110 after a full batch has its own first line, so it doesn't claim one cell per message: `Not written (by design): the approved batch's N cells are written; the last is {cell}.` (`batch.FULL_HEAD`, through `NhError(head=…)`), then the detail line `- The rest of the plan waits for the user's next message.` (`batch.FULL_LINE`) and E110's Next ("Don't write more cells. Reply with the remaining steps as a numbered list and ask which to do next."; a writer's: `RETURN_TO_WORKFLOW`).
- Only E110's cap changes. A batch is new cells: an edit of a cell this message didn't claim still gets E113, a re-run of one E114, an edit of an OK cell E112 (a writer's revision excepted, as before).
- **One cell per nh:qa-cell run** (C6a in a batch; C6b everywhere, per slot: "Per-slot writer runs" below): a writer whose run already owns a cell of this message gets E110 with the first line `Not written (by design): one new cell per nh:qa-cell run, and this run already wrote one.` (`batch.RUN_HEAD`) and `RETURN_TO_WORKFLOW`. Before C6a the cap of 1 did this; a cap of N must not let one run (one QA pass) write N cells. Another run's cell is `refuse_other_run`'s (6.4's "- Another nh:qa-cell run owns this message's cell.", with C6b's batch first lines).
- **Each step waits for the last one's result** (`batch.enter`, inside the lock, before E110; edits and re-runs too). In a going batch a claimed cell reads `running` until its report is built: `record_finish` (the run's done callback in `tools/common.py`) leaves a going batch's claimed cell to `batch.reported`, which sets its status and the stop together once its `check this` is known.

| A claimed step at the next gated call | Result |
|---|---|
| `running` (its result isn't in yet: a call sent in parallel) | E133 `The kernel is busy with step k of the approved batch, whose result isn't in yet.`, Next `Wait for step k's result; send this call again only if that result says to go on.` (a writer's: `RETURN_TO_WORKFLOW`). Nothing is written and the batch goes on: that result then stops it, or the call is sent again |
| neither `ok` nor `running`, at an `nh_add_cell` (the user typed into it before it ran: `conflict`) | the batch stops at that step (`not_ok`) and the call gets E123 |
| `ok`, every one | the call goes on to the next check |

  - In order, one at a time, none of this happens: each step's result arrives before the next call, and a non-OK one already stopped the batch. A full batch's next add skips it and gets E110.

**The stop rules**
- `TurnState.batch_stop` is 0 while the batch goes, else the step k it stopped at (1 ≤ k ≤ N). Once set it stays for the message (plan default 3: no retry of the failing cell in that reply).

| Cause | Where | k |
|---|---|---|
| a result's status isn't `ok`: `error`, `running`, `queued`, `aborted`, `timeout`, `interrupted`, `deleted`, `lost` | `report_run` (`batch.reported`), for add, edit, re-run, wait and interrupt alike | the reported cell's place in `state.claims` (a cell this message didn't claim, such as an earlier message's cell it waited for: the steps written so far, at least 1) |
| a result with a non-empty `check this` section | `report_run` | as above |
| an E12x refusal of a gated write: E120, E121, E122, E124 (C7), E125 | `batch.refused`, inside the lock (E125: `batch.refused_early`, under `hold_turn`) | the step the call would have written: the target's place in `state.claims` (E125 on an edit, before the notebook is read: its `cell_id` as passed, when that is one of the message's cells), or the steps written so far plus one; at most N |
| an `nh_undo` that removes or restores a cell, or finds nh's cell already deleted (E142) | `batch.undone`, inside the undo's lock | the undone cell's place in `state.claims`, or the steps written so far plus one; at most N |
| an earlier step neither `ok` nor `running` at the next `nh_add_cell` | `batch.enter` | that step's place |

- A report in a going batch sets the cell's status and the stop together, after its `check this` is known (`batch.reported`; `record_finish` leaves the status to it), so a parallel call can't see the step OK before its check is in: it sees `running` and gets E133 (above).
- Not stops: E110, E112, E113, E114, E133 (a busy kernel, or a step whose result isn't in yet: wait, then call again) and the other non-E12x refusals, which change nothing a batch depends on; E109 (a mode typed mid-message refuses every later write anyway); and E120 for an `nh_run` mode other than run, wait or interrupt (a malformed call: it reaches no lock and writes nothing).
- **E12x stops it too** (plan D3, "any E12x also stops it"): a refused step means the plan's next cell isn't in the notebook as approved, and the steps after it build on it. A lint refusal (E120) is the model's to fix in a normal message; in a batch it stops the run instead, so no rewrite happens unseen. Whether E120 and E121 should stop a batch is a call for V14 (Known gaps).
- **An undo stops it too**: the steps after an undone cell would build on a notebook without it. The undo itself goes through (it is the user's way back), its next block adds `The approved batch stops here, at step k of N: say which planned steps did not run.`, and later calls, undos included, get E123.
- **The refusal that stops the batch** (`batch.refused`) gets the detail line `- The approved batch stops here, at step k of N: nh changes nothing more this message.` before its Next. E120, E121 and E125 get `batch.REFUSED_NEXT` in place of their Next (E120's and E125's say to fix and call again; E121's asks the user how to proceed without the batch's report): "Don't call again: the approved batch stops here. Tell the user what each step did, which step nh refused and why, and what you would change; then wait." E122 keeps its own Next (ask nh's question, then stop). A writer's refusal keeps `RETURN_TO_WORKFLOW`. A refusal after the stop is E123, not this: E125, refused before the lock, becomes E123 once the batch has stopped (`batch.refused_early`).

**E123** (`policy/errors.py`)
- First line: "Not {verb}: the approved {what} stopped at step {step}; nh changes nothing more this message." (`{what}`: `batch`, or C7's `re-run`; `{step}`: "k of N"; `{verb}`: written for add and edit, run for `nh_run`, undone for `nh_undo`).
- Next: "Report the batch to the user: what each step did, then where and why it stopped (the error, the 'check this' finding or nh's question). No retry and no new cell this message; wait for the user." A writer's: `RETURN_TO_WORKFLOW`.
- Raised inside the lock, after `review_earlier_cells` and before every other check of the write path (`batch.enter`; `nh_undo`: after its E115), for every `nh_add_cell`, `nh_edit_cell`, `nh_run(mode="run")` and `nh_undo` of the message once `batch_stop` is set: new cells, retries, a writer's revision, an undo. E125 comes first (6.8: before the notebook is resolved), and after a stop it is E123 too (`batch.refused_early`, under `hold_turn`). E123 itself stops nothing more.
- **Gate order** (add): E125 (E123 after a stop), then in the lock E123, the waiting step's E133 and the `not_ok` stop, E110 (a writer's other-run and one-cell-per-run checks first, C6b's per-slot rules, then the cap), E133, E121, E120, E122. Edit: E125 (E123 after a stop), then E123 and the waiting step's E133, then 6.4's order (a writer's per-slot checks where 6.4 had `refuse_other_run`, and right after the target is read). Re-run: E123 and the waiting step's E133, then its own order. Undo: E115, then E123, then its own order.

**The per-cell result** (`report_run`)
- **Machine line** (`tools/common.py` `machine_line`): `turn=k/N batch` in place of `turn=k/max_code_cells` while the message has a batch (k: the cells it claimed): `nh: cell=nh-… exec=7 turn=2/3 batch retries=0/2 waits=0/2 undos=0/3`. Anything that reads the line by key (`qa-cell.js` reads only `revisions=`) is unaffected.
- **Next block** (`render.next_block(status, …, batch=(k, N, s), check_this=…)`; `s` is `batch_stop` after this report, 0 while the batch goes). A cell this message didn't claim (an earlier message's cell it waited for: k = 0) gets the plain block while the batch goes and the "stopped earlier" rows once it has stopped, so no block offers a retry that E123 would refuse. Main conversation:

| Case | Next |
|---|---|
| going, `ok`, k < N | "Step k of N of the approved batch ran OK. Give the user a short report on {cell}: what it did and the real numbers, surprises first, named by title and [n]. Then write the batch's next step (step k+1 of N) with nh_add_cell, without waiting for the user." k counts the batch's own cells, so this names no plan step (C6c review: C6a's "write step k+1 of the plan" named an already-written step when the batch didn't start at plan step 1, as after "go", then "run the next 3"); `planning.md` maps batch steps to plan steps |
| going, `ok`, k = N | "That was step N of N, the last of the approved batch." + the usual OK block, ending "Do not write another cell." |
| stopped here (s = k), `ok` with `check this` | "The approved batch stops at step k of N: {cell} ran, but its result needs a look (see 'check this'). Reply to the user about it: lead with the 'check this' finding, then what the cell did and the real numbers, and say which planned steps did not run. Write no other cell and don't change this one in this message; wait for the user." |
| stopped earlier (s ≠ k), `ok` with `check this` | "{Cell} ran, but its result needs a look (see 'check this'), and the approved batch stopped at step s of N. Reply to the user: lead with the 'check this' finding, then what each step did and where and why the batch stopped. Write no other cell and don't change this one in this message; wait for the user." |
| stopped, `ok` without `check this` (a wait after a `running` stop, a result after a parallel E125) | "{Cell} ran OK, but the approved batch stopped at step s of N. Report the batch to the user: what each step did, then where and why it stopped. Write no other cell; wait for the user." |
| stopped here, `error` | "The approved batch stops at step k of N: {Cell} failed. Don't fix it in this message: a batch has no retries. Explain in plain words: quote the failing code, what Python said, the likely cause and one fix; say which planned steps did not run; then wait." |
| stopped earlier, `error` | "{Cell} failed, and the approved batch stopped at step s of N. Don't fix it in this message: a batch has no retries. Explain in plain words: quote the failing code, what Python said, the likely cause and one fix; say where and why the batch stopped and which planned steps did not run; then wait." |
| stopped, any other status | that status's usual block, then "The approved batch stops at step k of N: say which planned steps did not run." (stopped earlier: "stopped at step s"; `running` and `queued` keep their one wait) |

  - At the batch's last step (s = k = N) every planned step ran, so the "stopped here" texts drop "say which planned steps did not run" (the other-status one ends "The approved batch stops at step N of N.").
- **nh:cell-writer's next block** (`_writer_next`): unchanged while the batch goes (each step's run returns its result to the workflow; C6b's slots). Once it has stopped, nothing more changes this message, so the writer gets no retry and no revision:

| Case | Next |
|---|---|
| stopped, `error` | "{Cell} failed, so the approved batch stops here, at step k of N: a batch has no retries. Return this whole result to the workflow as your final answer (status error). Don't change it again." (stopped earlier: "{Cell} failed, and the approved batch stopped at step s of N: …") |
| stopped here, `ok` with `check this` | "{Cell} ran, but its result needs a look (see 'check this'), so the approved batch stops here, at step k of N: no revision of it this message. Return this whole result to the workflow as your final answer (status ok). Don't reply to the user or write a second cell." |
| stopped, any other `ok` | "{Cell} ran OK, but the approved batch stopped at step s of N: no revision of it this message. Return this whole result to the workflow as your final answer (status ok). Don't reply to the user or write a second cell." |
| stopped, any other status | the writer's usual block, then "The approved batch stops at step k of N." (stopped earlier: "stopped at step s") |

**Cell approvals in a batch** (6.4; the composition, decided in C6a)

| Situation | Result |
|---|---|
| a step that asks nothing | written as the next step; `gate_cell` does nothing for it |
| a step that asks (E122) | the question is recorded as in 6.4 (pending, this message's turn) and the batch stops at that step: the reply reports the steps that ran and asks nh's question, then stops; later calls get E123 |
| the next message is a yes | 6.4's grant: that exact call is written once, as that message's one cell. No batch: the yes message's `prev_request` is None. The rest of the plan needs a new "run the next N" |
| a yes that could answer a cell question and a batch request (the request typed mid-turn into the asking message) | first ask wins: the cell call is granted, the batch is not (the grant's table) |
| the yes message of a batch | holds no question of the previous message (the table above), so 6.4's "held" row can't arise in a batch |
| headless | no batch, and E122 records nothing (6.4) |

**Events** (`.nh/log.jsonl`; never prompt text)

| Event | Fields |
|---|---|
| `batch_granted` | `session_id`, `turn_id`, `n` (asked), `total` (N) |
| `batch_not_granted` | `session_id`, `turn_id`, `n`, `reason`: `pending` (first ask wins) or `headless`; only for a record that asked for one |
| `batch_stopped` | `session_id`, `turn_id`, `step`, `total`, `reason`: the status, `check_this`, `not_ok` (an earlier step at the next add), `undo` or the refusal's code |

**Config** (`[turn] max_batch`, plan default 4)
- `defaults.toml`: `max_batch = 5` under `[turn]`, commented "the most steps one approved batch writes ("run the next N", after the user's yes)". `docs/harness-toml.md` documents it in `[turn]`.
- `config.load`: an integer from 2 to 20 (`turn_record.valid_max_batch`, C6b; C6a took any integer ≥ 1). A bool, a float, a string, anything outside 2–20 falls back to 5 with the problem "turn.max_batch must be an integer from 2 to 20" (a string or a bool is already `_merge`'s "turn.max_batch must be int"; the value stays 5).

| Bound | Why (C6b) |
|---|---|
| at least 2 | a batch request is n ≥ 2 (6.1); a cap of 1 would dress a one-cell message as a batch ("up to 1 new cells", "ask about the first 1") |
| at most 20 | `workflow_guard` counts a batch's launches in the run registry, which keeps the session's last `turn_record.MAX_RUNS` (20) runs: past 20 its count could never reach k, and the gateway's `RUN_OPEN_HEAD` would miss an owning run the registry dropped |

- The hooks (C6b) read it from `harness.toml` the same way (`common.setting`, then the same `valid_max_batch` check, default 5).
- `harness.toml` that isn't valid UTF-8 (C6b): `config.load` records the problem "harness.toml unreadable: …" and reads defaults, as for a TOML error (before, the `UnicodeDecodeError` escaped `load`); `tomlread.load` (the hooks, nhctl) reads it as no file.

**Docs** (C6a)
- `reference/errors.md` (model-facing): an E123 row (why the batch stopped, an undo included; report each step, no retry, no new cell, no re-run, no undo; the writer returns it); E110's row says a yes to "run the next N" allows N cells; E133's row adds the batch's waiting step (send it again only if the last result says to go on).
- `docs/troubleshooting.md`: an E123 row; E110's row quotes the batch's first line and names a cell beyond the batch's N or a second cell from one run; E133's row adds the batch's waiting step.
- `docs/harness-toml.md`: `max_batch` in `[turn]`.

**C6b: the hooks and the writer (built in C6b)**
- **`turn_record.approved_batch(record, turn_id) -> int | None`** (C6a, Python 3.9): the `n` of the previous message's batch request when `turn_id` is the record's turn and its answer is yes. The gateway's `decide()` and both hooks use it. Both add "not headless"; only the gateway adds "no pending question" (the hooks don't read the ledger: Known gaps).
- **Hook settings** (`hooks/nh_hooks/common.py`, stdlib, 3.9): `max_batch(data)` is `[turn] max_batch` from `settings()` (harness.toml), checked as `config.load` checks it (`turn_record.valid_max_batch`): an integer from 2 to 20 and not a bool, else 5 (a float, a string, a bool, anything outside 2–20, a missing or unreadable file, one that isn't valid UTF-8). No `NH_*` variable sets it, so hooks and gateway agree. `headless()` is `NH_HEADLESS == "1"`, the check `workflow_guard` already made for `approve_before_run`. The prompt hook reads `harness.toml` only for a part that shows a number (an ask with a batch request, a yes to one); `workflow_guard` reuses the settings it loads for `approve_before_run`.
- **Reminder** (`prompt_submit.mode_parts`, §6.0 f order: `[NO_PROMPT_ID]` + head + mode part + `drift_line` + `last_cell_line`, clipped at 400; `RULE`, `QA_REPORT`, `NO_PROMPT_ID` and C2's `MODE_PARTS` pin byte-identical). `max` is `[turn] max_batch` as the hooks read it, `k = min(n, max)`; the first matching row gives the part:

| Record | Part | Text |
|---|---|---|
| mode `explain` | `EXPLAIN` (6.2) | unchanged |
| mode `ask`, headless | `ASK_HEADLESS` | "[nh] No one can answer here (NH_HEADLESS=1): write nothing, and tell the user this needs their yes in an interactive session." Headless no yes can arrive and the gateway grants no batch (6.0 b), so the part neither promises one nor asks (as E122's headless Next); the gateway's E109 still refuses every write |
| mode `ask` with a batch request | `ASK_BATCH` | "[nh] Ask, don't write: one question in chat, "Run steps a-b in one reply?" (one cell per step), for the plan steps the user asked for{cap}; then stop." `{cap}` is " (at most {max}: ask about the first {max})" when n > max, else empty. The record keeps only `{batch, n}` ("run steps 7-9" is n = 3, with no start), so the part names no step numbers and no "next"; Claude takes them from the user's words and the plan. C6c's eval rounds added "(one cell per step)": next to `RULE`'s "One new cell this turn", one batch-asks-once run asked whether the steps should "go into a single cell" and offered only "go" for step 1. To keep the head in 400, C6c also shortened the cap clause (C6b's was " (nh runs at most {max} at once: ask about the first {max})") |
| mode `ask` otherwise (C7's re-run request, until C7 words its own) | `ASK` | "[nh] Ask, don't write: one question in chat, then stop." |
| mode `plan` | `PLAN` (C6c) | "[nh] Plan only this message: numbered steps in chat, never in the notebook; change nothing." C6b had none (the plan skill and E109 carried it); C6c's review added it, so a plan message's reminder no longer offers only `RULE`'s "One new cell this turn" while E109 refuses every write. Outside `MODE_PARTS`, which stays pinned as C2 left it |
| no mode, `approved_batch()` gives n, not headless; the message is absorbed (typed mid-turn) and classified as a no (a whole-message "stop", "no", "cancel", …) | `BATCH_STOP` | "[nh] The user said stop: write no more batch steps this reply; report what ran and wait." Advisory: the gateway doesn't stop the batch for it (Known gaps) |
| the same, absorbed and not a no | nothing | as before C6b (a message typed into the reply has no budget of its own); the yes message's head and each notification carry `BATCH` |
| no mode, `approved_batch()` gives n, not headless | `BATCH` | "[nh] Approved batch, for this reply in place of one new cell: up to {k} new cells, one plan step each, in order (ultracode: one nh:qa-cell run per step, each after the last report), a short report after each; stop at the first error or 'check this' and wait." |
| anything else | nothing | as 6.2 |

  - **The cap clause** (C6b): §6.3 first had "… ask about the first {max} of them)". Without a prompt id, `NO_PROMPT_ID` + `RULE` + that `ASK_BATCH` came to 401–403 chars (max 2–20), so the clip would have cut the part itself; the shorter clause kept it at 395 at most. C6c's "(one cell per step)" and shorter cap clause (" (at most {max}: ask about the first {max})") bring it to 397–399. Every head of a message with a prompt id fits in 400 whole: the longest, `QA_REPORT` + `BATCH`, is 397–398 (k 2–20), so the clip cuts only `drift_line` and `last_cell_line`, as before (`test_hook_prompt.py` pins the longest heads). Without a prompt id a human message's record has no turn, so it gets no `BATCH`, and `NO_PROMPT_ID` + `RULE` + its part fits (397–399 with `ASK_BATCH`'s cap clause; 397 at the default `max_batch`, pinned); a notification's `NO_PROMPT_ID` + first line + `BATCH` (494–552), and some with `ASK_BATCH` or `ASK_HEADLESS` (up to 477), do not, and the clip cuts the part: nh refuses every write of such a session anyway (`NO_PROMPT_ID`).
  - **The clip** (C6b, refining 6.2's): the part that runs past 400 is cut with "…" as before, but left out, with every part after it, when fewer than 24 of its characters would show (`prompt_submit.CLIP_MIN_CHARS`): `QA_REPORT` + `BATCH` leaves 1–2, and a batch step's report would otherwise end in a stub like "L…". Each part is redacted before the cut (6.8's order: redact, then clip).
  - **Heads** (6.2's rule; an alias keeps the record's `answer` and `prev_request`, so a notification sees the yes message's batch):

| Message | Head |
|---|---|
| a new message `/nh:plan <goal>` | `RULE PLAN` (C6c) |
| a new message "run the next 3" | `RULE ASK_BATCH` |
| the same with `NH_HEADLESS=1` | `RULE ASK_HEADLESS` |
| the next message "yes" (or "go" alone), interactive | `RULE BATCH` |
| the same with `NH_HEADLESS=1` | `RULE` (the gateway grants nothing headless) |
| typed into the yes message while Claude works: a whole-message no | `BATCH_STOP` |
| typed into it: anything else with no mode ("please stop", "wait", "also plot it") | nothing, as before C6b: `BATCH` would read as leave to go on, so the user's own words stand alone (Known gaps) |
| typed into it: an explain, plan or ask message | that mode's part (6.1: tighten only; the gateway's E109 refuses the batch's later writes) |
| a background task's notification in the yes message | its first line (`QA_REPORT`, `QA_EARLIER` or `BACKGROUND`), then `BATCH` |
| a notification in the ask message | its first line, then `ASK_BATCH` (headless: `ASK_HEADLESS`) |

  - `RULE` and `QA_REPORT` stay byte-identical, so `BATCH` says itself that it replaces the one new cell for this reply; after `QA_REPORT` ("write no new cell unless its writer wrote none", still true of the main conversation's own cells under ultracode), `BATCH`'s ultracode clause is what lets the next step's run launch.
  - `BATCH_STOP` shows with the absorbed message only: 6.1 keeps no trace of a typed no in the record (its answer is never applied), so a later notification of that message shows `BATCH` again (Known gaps). The batch part shows while the record says yes; after a gateway stop E123 holds (the hook doesn't read the ledger).
  - A message absorbed into an orphan's turn (6.1) shows its mode's part as any absorbed message does: "run the next 3" typed there gets `ASK_BATCH`, not `RULE`.
- **`workflow_guard`** (advisory; the gateway enforces). Deny order unchanged: a subagent, `approve_before_run`, the mode, then the run count:

| Message | Its runs | Launch |
|---|---|---|
| `approved_batch()` gives n and not headless: a batch of `k = min(n, max)` | fewer than k, none open | allowed |
| | k or more | denied: `WORKFLOW_BATCH_DONE_REASON` |
| | fewer than k, one open | denied: `WORKFLOW_BATCH_OPEN_REASON` |
| any other message | none | allowed |
| | one or more | denied: `WORKFLOW_AGAIN_REASON` (unchanged) |

  - **A message's runs** come from the data the guard already read: the run registry (`.nh/state/workflows/<session>.json`, written by the PostToolUse hook at each launch) entries with `turn_id` equal to the launch's canonical turn and `is_own_run` (nh's script, launched by name). **Open** is `turn_record.run_open`: no `done_ts` and launched under an hour ago. **Reported** is a `done_ts`, set by the run's task notification (`mark_done`, from `prompt_submit`) or by a TaskStop (status `killed`: a stopped run counts as one of the k and is no longer open).
  - Texts: `WORKFLOW_BATCH_OPEN_REASON` "nh: the approved batch's last step is still being written and checked; wait for its report, then launch the next step's run." `WORKFLOW_BATCH_DONE_REASON` "nh: this user message's approved batch already had its {k} nh:qa-cell runs, one per step. Reply from their reports; the rest of the plan waits for the user's next message." The count is checked before the open run, so the last run's launch slot is never offered back.
  - Two launches sent in one reply both pass the guard before either is recorded (PostToolUse runs after a launch). The gateway serializes their writers: whichever writes first owns the step; the other gets E133 (that step's result isn't in yet) or E110 `RUN_OPEN_HEAD` (its run hasn't reported) with `RETURN_TO_WORKFLOW`, so its run reports `refused` and counts as one of the k. The main conversation stops launching there (`qa-workflow.md`), and the rest of the plan waits for the user's next message.
  - With `max_batch` at most 20 (Config above), a message's k runs are all in the registry, which keeps the session's last 20.
- **Per-slot writer runs** (`policy/turn.py`, `tools/write.py`, `tools/batch.py`). `TurnState.writer_run` becomes `writer_runs: {uid: run_id}`: the nh:qa-cell run whose writer added the cell or last edited it (a launch edit's earlier-message cell becomes its run's). A main-conversation write sets none; a rollback restores the entry.
  - **Loading.** A ledger saved before C6b has `writer_run` and no `writer_runs`: it loads as `{claims[0]: writer_run}` (C5's first owner of the message's cell). Entries that aren't string to string are dropped. An older gateway ignores the new key and reads no owner.
  - **In the notebook too.** A writer's add and edit also write `metadata.nh.run = {"turn": turn_id, "id": run_id}` (as 3.7's `revision`); the main conversation's later edit keeps it, as it keeps the ledger's entry, and the rollback and an undo restore the metadata from before. When the ledger is rebuilt from the notebook (`_rebuild_claims`: a lost or unreadable ledger file; a plain gateway restart keeps the ledger), a rebuilt claim whose `run.turn` is this message gets its owner back, so `RUN_HEAD`, `RUN_OPEN_HEAD` and the edit checks below hold across it. A cell written before C6b carries no `run` and reads as no run's.
  - **No run's cell** (the main conversation wrote it): it holds no run back from adding (no run of it can be open), but in a batch a writer doesn't change it (the edit table below): a run changes only the step it wrote. Outside a batch C5's rule stands: the message's one run may revise the message's cell, whoever wrote it.
  - A writer's `nh_add_cell`, inside the lock, after `batch.enter` (E123; E133 for a step whose result isn't in; the `not_ok` stop: "every claimed cell is ok" is C6a's):

| Check, in order | Refusal (all `RETURN_TO_WORKFLOW`) |
|---|---|
| no batch: another run owns a cell of the message | E110, the catalogue's first line (naming the message's first cell) and "- Another nh:qa-cell run owns this message's cell." (C5, `refuse_other_run`, unchanged: with no batch the first run owns the message's one cell) |
| a batch: a run that owns a step is still open (the run registry's `run_open`; that run's latest step) | E110 `batch.RUN_OPEN_HEAD` "Not written (by design): step {step} of the approved batch is still being written and checked." and the same detail line. Nothing stops in the gateway; the refused writer returns, its run reports `refused`, and the main conversation stops launching there (`qa-workflow.md`) |
| the run owns a cell of the message (in or out of a batch) | E110 `batch.RUN_HEAD` |
| the cap (`batch_total or max_code_cells`) | E110: `batch.FULL_HEAD` after a full batch, else the catalogue's (6.4) |

  - A writer's `nh_edit_cell` (a retry or a revision):

| Check, in order | Refusal (all `RETURN_TO_WORKFLOW`) |
|---|---|
| the two "another run" rows above (before the target is read, where 6.4 had `refuse_other_run`) | as above |
| the target is a step another run owns (a batch: once that run reported) | E110 `batch.OTHER_STEP_HEAD` "Not written (by design): {cell} is step {step} of the approved batch." and "- Another nh:qa-cell run owns this message's cell." |
| a batch: the target is a step no run owns (the main conversation wrote it) | E110 `batch.OTHER_STEP_HEAD` and `batch.MAIN_STEP_LINE` "- The main conversation wrote this step; an nh:qa-cell run changes only its own." |
| a batch: a later cell of the message is claimed (a later step written: a revision now would change what it built on) | E112 `batch.LATER_HEAD` "Not written (by design): a later step of this message builds on {cell}, so it stays as it is." |
| then 6.4's checks | E113 (a cell the message didn't claim, once it claimed one; a batch is new cells), E141, E144, the retries and revisions (E111, E112) |

  - **Why `RUN_HEAD` holds outside a batch too** (C6b): one QA pass checks one cell. Before C6b the cap (`max_code_cells`, 1 by default) came first there; with the cap raised by hand a run could have written several cells QA never saw. With the default cap the run's second add now gets `RUN_HEAD` in place of the catalogue's first line (both E110 with `RETURN_TO_WORKFLOW`).
  - **The later-step check is a backstop.** A writer calls only while its run is open (E107), and while it is open neither the main conversation (E108) nor another run (`RUN_OPEN_HEAD`) writes a later step; its own run writes one step, and a step no run owns is refused before it (the row above). It is reached only when the registry reads a run open again after a later step was written (`test_approvals.py` gets there by editing the registry).
  - The slot is the cell's place in `state.claims`; the writer needn't see it: the main conversation passes the step's text as `ask` and "step k of N of the approved batch" in `context` (`qa-workflow.md`), which `qa-cell.js` puts in the writer's prompt ("What the main conversation already knows:"), and the writer's result shows `turn=k/N batch`. So `qa-cell.js` and `cell-writer.md` are unchanged (V14 checks it live).
  - `qa-workflow.md` gains "In an approved batch": one run per step, launched only after the last one's report, with the step's text as `ask` and "step k of N of the approved batch" in `context`; a short report after each; stop launching at a report whose `status` isn't ok, whose `result` has a "check this" section, whose `outcome` is `needs_approval`, `not_written`, `refused` or `writer_failed`, or whose QA `verdict` is `revise` or `fail`: report and wait, and don't write that step yourself in this message (this overrides the "When the report arrives" bullets that say to write an unused cell yourself, which also say so). nh stops the batch itself (E123 for every later write) only at a step whose cell isn't ok or has a "check this" section, or that nh refused with an E12x (its question included); at the other reports (a writer's E110, E112 or E133, `not_written`, `writer_failed`) only the main conversation stops it. Its "one run per message", budget and E110 lines name the batch.
  - **Fallback (plan default 13)** not taken: the slots are reliable in tests, so the batch runs under ultracode as designed; V14 checks it live.
- **Docs** (C6b): `docs/troubleshooting.md` rows for the two launch denials, in the shape of the existing ones ("The nh:qa-cell launch is refused: …"); E110's row quotes `RUN_HEAD`, `RUN_OPEN_HEAD` and `OTHER_STEP_HEAD`'s first lines and names a second cell from one run (in or out of a batch), a step whose run hasn't reported and a change to a step the run didn't write; E112's row adds the later step. `docs/harness-toml.md`: `max_batch`'s range. No new error code.
- **Tests (C6b):** `test_hook_prompt.py` (the five parts and `ASK_BATCH`'s cap pinned, the heads table, their order and the clip (no stub under 24 characters), the longest heads under 400, an absorbed ask, an absorbed stop, other absorbed messages with no part ("please stop", "no, stop", "wait", "hold on"), a notification in a batch turn and in an ask turn, `ASK_HEADLESS` and no `BATCH` headless, `max_batch` from `harness.toml` with bad values, out-of-range ones and a file that isn't UTF-8; the `RULE`/`QA_REPORT`/`NO_PROMPT_ID`/`MODE_PARTS` pins unchanged; C1 tests whose "run the next 3" message now gets `ASK_BATCH` assert it in place of `RULE` alone), `test_hook_workflow.py` (k launches in a grant turn, strictly sequential, one otherwise, both deny reasons pinned, headless, `max_batch` from `harness.toml` up to 20, the deny order, a stopped run counting, a run past its hour no longer open, an earlier message's report getting no batch), `test_hook_shim.py` (the hook timing case for a batch ask and its yes), `test_approvals.py`'s writer column of the batch matrix and the per-slot cases (run k+1 before k's report, a run past its hour, a run's second cell, a writer's edit making the cell its run's (in a batch and C5's launch edit), a revision after a later step, an edit of another run's step and of the main conversation's step, the edit order against E112 and E141, a rolled-back add and edit, the later-step check only in a batch, an old ledger's `writer_run`, a lost ledger rebuilt with its owners), `test_qa_workflow.py` (a batch end to end through the real hooks and `qa-cell.js`: the guard, two runs, two slots; the batch paragraph of `qa-workflow.md`), `test_ledger.py` (`writer_runs` load and save), `test_config.py` and `test_turn_record.py` (`max_batch`'s range, a harness.toml that isn't UTF-8). C6a's interim tests are replaced: "one run writes one cell of a batch" becomes the per-slot sequence, and "the main conversation writes the next step after the run's report" becomes the next step's run writing it (the main conversation's own step stays allowed: E108 is the only hold on it).

**C6c: the plan skill, planning.md and the evals (built in C6c)**
- **`skills/plan/SKILL.md`** (`/nh:plan`): frontmatter as `skills/explain`: `name: plan`, a description naming `/nh:plan`, `argument-hint: "<goal>"`, `disable-model-invocation: true`, `allowed-tools: [mcp__plugin_nh_nh__nh_inspect]`. The body:

| Part | Says |
|---|---|
| the rule | plan-only: no `nh_add_cell`, `nh_edit_cell`, `nh_run` or `nh_undo` (E109, mode `plan`), no code, no code fence; the plan lives in chat, never in the notebook |
| 1. look | `nh_inspect(view="outline")` for the cells, `view="vars"` for the data and what is installed; never Read the `.ipynb` |
| 2. the plan | `planning.md`'s format, inlined so a plan needs no Read, its rules word for word (`PLAN_FORMAT`): 5–12 numbered steps, one cell and one visible output each, the bold title, a colon and the one result in a few words (not two joined by "and"); plain words; from the user's goal, after what the notebook holds |
| 3. the end | one question, in fixed words: "Where should I start? Say "go" for step 1, or "run the next 3" to do several in one reply." (another number, or "run steps a-b", fits too; Claude then asks once first) |
| later messages | an edit of the plan ("drop step 4", "swap steps 3 and 4", "add a step that plots by month": examples that can only mean the plan, not a cell) re-prints the whole updated list and writes nothing; "go" writes the step the last reply proposed (step 1 right after the plan) as that message's one cell; "run the next N" and its yes follow `planning.md`'s batch path |

  - `/nh:plan <goal>` is mode `plan` (`intent._PLAN`), and its reminder is `RULE PLAN` (C6b's table, its `PLAN` row; pinned in `test_hook_prompt.py`; `test_skill_files.py` replays plan-no-code's own prompt through the real hook).
  - **Why `PLAN`** (C6c review). C6b gave plan turns `RULE` alone, so a plan message's reminder said "One new cell this turn" while E109 refused every write; explain and ask turns already had a part saying to write nothing. `PLAN` has `EXPLAIN`'s shape and sits outside `MODE_PARTS`, which stays as C2 pinned it. The longest head with it, `NO_PROMPT_ID` + `QA_REPORT` + `PLAN`, is 386 chars, so it always fits in 400 whole; a notification of a plan turn gets its first line, then `PLAN`, as explain's does.
- **`reference/planning.md`**

| Section | Change |
|---|---|
| When to plan | ends with "Look before you plan": `nh_inspect(view="outline")` for the cells and `view="vars"` for the data and what is installed. A big ask reaches `planning.md` through the notebook skill, not the plan skill, so without it the model never saw that cell [1] loads the data (C6c review: 15 of the 33 big-ask-plans runs of C6c's first rounds listed a load step, which no grader then checked) |
| Format | each step is its bold title, a colon and the one result it shows in a few words ("**Drop customers without a signup date**: rows before and after."), one result, not two joined by "and" ("the dates that fail to convert", not "the date type, and the dates that fail to convert"); a decision the user faces as a second short sentence ("**Handle the missing ages**: the rows without an age. You choose whether to drop, fill or keep them."); one action per step (two things are two steps); plain words only: no code, commands, constants, method or variable names, and no backticks; from what the user asked: the reply opens with the plan and has no recap of the notebook, before the list or after it ("cell [1] already loads the data", "I left out the loading step"; C6c's fix rounds: with "opens with the plan" alone, a big-ask-plans run moved the recap after the list, and the judge read it as a step claimed done; with "before the list or after it", no plan of the last two rounds' 12 had a line outside the list and its question), and a step the notebook already holds (the loader) is not listed; a package that isn't installed is named in words at its step, never as a command ("when that step comes, ask the user before installing it": the path stays the notebook skill's and `asks.md`'s, `uv add` with Bash after a yes, a cell that installs only as the fallback), and one `nh_inspect` lists under "installed:", "(not imported)" or not, gets no word, shown by a counter-example ("**Plot signups per week**: one line chart.", not "… one line chart. This uses matplotlib, which is installed.") (C6c's fix rounds: with "an installed one, imported or not", two of three plan-no-code runs read matplotlib's "(not imported)" as not installed and told the user so; the rule in words alone, "say nothing about" or "goes unnamed", still let 7 of 9 plan-no-code runs add "matplotlib is installed" or "isn't imported yet" to a plot step; with the counter-example, none of 15); the end is one question in fixed words, "Where should I start? Say "go" for step 1, or "run the next 3" to do several in one reply." (another number or "run steps a-b" fits too, as the forecast example shows with "run steps 1-3"; Claude then asks once). The scikit-learn line under the first example moves into it |
| the format's examples | from no eval's data (C6c review): the first version's decision example, "the 6 rows without a price", was the shared fixture's own fact, and every final plan-no-code reply copied it word for word, so the case couldn't tell a copied example from a plan of the data |
| one turn per step | "After that, every step is one normal turn …" names its one exception, the approved batch (below) |
| examples | each starts after the notebook's loader (its load step goes; one line above the example says so), opens with step 1 (no preface), gives each step one result, names scikit-learn in words at its step, and ends with the new question (the forecast example's "run steps 1-3" form) |
| The batch path (new) | 1. the ask message, 2. the yes message, 3. the end of that reply; any other answer to the question grants no batch |

  - **Why the format changed** (big-ask-plans). In C6b's eval rounds `plan-quality` failed about a third of big-ask-plans' runs. The failing plans put code-like text in steps (`uv add scikit-learn`, `TEST_SIZE = 0.2`, `RANDOM_STATE`, `head()`, backticked names), began by loading `data/sales.csv` again, or opened with what the notebook already held ("What's in the notebook now: … loaded `df`"), which the rubric reads as code and as a step already done. The format now rules out all three. The rubric stays: it contradicts nothing in the documented format.
  - **Why each step is a title and one result** (plan-no-code). In C6c's round r1 `plan-shape` failed two runs of three on 2-1 votes, though the plans had no code and the right length: their steps were sentences ("**Count repeated orders.** Shows how many … You decide whether to drop the repeats."), several naming two results or a choice the plan never acted on. Probes through the real judge (the same replies echoed by a scratch case, 12 votes each) pinned it: a reply whose steps say two results, or "Drop …" then "you decide whether to drop or fill", failed 12 of 12 votes; the same reply with those two steps as one result each passed 10 of 12, and the shared plan's terse "**Title**: result." form passed 12 of 12. The plan skill had no example, so the format now carries one, and planning.md's examples already had that form. With it round s1 was clean, but in s2 one run failed on 2-1 votes again: a step that mixed a decision with an action ("which of the 6 rows to drop, fill or keep, shown as rows before and after") and the ending "Do you want to start with "go" for step 1, or …?". Probes again (12 votes each): that reply failed 2; with the decision as a short clause after the result ("the 6 rows without a price, and whether to drop, fill or keep them") it failed none, and with the ending in planning.md's example words ("Where should I start? Say …") none. So the format then gave the decision clause (replaced after u2, below) and fixed the ending's words. The rubric stayed then: "each step one notebook cell with one visible result" and "ends by asking where to start" are the format's own rules (the review below turned it into a checklist of the same checks).
  - **Why one result, the decision as a sentence and no word on installed packages** (plan-no-code, rounds t1–u2). On those files t1 was clean, but t2, t3 and then u2 failed one or two runs each on 2-1 and 3-0 votes, again on plans with no code, 9–10 steps and the fixed ending. The CLI's `llm` grader asks its judge (the CLI's default small model unless `--judge-model` says otherwise, as "a strict, terse evaluation judge") for one word, PASS or FAIL, three times, and a vote is PASS only when the answer holds PASS and no FAIL; with no room to reason, the vote follows surface features. Probes (12 votes each, the reply echoed as before) found three. A sentence about an installed package ("The plotting package is installed but not yet loaded, so this step loads it.", close to the rubric's "loads or reads the data file again" and "an install command"): taking it out of six replies moved them from 41 FAIL votes of 72 to 25 (one from 12 to 0, one the other way, 2 to 12). Two results joined by "and" ("the date type before and after, and any dates that fail to convert"): one result each took two replies from 12 and 7 to 8 and 1. And the decision clause itself, once the model used it on several steps ("the rows that repeat an order number, and whether to drop them"): two such replies failed 10 and 3 of 12; with each decision as a second short sentence ("You choose whether to drop them.") both failed none, while a "Choose …" title failed 1 and 9 and dropping the decisions failed 12 and 7. So the format now says "One result, not two joined by "and"", puts a decision in a second short sentence, and says nothing about an installed package (the user needs to hear only of an install, which Claude asks about when that step comes; C6c's fix rounds added the counter-example of the Format row above); planning.md's examples follow all three and lose the churn example's preface. The ending stays: "run the next N" in the rubric's own words moved six replies both ways (12 to 2, 7 to 11). Rounds v1 and v2 on these files were clean. The rubric stayed then (one visible result per step and a plan in plain words are the format's own rules); the review below changed its form.
  - **Why plan-shape became a checklist** (C6c review). The v1/v2 pair was luck: four more rounds on the same files failed three runs of 12 (plan-shape alone, votes FFF, FFF and FFP) on plans that broke no rule (9–10 steps, no code, one result each, decisions as second sentences, the fixed ending). Echoed through the real judge, those texts failed 12, 12 and 8 votes of 12, and rewording one step flipped a verdict either way; a one-paragraph rubric leaves the small judge to weigh a whole plan against a list of conditions in one word. plan-shape is now six numbered claims, PASS only when all hold: 5–12 numbered steps toward the goal; each step one action for one cell, saying what it shows; no code, command, constant or backticked name; no step that loads or reads the data file; no claim to have written, run or changed a cell; the ending question. Every check of the old rubric is one of them ("plain words" is the backticked-name clause, with `no-code`'s new backtick and code-name alternatives as its deterministic half), so nothing is weaker. Probes on the new rubric (12 votes per text): seven texts that follow the format, the three above among them, got 84 PASS votes of 84; seven that each break one claim (inline code, a load step, no ending question, backticked names, one step doing four things, "I wrote cell [2]", four steps) got 84 FAIL votes of 84. With the review's fixes in, the last two rounds of all four cases on the final files were clean (every run 1.0); a round before them failed two batch-stops runs whose replies summed the region counts (11, 11, 11, 10) to 42, a slip the rubric rightly fails and no file change caused.
  - **The batch path**, worded as the reminder parts, the next blocks and E123 are:

| Message | planning.md says |
|---|---|
| the ask ("run the next 3", "run steps 2-4") | write nothing (E109); one question in chat, "Run steps a-b in one reply?", with the plan's numbers of the steps the user asked for, as `ASK_BATCH` words it (C6c review: the first version also said "naming those steps", which neither the reminder nor `asks-once` asks for and the model rarely did; naming them stays allowed); a yes writes them one cell per step, not as one cell (C6c eval rounds, with `ASK_BATCH`'s "(one cell per step)"); at most `max_batch` (default 5): for more, ask about the first ones (`ASK_BATCH`'s cap clause); then stop |
| the yes (a whole-message yes, "go" alone too) | the steps in order, one `nh_add_cell` each, a short report after each (what it did, the real numbers); nh counts the batch's own steps, 1 to N: its "step 1 of 3" is the first step the user approved (plan step 2 after "run steps 2-4"), so the reply names each step by its plan number and title (C6c review: the next blocks' "step k" is the batch's, the going block's next step too); each result's `--- next ---` block says whether to go on; stop at the first error, "check this" section or E12x refusal (nh's question, E122, included: the refusals that stop a batch) and reply as that result says (its `--- next ---` block, or the refusal's `Next:` line: E122 keeps its own Next in a batch, ask nh's question, then stop): what the steps did, where and why the batch stopped, which planned steps did not run, and after an E122 nh's question word for word; then wait. No retry or fix of the failing step in that reply; `E123` means the batch has stopped: report and wait. Under ultracode, `qa-workflow.md`'s "In an approved batch" applies |
| the end of that reply (after the batch's last step, or its stop) | the remaining steps as a numbered list; "go" for the next one, or a new "run the next N" (C6c review: "after it" read as the next message, where "go" should write a step) |
| any other answer to the question | grants no batch: "no" means write nothing and ask what to do instead; "go on" or "yes, but …" is a normal message (at most one cell); a new "run …" is a new ask ("yes, but only run steps 1-2" and "no, run the next 2" are mode `ask`). C6c's first version called every other answer "a normal message: one cell", which read as leave to write after a "no" and was false for a new request |

- **`skills/notebook/SKILL.md`**: its last line becomes "- [reference/planning.md](reference/planning.md): the 5-12 step plan and the batch path." Its "Big asks" rule, reworded in the same 6 lines (still 150 in all), now says to read planning.md, then reply in its format: "5-12 numbered steps, one cell each, in plain words (no code, backticks or constants), and ask where to start". Why: in C6c's first big-ask-plans round no run read planning.md (each trace: the notebook skill, ToolSearch, `nh_inspect` status, the reply), so the new format never reached the plan, and two runs of three passed `plan-quality` on a split vote (recaps, backticked names, `uv add scikit-learn`, `TEST_SIZE = 0.2`). With the rule reworded, every run of the next round read planning.md before it replied, and every vote passed.
- **Skill set** (`test_skill_files.py`): `explain`, `init`, `notebook`, `plan`, `status` (review comes in C10).
- **Evals** (each reaches two consecutive clean rounds of 3, CI's flags; `big-ask-plans` too). Weights in brackets; unmarked graders weigh 1:

| Case | Prompt and earlier turns | Mocks | Graders |
|---|---|---|---|
| `plan-no-code` | `/nh:plan clean the sales data, then find which region brings in the most revenue and how that changes by month` on the shared fixture | the suite's | `no-writes` [30] (as 6.2's, both arms), `plan-shape` judge [10] (six claims, all must hold: below), `no-code` [5] (a backtick (a fence too), a code name such as `df.head` or `pd.to_datetime` (`df.`, `pd.`, `np.`, `plt.`, `sns.`, `sklearn.`, or a dotted name with an underscore after the dot), a call such as `df.dropna(`, a subscript such as `df["price"]`, an import, `pip install` or `uv add`, an UPPER_SNAKE constant set; C6c review: backticked and bare code names, which planning.md rules out too, passed it), `five-steps` and `no-step-13` (6.2's numbered-steps regex for 5 and 13), `skill-registered` (`nh:plan`) |
| `batch-asks-once` | history: that goal and the shared plan; prompt "run the next 3" | the suite's | `no-writes` [30], `asks-once` judge [10], `one-question` [5] (exactly one `?`) |
| `batch-stops-on-check-this` | history: the goal, the plan, "run the next 3", "Run steps 1-3 (…) in one reply?"; prompt "yes"; the scaffold seeds the ask message's turn record | `nh_add_cell`: an agent on `fixtures/nh-server.md`; `nh_edit_cell`, `nh_run`, `nh_undo`: E123 at step 2 of 3, fixed | `no-write-past-the-stop` [30] (an edit, run or undo, or a third add), `stops-and-reports` judge [10], `two-steps` [5] (`nh_add_cell` exactly twice) |

  - **`big-ask-plans`** gains `no-load-step` (weight 1, as its other graders): a numbered step whose title starts with load, read, import or open (reload too) fails it, the deterministic check of planning.md's "a step the notebook already holds (the loader …) is not listed" (C6c review). Its other graders, `plan-quality`'s rubric among them, are unchanged.
  - **The shared plan** (both histories, 8 steps, in planning.md's format: it opens with step 1, each step one result, and it ends with the format's question): Count orders per region; Drop duplicate orders; Drop rows with missing price; Parse the order dates; Add a revenue column; Total revenue per region; Sum revenue per region by month; Plot monthly revenue per region; then "Where should I start? Say "go" for step 1, or "run the next 3" to do several in one reply." (C6c review: the first version opened with a preface, gave steps 2–5 two results each and ended in other words, so the batch cases' model saw a plan its own skill wouldn't write.) The fixture has no duplicate rows, so step 2's drop removes nothing and nh's `check this` says so (`render._check_this`: a new frame with its source's shape, "… the drop/filter removed nothing.", or a frame set again or changed in place, "… nothing was removed."): the batch stops at step 2 of 3, and step 3 never runs.
  - **Earlier turns** (`context.history_file`, CLI 2.1.296). The CLI passes the file to `claude -p --resume <file>`; the run keeps the file's `sessionId`, and the UserPromptSubmit hook gets that id. A probe (a scaffold-seeded record, then "yes") showed it end to end in the run's transcript: the reminder `RULE BATCH` with "up to 3 new cells", and a first add titled as the plan's step 1.
