So batch-stops' scaffold seeds `.nh/state/turns/<sessionId>.json` with the record the hook writes for "run the next 3" (`turn_record.opened`: mode `ask`, request `{batch, n: 3}`, `prev_turn_id` the plan message's), and the real hook does the rest. batch-asks-once needs no record ("run the next 3" opens its own turn). The SessionStart hook sees another session id: harmless, it reads only the project.
    - The history files are minimal transcripts: user and assistant entries in a `parentUuid` chain, one fixed `sessionId` per case, assistant `model` `<synthetic>`. With a history file the CLI runs the with-arm only.
    - A run writes its own transcript as `<sessionId>.jsonl` next to the history file, in the case folder; `.gitignore` covers it.
  - **The agent mock** (batch-stops' `mocks/nh/fixtures/nh-server.md`, as C5b's init-url-data one): one message with an approved batch of 3; each `nh_add_cell` that isn't refused writes the next step k and runs it as [k+1]. Templates: a step that goes on, the last step, a step with `check this` (and the last such), a failed step (and the last such), E120 (it stops the batch), E123 (after a stop) and E110 (after 3 steps). Its "Check this" section gives the gateway's rules this data can hit, each replayed (`kept`, `lost`, `now 0`, `empty`, the twin and the frame set again), and a frame that changed shows its rows' and nulls' change (`df: DataFrame 43×6 → 37×6 (-6 rows); nulls price 6 → 0`); it never plays readability hints. Its data facts read the same under the suite's pandas 3.0.6 and the pandas 2.2.3 it names (checked by hand with `uv run --with pandas==2.2.3`), so `test_skill_files.py` checks them under any pandas. `nh_edit_cell`, `nh_run` and `nh_undo` are fixed E123 texts (`error: true`), replayed after a stop at step 2 and pinned byte for byte (`EXACT_MOCKS`): the graders fail any such call, so no other state reaches a score. `nh_inspect` is the suite's overview.
  - **Weights** (`claude plugin eval`: a run scores its passed weight over its total, a case its mean over runs; CI passes at 0.8):

| Grader share of the total | So |
|---|---|
| the heavy one over 60% | one run that writes (plan-no-code, batch-asks-once) or goes past the stop (batch-stops) fails the case beside two perfect runs |
| the judge over 20% | a judge failing every run fails it |
| the judge plus the light regex over 30% | code in the plan, a second question (or none), or a batch that stops early or never starts fails in two runs of three |
| one judge slip, one cheap miss | still passes |

    - A case's two failure kinds fail disjoint graders, so both can't fail it in one run (each would need over 60%). The write (the stop, in batch-stops) gets the single-run guarantee, as in 6.2; C6c's eval rounds, which want every run at 1.0, catch the rest. `test_skill_files.py` checks each case against this table.
- **Docs** (C6c): both READMEs (`/nh:plan` in the commands, "run the next N" in the flow; the question asked once, stops at the first error, "check this" finding or nh question, any other answer no batch); `docs/troubleshooting.md` one row (the batch question and the answers that don't start the batch: not a whole-message yes, one that names steps itself and gets the question again, headless, and nh's own question in the asking message, first ask wins). No new error code.
- **Gateway** (C6c review): the going next block's last sentence (C6a's next-block table above). No other gateway text changes.
- **Tests** (C6c, `tests/unit/test_skill_files.py`): the skill set; the plan skill's frontmatter and body pins (E109, the views, "run the next N", the format rules it shares with planning.md, word for word); planning.md's batch path (the reminder parts' and next blocks' words, the classifier's answers); the notebook skill's pointer line and big-ask rule; plan-no-code's prompt classified as mode `plan` and replayed through the real hook (`RULE PLAN`), batch-asks-once's through it under the history's session (`RULE ASK_BATCH`); the history files (rebuilt by the test's builder: the chain, one session id, the shared plan in both, and the `.gitignore` line for the run's transcript); the shared plan in planning.md's format (no preface, one result per step: no ", and" or ";", the plan skill's own ending); the format's examples from no fixture's data; plan-shape's six claims pinned; `no-load-step`'s regex on load steps and plans; batch-stops' seeded record equal to the one the real hook writes for the history's ask (ts aside), and the real hook giving `RULE BATCH` with 3 for its "yes"; the fixed E123 mocks in `MOCK_SCENARIOS` (new entries last) through the new `MOCK_PROMPTS` (the messages a scenario sends before its calls: "run the next 3", then "yes"); the agent mock's templates against the real gateway in each state (`BS_SCENARIOS`), its worked examples and data facts; each grader's regex on good and bad replies and `mock_calls` lines (compact and spaced); the weight table above; `test_agent_mocks_are_on_one_description_each` takes the new folder. Outside it (C6c review): `test_hook_prompt.py` pins `PLAN` and the plan heads (a plan message, its notification, a plan typed into a yes message), and `ASK_BATCH`'s new words, its shorter cap clause and the longest head without a prompt id (397 at the default `max_batch`), `test_r2_render.py` and `test_r3_gateway.py` the going block's new words, and `test_r3_gateway.py` replays V14's order (a plan, "go", "run the next 3", "yes": the batch's first cell, plan step 2, is its step 1 of 3, and its next block names the batch's step 2).

**Known gaps**
- **What Claude asked.** The gateway sees the user's request and their yes, not the question. A "yes" to a reply that asked something else still grants the batch the user's previous message requested. The request is the user's own words, so the batch is no surprise; the ask-turn part tells Claude to ask the one question.
- **Steps are the model's.** The gateway counts cells, not plan steps: it can't tell that the batch's cells are plan steps 3–5 in order. Its texts count the batch's own steps ("step 1 of 3", "the batch's next step (step 2 of 3)"), and `planning.md` maps them to plan steps (the batch's step 1 is the first step the user approved, reported by plan number and title); V14 checks it live.
- **E120 and E121 stop the batch** (plan D3). If V14 finds that too strict (a note slip ends a batch), exempting them is a one-line change for the user to decide.
- **The batch part after a stop.** The reminder can't see `batch_stop`; a notification after a stop still shows `BATCH`, and the gateway's E123 holds.
- **The batch part where the gateway grants none.** The hooks don't read the ledger, so when a cell question of the previous message is pending (first ask wins: `batch_not_granted`, reason `pending`), `BATCH` still shows; the gateway holds the message to one cell, and its E110 says so. Headless is checked on both sides.
- **A "stop" typed mid-batch.** C1's rule holds (6.1, user decision 2026-09-29): a message absorbed mid-turn only tightens, and its yes or no is never an answer, so the gateway doesn't stop a going batch when the user types "stop" while Claude writes it. C6b's `BATCH_STOP` part tells Claude to stop, but only for a whole-message no (6.1's no set: "stop", "no", "cancel", …); "please stop", "no, stop", "wait" or "hold on" get no part (not `BATCH`), and only the user's words ask Claude to stop. `BATCH_STOP` shows with that message only: the record keeps no trace of the no, so a later notification of the message (a run's report under ultracode) shows `BATCH` again, and only the conversation carries the user's stop. Making the gateway stop it too (an `absorbed_no` flag in the record, E123 with reason `user`), or the reminder keep `BATCH_STOP` for the rest of the message (the same flag, read by the hook only), changes C1's record, so it is the user's call. A "stop" typed after Claude's reply ended (under ultracode, while a step's run works) is a new message: it opens a turn with no batch, and the run's report then reads as an earlier message's (`QA_EARLIER`).
- **Ultracode in a batch** (C6b; C6a had one run per message, so the main conversation wrote the steps after the first). `workflow_guard` allows k launches, one at a time, and the gateway's per-slot rules hold each run to one step, written only after the last step's run reported. Both are about runs, not steps: the main conversation may still write a step itself (E108 holds it while a run is open), as without ultracode, and nh counts the batch's cells, whoever writes them. Two launches sent in one reply both pass the advisory guard; the gateway lets the first writer write and refuses the other (E133 or `RUN_OPEN_HEAD`), whose run then reports `refused` and ends the batch's launches for that message (`workflow_guard` above).
- **A step whose writer wrote nothing, in a batch.** Its report's reminder is `QA_REPORT` + `BATCH`, and the pinned `QA_REPORT` says "write no new cell unless its writer wrote none", which reads as leave to write that step. In a batch `qa-workflow.md` says the opposite (stop launching, report, don't write that step yourself in this message), as 6.4's "Leave to write" bullet does for `needs_approval`, and the gateway accepts the main conversation's write there (a `refused`, `not_written` or `writer_failed` report stops nothing), so `qa-workflow.md` is the only guard. `QA_REPORT` + `BATCH` is 397–398 chars, so no part can be added to that reminder.
- **A result nh couldn't check.** When the after-probe fails (the kernel busy with the user's own run in JupyterLab, a probe timeout), an OK result has no `check this` section and the batch goes on. nh's own steps can't cause it: the next step waits for the last result (E133). Stopping on it (fail closed) needs its own next block; V14 shows whether it happens.
- **The plan is the model's.** nh records that a message was `/nh:plan`, not its steps: a re-printed list after an edit, "what's left?" and the steps a batch writes are advisory (plan default 6; "Steps are the model's" above).
- **batch-stops seeds nh's record.** A history file is static, so the ask message never ran through the hook; the scaffold writes the record the hook would have written (`test_skill_files.py` checks it equals the one the real hook writes for the history's messages). If a later CLI stops keeping the history's session id on resume, no `BATCH` part shows, and the case grades the model on the conversation and the mocks alone.

**Tests** (C6a)
- `tests/gateway/test_approvals.py`, the batch kind of the approval matrix, main conversation: {batch} × {yes, no, other, "go" alone, "go on"} × {same message, next message, two messages later}; the stops on an error, on `check this`, on `running` and on `queued`, each followed by E123 for a new cell and for a retry; the `max_batch` cap, and N under a raised `max_code_cells`; single use (a second "yes" later grants nothing); an E122 inside a batch (stops it; the next yes grants that call alone), on an add and on an edit; E125 on an add and on an edit, after a full batch (step N) and after a stop (E123); E121's stop; a non-E12x refusal (E112) that doesn't stop it; a stop on the last step (E123 before E110); first ask wins, and an older message's pending question that doesn't block it; headless; a batch request typed mid-turn is granted by the next yes; an edit and a re-run as the yes message's first call; parallel steps (E133 until the last result is in, with no exec delay and with the after-probe held; the step then sent again; a full batch's parallel add gets E110, not E133); a wait on an earlier message's cell (the plain block; its error stops the batch at the steps written so far); an undo (one that removes a step, one that restores an edited step, one that finds the step already deleted: each stops a going batch; E123 after a stop); a writer's second cell from one run; a writer's failed step (no retry); a writer's step sent before the last result is in (E133 with `RETURN_TO_WORKFLOW`); the main conversation writing the next step after the run's report (C6a's interim; C6b replaced it with the next step's run: Tests (C6b)); an absorbed "stop" (the gateway goes on: Known gaps).
- `tests/gateway/test_gateway.py`: E110 at N and the batch's E110 first line and detail; the plain cap unchanged without a batch; E123's texts; `turn=k/N batch` in a real result; an E12x stop's detail line and Next.
- `tests/gateway/test_r3_gateway.py`: the batch's next blocks through the gateway (going, last step, stopped by an error).
- `tests/unit/test_r2_render.py`: `machine_line`'s batch form and every `next_block` batch case, the writer's included.
- `tests/unit/test_config.py`: `max_batch`'s default and its validation.
- `tests/unit/test_turn_record.py`: the `approved_batch` table.
- `tests/unit/test_skill_files.py` (unchanged): E123 joins `CATALOGUE`, so its rows in `errors.md` and `troubleshooting.md` are required; `max_batch` is in `harness-toml.md`.

### 6.4 Cell approvals: package install, network, outside writes

Chunk C5, built as micro-steps; this section is C5a's (plan D0 b as applied to cells, D4's ask level and gateway gate, plan defaults 1 and 2) and C5b's (D4's `network` rule, the approved hosts and `nhctl scaffold` writing them, plan default 12; from "L012 `network`" below). **Step e (C5e): spike V13** ran the E122 flow under `claude -p` with scripted and resumed messages: the gate behaved as this section says in all five cases, and a `-p` run is headless to the gateway only with `NH_HEADLESS=1`. Its review added probes: a yes queued while the asking reply is still being written grants (Known gaps), and Claude Code can pass a stdio server an allowlisted environment only, so `.mcp.json` now forwards `NH_HEADLESS` itself ("Spike V13" below, Known gaps; `spikes/RESULTS.md`).

**The ask level** (`config.load`, `lint._level`)

| Level | A finding at it |
|---|---|
| `off` | is skipped |
| `hint` | is reported after the run |
| `error` | refuses the cell (E120) and counts toward `max_lint_rejects` (E121) |
| `ask` | holds the cell until the user's yes (E122, below); never counts toward E121 |

- **Ask rules.** `config.ASK_RULES` names the rules that can ask: `package_install`, `network` and (C5c) `outside_write`. Each one's finding carries the clause the gate puts to the user, and `test_lint_hard.py` pins that for every rule in the set.
- `config.load` accepts `off`, `hint` and `error` for each `[lint.rules]` key, and `ask` for an ask rule. Any other value falls back to the key's default, with the problem "lint.rules.<key> must be off|hint|error|ask" (an ask rule) or "lint.rules.<key> must be off|hint|error" (any other rule, `"ask"` included). Why not every rule: another rule has no words for the user's question, and the cell key (below) leaves out the note, so a yes to a note rule would approve any note.
- `_level` maps a rule at `ask` to `ask` in both modes. An `ask` that reaches a rule outside `ASK_RULES` (only a `Config` built by hand can hold one) maps to `error`: it fails closed.
- **Strict mode.** `[lint] mode = "strict"` turns every hint into an error (plan default 7) and leaves an ask an ask. An ask is no readability hint: it already holds the write until the user answers, and only the user decides an install. A user who wants installs refused outright sets the rule to `"error"`; `"off"` writes them without asking.
- `Severity` is `error` | `hint` | `ask`. `Issue` gains `question: str = ""`, the plain clause an ask puts in the user's question (an ask rule's finding sets it, at every level; no other rule's does). `LintReport` gains `asks: list[Issue]`, in `_CHECKS` order. `ok` stays "no errors": an ask is no fault in the code.
- **Consumers.** `_lint_failure` and E120 list errors only; `_hint_lines` and the `hints` field of `cell_added`/`cell_edited` list hints only; the gate below reads `asks`. So an ask is never shown as a hint, never dropped (a cell with asks is written only through the gate), and never counted as a lint reject. A granted cell's result names no ask.

**L009 `package_install`** (default `"ask"`, was `"error"`: plan default 2)
- **Detection** is unchanged (`patterns.CELL_PACKAGE_INSTALL`, `_MAGIC_INSTALL`, `_OS_INSTALL`, and `_SHELL_INSTALL` in a shell cell): an install or `uv add` in those forms, and a removal only as `%pip`/`%conda`/`%mamba uninstall|remove`. What it misses is under Known gaps.
- **What the text reads.** In a cell detection flagged: every line with a package command, that is the lines detection found plus any `!`/`%` line, `os.system`/`os.popen`/`subprocess` call or (in a shell cell) shell line that installs, adds, uninstalls or removes. Each line is split into commands at `&&`, `||`, `;` and ` |`. A command is a tool word (`pip`, `pip3`, `conda`, `mamba`, `micromamba`, `uv`) and then its verb (`install`, `add`, `uninstall`, `remove`; not `--add`) with no other tool word between them, so `uv pip install` reads from its `pip` and the scan stays linear in the line's length (a test pins it).
- **Kinds.** `uninstall`/`remove` is a removal, `uv remove` a dependency removal, `uv add` a dependency add, and any other command an install (`uv pip install` too). The text has one clause per kind, in the order the kinds first appear, so a cell that installs one package and removes another says both, whichever line or command comes first.
- **Names.** The words after the verb, once each per kind, up to the end of the command, of a Python call (`)`, `']`, `"]`, a quote that closes a string argument), a redirect (` >`) or a comment (` #`). Options and their values are left out, except:
  - `-r`/`--requirement <file>` names "the packages in `<file>`";
  - `-e`/`--editable <path>` names "`<path>` (editable)";
  - for pip and uv, `-i`/`--index-url`/`--extra-index-url`/`--index`/`--default-index`/`-f`/`--find-links` add "(from `<url>`)" after that command's names; for conda, `-c`/`--channel` adds "(from channel `<name>`)". (`-c` is pip's constraints file, and `-f` conda's `--force`: both left out there.)
  - A kind with no names reads "packages".
- **The suggested command** (`uv add …`, `uv remove …`) keeps the kind's packages, `--editable <path>`, `-r <file>`, and the pip/uv index options as written (uv takes pip's names), never conda's channels; with none, `<package>`. A word the shell treats specially is double-quoted (`"pandas>=2"`), so the question still reads cleanly inside E122's single quotes.

| Kind | Message clause | Question clause | Fix part |
|---|---|---|---|
| install | "installs `seaborn` into the kernel only" | "installs `seaborn` into the kernel only, and the next env sync removes it (`uv add seaborn` keeps it)" | "install it with `uv add seaborn`" |
| `uv add` | "adds `seaborn` to the project's dependencies" | "adds `seaborn` to the project's dependencies with `uv add`" | "run `uv add seaborn`" |
| removal | "removes `seaborn` from the kernel" | "removes `seaborn` from the kernel" | "run `uv remove seaborn`" |
| `uv remove` | "removes `seaborn` from the project's dependencies" | "removes `seaborn` from the project's dependencies with `uv remove`" | "run `uv remove seaborn`" |

- **Message**: "The cell " + the message clauses joined by "; it also " + " (`<first line>`[ (+N more)])." **Question clause**: the question clauses joined the same way. **Fix**: "Remove the install" (an install only), "Remove it" (one other kind) or "Remove the package commands" (several kinds), then "and ask the user; after a yes, " + the fix parts joined by ", then " + " through Bash", ending ": the next env sync removes kernel-only installs." when one kind is an install, "." otherwise.
- E.g. `%pip install plotly` and `%pip uninstall -y seaborn`, in either order: "installs `plotly` into the kernel only, and the next env sync removes it (`uv add plotly` keeps it); it also removes `seaborn` from the kernel" (the removal first when it comes first). `%pip install torch --index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cu121`: "installs `torch` (from `https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cu121`) into the kernel only, and the next env sync removes it (`uv add torch --index-url https://download.pytorch.org/whl/cu121` keeps it)".
- In a conda project (`[project] env_manager = "conda"`) the lasting way is environment.yml, since `nhctl env sync` runs `conda env update --prune`: an install's clause ends "(adding it to environment.yml keeps it)" and its fix part is "add `seaborn` to environment.yml and run `nhctl env sync`"; a removal's fix part is "remove `seaborn` from environment.yml and run `nhctl env sync`" (the fix parts name the packages, so two kinds don't read "it … it"). Several names read "them".
- At `error`, E120 keeps its L009 Next line: ask, then `uv add` with Bash (in a conda project, environment.yml and `nhctl env sync`), then the cell without the install.

**L012 `network`** (C5b; default `"ask"`; in `_CHECKS` right after L009, so a cell that installs and downloads asks both in one question, the install first)
- **The scan** (`lint/network.py`: stdlib plus `_shared/hosts.py`, `lint/magics.py` and `lint/secret_scan.py`'s shell reader, importable on Python 3.11 like `secret_scan.py`). `scan(masked, lines, tree, seed)` returns the cell's network sites in source order, each with the hosts it reaches (host keys, below; loopback ones dropped) and whether it reaches a host nh can't read. L012 is the only reader; it runs only when the rule isn't `off`. A scan that raises on odd code finds nothing (`lint_cell` skips a check that raises, as for L011): the cell is written with no question, never refused.
- **Earlier cells** (`network.bindings(code_above)`, the `seed`). The gateway passes the code cells above the target (`code_sources_before`, as for `names_above`) as `lint_cell(…, code_above=…)`, a keyword that defaults to none, so lint stays a pure function of its inputs. The same walk runs over them in notebook order and keeps what they bind, never their sites: names (the URLs, hosts, literal strings and sessions they hold) and import names. So a `TRIPS_URL` bound in a cell the user approved, a `client = httpx.Client(base_url=…)` or an `import requests as rq` made in an earlier cell counts in this one, and the next cell that reads from that host asks again. A cell above that doesn't parse binds nothing; a walk that raises leaves the seed empty, and the cell itself is still scanned. Each cell's walk is cached on its source and the cells before it (a digest of their sources is the seed's key, so a cached call costs a lookup per cell).
- **A network URL** is a string whose stripped text is a URL with one of these schemes and a host: `http`, `https`, `ftp`, `ftps`, `sftp`, `scp`, `ssh`, `git`, `rsync`, `ws`, `wss`, `s3`, `s3a`, `s3n`, `gs`, `gcs`, `az`, `abfs`, `abfss`, `adl`, `wasb`, `wasbs`, `hdfs`, `webhdfs`, `hf`, `smb` (`hosts.NETWORK_SCHEMES`). Its userinfo may hold anything (`%40`, `$`, a `{}` template): it is dropped. A database URL (`postgresql://…`, `sqlite://…`), `file://`, a bare path, prose that holds a URL (`"see https://…"`, whitespace in the address) and a URL whose host is a template (`https://{host}/x`: built at run time) are none. A network URL whose host can't be read (an IDNA error, an IDNA deviation character: Matching, below) reaches "a host nh can't read".
- **Host keys** (`hosts.network_url`): what a site names and the approved list holds. For most schemes the URL's host (Matching, below). An object store's name is a bucket, not a host, so its key keeps the store: `s3://<bucket>` (`s3`, `s3a`, `s3n`), `gs://<bucket>` (`gs`, `gcs`), `az://<container>` (`az` with no `@account…` host; with one, and for `abfs[s]`/`wasb[s]`/`adl`, the account's host). So `trips` approved for S3 doesn't approve `gs://trips`, and `data.example.org` doesn't approve a bucket of that name. `hf://…` reaches `huggingface.co`, whatever its repo type (`datasets`, `models`, `spaces`).

| Source | Fires | Hosts it names |
|---|---|---|
| a network URL literal passed to a call: positional, keyword, `*`/`**`, or inside a list, tuple, set or dict display (`pd.read_csv("https://…")`, `pl.scan_parquet("s3://…")`, `df.to_csv("s3://…")`, `subprocess.run(["curl", "https://…"])`, a function of the cell's own) | unless the call is quiet, or the URL is a label keyword's value (below) | the URL's host key |
| the same through a name (simple dataflow in statement order, as 6.7's walk; names from earlier cells too): `TRIPS_URL = "https://…"` then `pd.read_csv(TRIPS_URL)`; a list, tuple, set or dict of URLs and its subscripts, `.values()`, `.items()`, `.get()`, `.copy()`, `.pop()`; a `for`, comprehension or walrus target; `cfg["url"] = URL`; `a or b`; a parameter's literal default (`def load(url="https://…")`); `tqdm(URLS)` | as above | as above |
| a string nh can read the start of. nh renders it from literal text, f-string parts, `+`, `%` (`%s`, `%d`, …), `.format` (`{}`, `{0}`, `{name}`), `str()` and names bound to one literal string (in the cell or above). Its host counts when the scheme and the host are literal: unknown parts only in the userinfo (`f"https://{user}:{pw}@data.example.org/x"`), the port (`f"https://data.example.org:{port}/x"`), or after the `/`, `?` or `#` that ends the host (`f"https://api.example.org/v1/{x}"`, `"https://…/" + x`, `"https://…/%s" % x`, `"https://…/{}".format(x)`). A literal host from a name counts too: `HOST = "data.example.org"` then `f"https://{HOST}/trips.csv"`, `"https://" + HOST + "/x"`, `"https://%s/x" % HOST`, and a name bound to one of several literal hosts (`if`/`else`). Or a string that starts with a URL-holding name: `f"{BASE}/items"`, `BASE + "/items"`, `"{}/x".format(BASE)`, `urljoin(BASE, x)`, `os.path.join(BASE, x)`, `"/".join([BASE, x])`, `URL.strip()` (and `rstrip`, `lstrip`, `removeprefix`, `removesuffix`, `replace`, `lower`, `casefold`, `encode`, `decode`), `URL.split(…)[0]` (and `rsplit`, `partition`, `rpartition`; only item 0 keeps the URL's start), `URL[:n]`, `str(URL)`, `shlex.quote(URL)` | as above | as above |
| a network library call: `requests.get`, `post`, `put`, `patch`, `delete`, `head`, `options`, `request` (also as `requests.api.…`); the same and `stream` on `httpx`; `urllib.request.urlopen`, `urlretrieve`; `urllib3.request`; `aiohttp.request`; `socket.create_connection`, `getaddrinfo`, `gethostbyname(_ex)`; `ftplib.FTP`, `FTP_TLS`, `smtplib.SMTP`, `SMTP_SSL` given a host (they connect as they are made; with none, only their `connect` does, as a session below) | always, whatever the URL's source: the call itself reaches the network (a deviation from plan default 12, below) | its URLs' hosts, a bare host literal (`FTP("ftp.example.org")`, `FTP(host="ftp.example.org")`, `create_connection(("example.org", 443))`, `create_connection(address=("example.org", 443))`, by itself or through a name bound to a string), else "a host nh can't read" |
| a network method (`get`, `post`, `put`, `patch`, `delete`, `head`, `options`, `request`, `stream`, `send`, `open`, `urlopen`, `connect`, `connect_ex`, `sendto`, `ws_connect`) of a session made in the cell or above: `requests.Session()`/`session()`, `httpx.Client`/`AsyncClient`, `aiohttp.ClientSession`, `urllib3.PoolManager`/`ProxyManager`/`HTTPConnectionPool`/`HTTPSConnectionPool`, `urllib.request.build_opener`, `socket.socket`, `http.client.HTTPConnection`/`HTTPSConnection` (which connect only when a request is made), and the FTP and SMTP classes above; called on the constructor (`requests.Session().get`), or on a name it was bound to (`s = …`, `with … as s`, `async with`) | always | its URLs' hosts, a bare `(host, port)` literal (`sendto`'s address, its last argument), else the session's own (`httpx.Client(base_url=…)`, `HTTPSConnection("api.example.org")`, a bare host literal as above), else "a host nh can't read" |
| a shell command (the next table): `!`, `!!`, `%sx`, `%system`, `x = !…`, a shell cell's lines (`%%bash`, `%%sh`, `%%system`, `%%sx`, `%%!`, `%%script` with a shell, its options such as `--bg`, `--out <name>` skipped), `bash`/`sh`/`zsh` `-c '…'`; a Python shell call (`os.system`, `os.popen`, `subprocess.run`/`call`/`check_call`/`check_output`/`Popen`/`getoutput`/`getstatusoutput`, `get_ipython().system`/`getoutput`) whose command nh renders as above (a string, an f-string, `+`, `%`, `.format`, a name bound to a literal command, `shlex.split(…)`, `shlex.join([…])`, `shlex.quote(…)`, `" ".join([…])`), or a list whose elements it renders so (unknown parts as `{name}` or `?`; a list that runs `bash -c '…'` has its script read); a command with no literal text (a name bound to a list, `ARGS = ["curl", "-O", URL]` then `subprocess.run(ARGS)`) reaches the network URLs it holds; `get_ipython().run_line_magic("sx"`/`"system", line)` and `.run_cell_magic(<a shell cell magic>, "", body)` | as the next table says | as the next table says |
| Python that runs elsewhere in the cell: `%time`, `%timeit` and `%prun` with a statement, `get_ipython().run_line_magic` of them, `.run_cell_magic(<a Python cell magic>, …)` and `python`/`python3 -c '<code>'` in a shell command | as that code (scanned like a cell, with the names so far) | its sites' hosts |
| `%load <url>` (also `run_line_magic("load", …)`) | yes | the URL's host key |

Shell commands (each command a shell line runs, after `sudo`, `env` and `NAME=value` prefixes):

| Command | Fires | Hosts it names |
|---|---|---|
| `curl`, `wget` | with any non-option argument (not `curl --version`) | its network URL words, IPython's `{name}` and `$name` of a URL-holding Python name; else "a host nh can't read" |
| `kaggle`, `gdown`, `huggingface-cli`, `hf`, `gh`: they download by name | with any non-option argument | as above |
| `ssh`, `sftp` | always | the target's host (`[user@]host`), else "a host nh can't read" (an ssh alias) |
| `ftp`, `telnet`, `nc`, `ncat`, `netcat`, `ping`, `ping6`, `dig`, `nslookup`, `host`, `traceroute`, `whois` | always | their bare-host and URL words, else "a host nh can't read" |
| `git clone`, `git submodule add` | with a remote: a network URL or `[user@]host:path` word (a path or `file://` is local), or a word nh can't read (`$REPO`, a Python name holding no literal string) | its host; for a word nh can't read, "a host nh can't read" |
| `git fetch`, `pull`, `push`, `ls-remote` | unless the repository is a path or `file://` | a remote's host; a remote's name (`origin`) or none: "a host nh can't read" |
| `git submodule update` | always | "a host nh can't read" (other `git submodule` subcommands stay here) |
| `scp`, `rsync` | with a remote argument (`[user@]host:path`, `host::module`, `rsync://`) | its host |
| `pip download` (`pip3`, `python -m pip`) | always (an install is L009's) | its URL words, else "a host nh can't read" |
| `apt`, `apt-get`, `brew`, `npm`, `yarn`, `pnpm` with `install`, `i`, `add`, `ci`, `update` or `upgrade` | always | its URL words, else "a host nh can't read" |
| any other command given a network URL (`aws s3 cp s3://…`) | yes | its network URL words |
| `echo`, `printf`, `print`, `true`, `:`; the package commands L009 reads | never | |

- A loopback word (`localhost:8888/api`, `127.0.0.1`) is no host in any row.
- **Shell reading** reuses 6.7's lexer through two public helpers in `secret_scan.py`: `shell_commands(text)` gives each command's words (quotes, `;`, `&&`, `||`, pipelines, `sudo`/`NAME=value`/keyword prefixes taken off, a line whose quotes don't close read as raw text), with the commands inside `$(…)`, backticks and `bash`/`sh`/`zsh -c '…'` scripts, four levels deep; `shell_lines(body)` joins a shell cell's lines while a quote is open or a line ends in `\`. `env` with its options and settings in front is taken off too. A Python list command is joined with `shlex.join` and read the same way.
- **Quiet calls** (a URL in them is text, not a request): `print`, `pprint`, `pp`, `display`, `str`, `repr`, `ascii`, `format`, `len`, `bool`, `type`, `isinstance`, `issubclass`, `hash`, `id`, `callable`, `help`, and a call whose dotted name ends in one of these (`rich.print`); IPython's `Markdown`, `HTML`, `Latex`, `Code`, `IFrame`, `display_markdown`, `display_html`; `warnings.warn`; logging calls on a receiver named as 6.7's logging sinks; an exception class (a name ending in `Error`, `Exception` or `Warning`); `os.getenv`, `os.getenvb`, `os.environ.get`, `os.environ.setdefault` (an env read with a URL default: plan default 12); `json.dumps`, `json.dump`; `urllib.parse`'s, `re`'s, `logging`'s, `IPython.display`'s, `textwrap`'s, `os.path`'s, `posixpath`'s, `ntpath`'s, `pathlib`'s, `hashlib`'s and `shlex`'s functions (`Path(URL).name`, `os.path.basename(URL)`, `hashlib.sha256(URL.encode())`); containers and frames that hold a URL as a value (`pd.DataFrame`, `Series`, `Index`, `Categorical`, `array`, `asarray`, `np.where`, `np.select`, `Counter`, `OrderedDict`, `defaultdict`, `deque`, `HttpUrl`, `AnyUrl`, `AnyHttpUrl`, `yarl.URL`); str methods (`startswith`, `endswith`, `replace`, `split`, `strip`, `join`, `format`, `title`, …); file writes (`write`, `writelines`, `writerow`, `writerows`, `write_text`, `write_bytes`); frame and series methods that compare, map or relabel (`isin`, `eq`, `ne`, `lt`, `le`, `gt`, `ge`, `fillna`, `where`, `mask`, `assign`, `rename`, `groupby`, `contains`, `drop`, `query`); chart and form labels (`set_title`, `set_xlabel`, `set_ylabel`, `set_zlabel`, `suptitle`, `supxlabel`, `supylabel`, `xlabel`, `ylabel`, `text`, `annotate`, `figtext`, `legend`, `set_label`, `set_text`, `set_caption`, `set_xticklabels`, `set_yticklabels`, `add_annotation`, `update_layout`, `update_xaxes`, `update_yaxes`, `properties`, `add_argument`); the request builders `urllib.request.Request`, `requests.Request` and `httpx.Request` (their URL counts at the call that sends them: `urlopen(Request(URL))`).
- **Label keywords.** In any other call (not a network library call or a session's method), a URL given as `title`, `label`, `xlabel`, `ylabel`, `zlabel`, `desc`, `description`, `caption`, `help`, `name`, `text` or `legend` is text: `df.plot(title=URL)`, `ax.plot(x, y, label=URL)`, `tqdm(rows, desc=URL)`.
- **Import names** are followed (`import requests as rq`, `from urllib.request import urlopen`, `from httpx import AsyncClient`, `from http import client`), in the cell and above; with no import, a module's name (`requests`, `httpx`, `urllib`, `urllib3`, `aiohttp`, `socket`, `http`, `ftplib`, `smtplib`, `subprocess`, `os`, `shlex`, `tqdm`, …) is that module, as 6.7 does for `os`, and `urlopen`, `urlretrieve` and `Request` are `urllib.request`'s, `urljoin`, `urlparse`, `quote` and the rest of `urllib.parse`'s functions are its, `getenv` and `environ` are `os`'s, `np` is NumPy and `Path` is `pathlib.Path`.
- **Not network** (plan default 12; nothing fires):

| Code | Why |
|---|---|
| database connections: `create_engine("postgresql://…")`, `pd.read_sql(q, "mysql://…")`, `psycopg.connect(host=…)`, `sqlite3.connect(…)` | not a network scheme, and no network API |
| an env-sourced URL given to a reader: `pd.read_csv(os.environ["DATA_URL"])`, the first cell's `DATA_URL` read from `.env` | a reader may get a local path; nh can't tell. A network library call with it still asks (above): the call is the network call |
| a URL whose host isn't in the code: `f"https://{host}/x"` with `host` a parameter or from the env, `BASE + path` with `BASE` from the env, a function's return value | the host isn't in the code (a network library call still asks). Only a non-literal host is exempt: a host bound to a literal in the cell or above is in the code (the Source table) |
| a loopback host: `localhost`, `*.localhost`, `127.0.0.0/8`, `::1`, `0.0.0.0`, `::` | a local JupyterLab, API or database is no network |
| `file://…`, plain paths, `git clone ../repo` | local |

- **Deviation from plan default 12, for the user to accept.** The default reads "env-sourced URLs are not detected as network". nh keeps that for readers and database connections, which may get a local path or a database: `pd.read_csv(os.environ["DATA_URL"])` and the first cell's `.env` URL write with no question. A network library call or a fetcher still asks when its URL comes from the env (`requests.get(os.getenv("API_URL"))`, `!curl -o t.csv $DATA_URL`): the call itself reaches the network, and nh can't tell which host, so it names none ("connects to the network"). The same holds for a run-time URL given to such a call.
- **Known false positives (L012).** A call nh doesn't know as quiet, given a whole network URL, reads as a request: `.get(URL)`, `.pop(URL)` or `.setdefault(URL)` on a dict or a cache (a session's `get`, made in code nh can't see, has the same form), a URL kept as a value by the cell's own function or a library call not in the quiet list (`make_row(URL)`, `Record(url=URL)`), and `df["src"].map({URL: …})`. The question then names a host the cell never contacts. The remedy is the user's yes for that cell, or `network = "hint"` (or `"off"`) in `harness.toml`, not approving the host: an approved host lets real requests to it through too.

- **Approved hosts are skipped**: a site whose every host is in the approved list (below) is dropped; a site that reaches a host nh can't read is never dropped. What's left names its unapproved hosts.
- **Text** (the hosts in source order, once each; `H` = "`a`", "`a` and `b`", "`a`, `b` and `c`", or "`a`, `b`, `c` and N more"):

| Hosts left | Question clause | Message |
|---|---|---|
| named only | "connects to `H` over the network" | "The cell connects to `H` over the network (`<where>`[ (+N more)])." |
| none nh can read | "connects to the network" | "The cell connects to the network (`<where>` …)." |
| both | "connects to `H` and other hosts over the network" | as the first, with that clause |

- `<where>` names the first site without its arguments, so no literal in them (a password in `auth=`, a token in a header) is quoted: the call's dotted name with `()` for a call in the chain (`pd.read_csv`, `requests.Session().get`, `os.system`), the line magic and command (`!curl`, `!git clone`, `!python -c`, `%load`), or a shell cell's command (`curl`); a site inside a Python shell call or an IPython call is named by that call (`os.system`, `get_ipython().run_line_magic`); one in a magic's statement (`%time`, `%timeit`, `%prun`) or a Python cell magic is named as in any cell (`requests.get`). `(+N more)` counts the other sites left.
- A host is never a URL: no scheme, userinfo, port, path, query or fragment (`https://user:pw@data.example.org:8443/x.csv?token=…` reads `data.example.org`), so no URL credential reaches the finding. E122, its question (`secrets.current().redact`) and every tool result are redacted again (6.8).
- **Fix**: "Don't reach the network from this cell: ask the user to download what it needs into the project (for example `data/raw/`), then read it from there." At `error`, E120's Next for L012 (after L009's, which wins when both refuse): "Don't call again yet: this project refuses cells that reach the network. Ask the user to download what the cell needs into the project (for example data/raw/), then write the cell to read it from there."
- E.g. `TRIPS_URL = "https://data.example.org/trips-2023.csv"` then `trips = pd.read_csv(TRIPS_URL)`: E122 with `- L012: The cell connects to `data.example.org` over the network (`pd.read_csv`).` and the question "This cell connects to `data.example.org` over the network. Run it as it is?".

**Approved hosts** (`_shared/hosts.py`, Python 3.9 stdlib, shared by the gateway and `nhctl`)
- **The file**: `<project>/.nh/state/approved_hosts.json` (`Layout.approved_hosts`), a JSON list of host keys, e.g. `["data.example.org", "s3://trips-bucket"]`: a host lowercase, IDNA (punycode) ASCII, with no scheme, port, userinfo or trailing dot; a bucket as its store's key (Host keys, above). It lives in `.nh/`, which scaffold git-ignores: the list is local to the clone and is not committed, so each clone approves its own hosts.
- **Who writes it**: `nhctl scaffold` (below) and the user, by hand. Claude doesn't, as far as a model that follows instructions goes (6.0 b's trust boundary): no nh tool writes it, `file_guard` denies Write and Edit into `.nh/`, and the best-effort shell guard (`SHELL_NH_STATE_WRITE`) denies the usual shell writes (`>`, `tee`, `cp`). What it misses is under Known gaps. A yes to E122 approves that one cell once (the gate above), never its host: the list is unchanged.
- **The gateway reads it** per `nh_add_cell` and `nh_edit_cell` call, main and writer alike (`hosts.read_approved(svc.layout.approved_hosts)`), and passes the set to `lint_cell(…, approved_hosts=…)`, a keyword that defaults to none: lint stays a pure function of its inputs. It is decoded as UTF-8 with or without a BOM. A missing, unreadable or corrupt file (not JSON, not a list, or over 1 MiB, `hosts.MAX_FILE_BYTES`) reads as empty; an entry that isn't a string or a host key is skipped; it never raises.
- **Problems are shown** (`hosts.problems(path)`, never raising), as config lines beside `harness.toml`'s (`config_lines(cfg, layout)`): in `nh_inspect`'s status, outline and overview, and in the `--- config ---` section of `nh_add_cell`, `nh_edit_cell`, `nh_run` and `nh_undo` results. A missing file is no problem. An entry is named by its place in the list, never quoted (a pasted URL may hold a token):

| File | Line |
|---|---|
| not readable, not JSON, not a list, over 1 MiB | "`.nh/state/approved_hosts.json` is not a JSON list of host names: nh approves no host from it." |
| entries that aren't host keys (a URL, a path, a number, a wildcard) | "`.nh/state/approved_hosts.json` entries 2 and 5 are not host names (write the host only, such as `data.example.org`): they approve nothing." (one entry: "entry 2 is not a host name … it approves nothing"; after three, "and N more") |

- **Matching**: a URL's host is lowercased, its userinfo, port and IPv6 brackets dropped, a trailing dot dropped, and IDNA-encoded (Python's `idna` codec, IDNA 2003); the list's entries are normalised the same way; then exact match on the host key. A subdomain isn't covered: with `data.example.org` approved, `api.data.example.org` asks (a host is one name; a wildcard would approve hosts nobody saw). A host nh can't read is never approved. IDNA 2003 maps the deviation characters (`ß`, `ς`, U+200C, U+200D) to other names than the IDNA 2008/UTS 46 encoding `requests` and `httpx` send (`faß.de` is `fass.de` to the codec, `xn--fa-hia.de` on the wire), so a name holding one is a host nh can't read, and a list entry holding one is skipped.

**`nhctl scaffold`** (`_shared/scaffold/core.py`; the CLI in `scripts/nhctl/scaffold.py` needs no change)
- When the data is a URL (`data.mode` `url`, with or without credentials in `.env`) with a network scheme and a host key that isn't loopback, scaffold merges that key into the file (`hosts.approve`): created, `updated` when other entries are there (kept as they are, odd ones included), `kept` when it is already listed. Written atomically (a temp file, then a rename). Only the host key (`data.example.org`, `s3://trips-bucket`, `huggingface.co`): never the URL, its path, query or credentials, however its password is written (`%40`, `$`).
- A file that isn't a JSON list (or can't be read) is left alone (`skipped`), with a warning naming the host: "`.nh/state/approved_hosts.json` is not a JSON list of hosts, so nh left it alone and did not approve <host>: the first cell will ask before reading the data." Not written: no data, a file or folder (`copy`, `in-place`), a database URL, `file://`, a loopback host.
- **Report**: `paths` gains `.nh/state/approved_hosts.json` with its action (the human output lists it with the others), and `data` gains `approved_host` (the host key, or `""`). A rerun reports it `kept`, so scaffold stays idempotent.
- **The first cell.** A plain URL is a literal in the first cell (`DATA_URL = "https://…"`, `pd.read_csv(DATA_URL)`): its approved host lets it through with no question, which matters most there, since `/nh:init`'s message has no turn record and a question asked in it can't be granted by the next message (the gate's "No record"). A credentialed URL is read from `.env` (6.7's first cell), not network anyway. `test_lint_hard.py` pins both: first-cell.md's canonical cell with a URL in place of its path has no L012 with the host approved, and asks without it.

**L013 `outside_write`** (C5c; default `"ask"`; in `_CHECKS` right after L012, so a cell that installs, downloads and writes outside the project asks all three in one question, in that order)
- **The scan** (`lint/writes.py`: stdlib plus `lint/network.py`, `lint/magics.py` and `secret_scan.py`'s shell reader, importable on Python 3.11 like them). It reuses L012's walk, not a second dataflow: `writes._Scanner` subclasses `network._Scanner`, so statements, names (in the cell, and in the cells above through `writes.bindings(code_above)`, walked and cached per cell as L012's `bindings` are, in a cache of its own; L013's seed also carries the functions those cells define, below), imports, `!` lines, line magics, shell cells, `os.system`/`subprocess`, `get_ipython()` calls, `bash -c`, `python -c` and `%timeit`/`%prun`/`%time` statements are read the same way. It overrides four things: what a value holds (a path: its literal text, unknown parts kept as holes, below), what a call does (the sinks below, in place of network sites, and a call to a function the cell defines), what a function body is (its parameters stand for the call's arguments, and its folder moves stay in it, below) and what a shell command does (the shell table below, from `secret_scan.shell_stages`, which also gives each command's redirection targets). `network.py` gains one shared helper (`_without_env`, the `env -i K=v` prefix); its behaviour is unchanged. `scan(masked, lines, tree, seed)` returns the cell's writes in source order (a shell command's own paths before its redirections'): where (the call or command, never its arguments), the path as written (holes for unknown parts, joined to the folder the cell moved to, below) and whether it removes. L013 is the only reader; it runs only when the rule isn't `off` and a project root is given. A scan that raises finds nothing (`lint_cell` skips a check that raises): the cell is written with no question, never refused.
- **Inputs** (pure, as `approved_hosts`): `lint_cell(…, project_root=…, notebook_dir=…)`, keywords that default to none and `""`. The gateway passes `svc.project` and the notebook's folder relative to it (`posixpath.dirname(ref.rel_path)`) on every `nh_add_cell` and `nh_edit_cell`, main and writer alike. With no project root L013 finds nothing.
- **Sinks** (a call nh reads through the same import names as L012: `import shutil as sh`, `from pathlib import Path`; with no import, `np` is NumPy and `Path` is `pathlib.Path`, and to L013 `pd` is pandas, `plt` is `matplotlib.pyplot` and `tf` is TensorFlow):

| Call | The path it writes (W) or removes (R) |
|---|---|
| a frame's or series' `.to_csv`, `.to_parquet`, `.to_excel`, `.to_pickle`, `.to_json`, `.to_feather`, `.to_hdf`, `.to_stata`, `.to_html`, `.to_latex`, `.to_markdown`, `.to_xml`, `.to_orc`; `.to_netcdf`, `.to_zarr`, `.to_file` (xarray, geopandas); `ExcelWriter(…)` | W: the first argument, or `path`, `path_or_buf`, `path_or_buffer`, `buf`, `excel_writer`, `fname`, `filename`, `store`, `file` |
| `.write_*` (polars `write_csv`, `write_parquet`, …; plotly `write_image`, `write_html`), except `write_text`, `write_bytes` (below), `write_database` and `write_clipboard`; polars `.sink_*` | W: the first argument, or `file`, `path`, `target`, `workbook`, `fname`, `filename` |
| `.savefig` (`plt`, a figure), `.save` (PIL, Keras, openpyxl, Spark's `.write.save`), `.save_model`, `.save_weights`, `.save_pretrained`, `.save_to_disk` (Hugging Face datasets), `.to_disk`, `.tofile` | W: the first argument, or `fname`, `fp`, `filepath`, `filename`, `file`, `path`, `save_directory`, `dataset_path` |
| a Spark frame's `.write.csv`, `.parquet`, `.json`, `.orc`, `.text`, also after the writer's `.mode(…)`, `.option(…)`, `.options(…)`, `.format(…)`, `.partitionBy(…)`, `.bucketBy(…)`, `.sortBy(…)` | W: the first argument, or `path` |
| `numpy.savez`, `savez_compressed`, `savetxt` (`np.save` is a `.save` above) | W: the first argument, or `file`, `fname` |
| `matplotlib.pyplot.imsave`, `matplotlib.image.imsave`, `cv2.imwrite`, `imageio.imwrite` (and `imageio.v2`'s and `imageio.v3`'s), `skimage.io.imsave`; `scipy.io.savemat`, `scipy.io.wavfile.write`, `soundfile.write`; `logging.FileHandler`, `logging.handlers.RotatingFileHandler`, `TimedRotatingFileHandler`, `WatchedFileHandler` | W: the first argument, or its keyword (`fname`, `filename`, `uri`, `file_name`, `file`) |
| `logging.basicConfig(filename=…)` | W: `filename` |
| `joblib.dump`, `pickle.dump`, `torch.save`; `pyarrow.parquet.write_table`, `pyarrow.feather.write_feather`, `pyarrow.csv.write_csv`; `urllib.request.urlretrieve`; `pandas.to_pickle` (a frame's `.to_pickle` is above); `tensorflow.saved_model.save` | W: the second argument, or its keyword (`filename`, `file`, `f`, `where`, `dest`, `output_file`, `filepath_or_buffer`, `export_dir`) |
| `torch.onnx.export` | W: the third argument, or `f` |
| `open`, `io.open`, `builtins.open`, `codecs.open`, `gzip.open`, `bz2.open`, `lzma.open`, `tarfile.open`, `zipfile.ZipFile` | W: the first argument (or `file`, `filename`, `name`), when the mode (the second argument, or `mode`) is a string nh reads holding `w`, `a`, `x` or `+`; for `tarfile.open`, only the mode's part before `:` or `|` (`r:xz` and `r|xz` read, `x:xz` writes). No mode reads; a mode nh can't read isn't counted |
| a path's `.write_text`, `.write_bytes`, `.touch`, `.mkdir`, `.symlink_to`, `.hardlink_to`, `.open(<mode>)` (mode as above) | W: the path itself |
| a path's `.unlink`, `.rmdir` | R: the path itself |
| a path's `.rename(<target>)`, `.replace(<target>)` (one argument: a `str.replace` has two) | W: the target; R: the path itself |
| `shutil.copy`, `copy2`, `copyfile`, `copytree`; `os.symlink`, `os.link` | W: the destination (the second argument, or `dst`) |
| `shutil.move`, `os.rename`, `os.replace`, `os.renames` | W: the destination (as above; `new` for `os.renames`); R: the source (the first argument, or `src`; `old`) |
| `os.mkdir`, `os.makedirs`; `shutil.make_archive` (its `base_name`: the question names it without the extension the archive gets) | W: the first argument, or its keyword (`path`, `name`, `base_name`) |
| `os.remove`, `os.unlink`, `os.rmdir`, `os.removedirs`, `shutil.rmtree` | R: the first argument, or `path` (`name` for `os.removedirs`) |
| `%%writefile <path>` (`-a` too), `%%file <path>`, `get_ipython().run_cell_magic("writefile", "<path>", …)` | W: its path (IPython fills `{name}` and `$name` from Python first) |

- **Decided: a removal is a write** (the scope left `mkdir`/`touch` to decide; plan D4 names writes only). Creating a folder or a file, and removing one or moving it away, change the place outside the project as much as writing a file there does, so `mkdir`, `touch`, removals and a move's source ask too. The question says which: "writes to" or "removes".
- **The same through a name**: a path bound earlier in the cell or in a cell above (`OUT = Path("/data/exports")`, then `df.to_csv(OUT / "trips.csv")`; `def save(path="/data/x.csv")`), as L012 reads URLs. A path held in a container or an attribute isn't read (`paths["out"]`, `cfg.out_dir`), nor a loop over several, nor a name bound to one of several literal paths (`if`/`else`).
- **A function the cell defines** (or a cell above): a `def`, or a lambda bound to a name, is read once where it is defined, with each parameter standing for whatever a call gives it. A write whose path needs no parameter counts there, as any write in the cell, and only there: a later cell that calls a function from a cell above with such a write doesn't ask (Known gaps; L012 reads a function from a cell above the same way). A write whose path holds a parameter (`frame.to_csv(path)`, `Path(out_dir) / name`, `f"{folder}/x.csv"`, a call to another such function) is kept with the function, and each call by its name writes it with the call's arguments filled in (by position or keyword, else the parameter's default; one nh can't read is unknown), named by the call: `def export(frame, path): frame.to_csv(path, index=False)` then `export(trips, "~/trips.csv")` reads "writes to `~/trips.csv`, outside the project (`export`)". A join restarts at an absolute argument as Python's does (`os.path.join(BASE, path)` given `/x.csv` is `/x.csv`); an f-string doesn't. Where it is defined, each write whose parameters all have defaults nh reads is made with them, as a call with no arguments would (`def save(path="/data/x.csv"): trips.to_csv(path)` writes `/data/x.csv` there, named by its sink, as before); a write that needs a parameter with no default waits for a call. An `open` mode given as a parameter reads as its default, there and at every call (`def dump(path, mode="w"): open(path, mode)` writes; with no default, or `"r"`, it doesn't). The body's names (parameters and locals) stay in the body: a function's local is no name outside it. Not read: a method (`saver.save(path)`), `*args`/`**kwargs`, a function reached through another name (`f = export`, a dict of functions, `functools.partial`), or a parameter the body reads only through `.parent` or `dirname` (Known gaps).
- **A path nh reads** (the value of an expression):

| Code | Path |
|---|---|
| a string literal; a name bound to one, in the cell or above (`name := …` too) | its text |
| an f-string, `+`, `%`, `.format`, `str(…)`, `"/".join([…])` (L012's rendering) | the literal parts as written, unknown parts as holes |
| `Path(…)` (also `PurePath`, `PosixPath`, `PurePosixPath`), `os.path.join(…)`, `.joinpath(…)`, `a / b` with a path on either side | joined; an absolute part starts the path over |
| `.expanduser()`, `os.path.expanduser`, `normpath`, `os.fspath`, `str(…)` | the same path |
| `.resolve()`, `.absolute()`, `os.path.abspath`, `realpath` | the same path; a relative one from the folder the cell is in (a `~` path stays as written) |
| `.parent`, `os.path.dirname(…)`; `.with_name(n)`, `.with_suffix(s)` | its folder; the folder and `n`; the path with its suffix replaced by `s`. A folder is text, as pathlib's (`Path("..").parent` is `.`), except above the folder the cell is in and the home folder, where it climbs (`Path.cwd().parent` from `notebooks/` is the project root; `Path.home().parent` reads `~/..`) |
| `Path.home()`, `os.path.expanduser("~")`, `os.environ["HOME"]`, `os.getenv("HOME")`, `os.environ.get("HOME")`; `$HOME` in a shell word | `~` |
| `Path()` | `.` |
| `Path.cwd()`, `os.getcwd()` | the folder the cell is in: the notebook's, or where the cell moved it (below) |

- **A path with unknown parts** (f-string paths, decided): each unknown part stands for text within one folder name (it holds no `/`), as the scanner already assumes when `f"../data/{x}.csv"` stays inside. The path is resolved with its unknown parts in it, and it counts when no path it can stand for is in the project or an exempt place. `f"/data/exports/{name}.csv"` lands in `/data/exports` (outside); from `notebooks/`, `f"../../trip_{n}.csv"` lands in the project's parent (outside) and `f"../data/processed/{name}.csv"` stays inside; with the project at `/home/me/proj`, `f"/home/me/{name}.csv"` and `f"/home/{user}/x.csv"` are outside, while `f"/home/me/{name}/x.csv"` and `f"/home/{user}/proj/x.csv"` may be the project (`{name}` may be `proj`) and don't ask; `f"/{name}/x.csv"` may be `/tmp/x.csv` and doesn't ask either. It is named by the folder before its first unknown part and `/…` (`/data/exports/…`, `/home/me/…`). A path that starts with an unknown part (`f"{out_dir}/x.csv"` with `out_dir` from the env, `$DEST` in the shell) isn't read: it may be absolute.
- **The folder the cell is in.** Relative paths start from the notebook's folder: the kernel runs there (first-cell.md "Paths"). In the cell, `os.chdir(<path>)`, `%cd <path>` and `%pushd <path>` move it for the rest of the cell (`%cd` alone: `~`); a shell `cd <path>` (or `pushd`) moves it for the rest of that `!` line or shell call (each starts in the kernel's folder), and for the rest of a shell cell. A folder nh can't read (`cd $DIR`, `cd -`, `%popd`) leaves the relative paths after it unread. An earlier cell's `%cd` isn't followed (Known gaps). Defining a function runs nothing, so an `os.chdir` in a function body or a lambda moves the folder for the rest of that body only, and calling it moves nothing for the code after the call, nor, when the body moves to a parameter's folder, for the body's own writes at the call (Known gaps); `with contextlib.chdir(<path>):` (Python 3.11+, also `from contextlib import chdir`) moves it for the `with` body, and the folder before it holds after. An `os.chdir` under `if`/`while`/`try` counts as run (Known gaps).
- **Shell commands** (each command a shell line runs, read as L012 reads them: `!`, `!!`, `%sx`, `%system`, `x = !…`, shell cells, `os.system`/`os.popen`/`subprocess` and `get_ipython().system` with a command nh renders, `bash -c '…'`; `sudo`, `env` and `NAME=value` prefixes taken off; a subshell's `(` glued to its first word and `)` glued to its last are taken off too: `!(cd /data && touch x.csv)` writes `/data/x.csv`, and the folder before the `(` holds again after its `)`. A `( cd …` with a space after `(` is read as a plain command list: nh can't see where it starts, so its `cd` holds for the rest of the line, as a `bash -c 'cd …'` one does (Known gaps)). Words are unquoted; in `!` lines (and `%%writefile`'s line) IPython's `{name}` and `$name` are filled from Python names holding a path nh reads (`${name}` keeps its `$`, as IPython leaves it: `$/data/x.csv` is a relative path), the shell's `$HOME` and `${HOME}` read `~`, and any other name is unknown (`{HOME}` with no Python `HOME` too: IPython then leaves the line as written):

| Command | Writes (W) or removes (R) |
|---|---|
| any command's `>`, `>>`, `>\|`, `&>`, `&>>`, `2>` (any descriptor) or `>&<file>` redirection (also with no command: `> file`) | W: the file (`>&2`, `>&-` are no file) |
| `tee` (`-a`) | W: each file |
| `cp`, `ln`, `rsync` | W: the destination: `-t`/`--target-directory`'s folder, else the last of two or more arguments (`-S`/`--suffix`'s value is none; `ln` with one argument links into the current folder); an rsync remote (`host:path`, `rsync://`) is L012's |
| `mv` | W: the destination, as `cp`; R: each source |
| `mkdir`, `touch` | W: each argument |
| `rm`, `rmdir`, `unlink` | R: each argument |
| `curl -o <file>` (`--output`, `--output-dir`), `wget -O <file>` (`--output-document`, `-P`/`--directory-prefix`), short options combined or glued too (`-sLo out.csv`, `-Oout.csv`) | W: that file or folder (`-` is standard output) |
| `dd of=<file>` | W: that file |
| `zip` | W: the archive, the first plain argument (`-b`, `-n`, `-P`, `-t`, `-Z` take a value), and `-O`/`--output-file`'s file |
| `jupyter nbconvert` (`jupyter-nbconvert`) | W: `--output-dir`'s folder |
| `python -c '<code>'` | as that code, from the folder the shell line is in |

- **Outside** (`writes.outside(path, root, notebook_dir)`: pure text with `posixpath`, nothing on disk):

| Path | Outside when |
|---|---|
| starts with `~` (`~/x.csv`, `~user/x.csv`) | always, shown as written. Whether `~` expands depends on the library (pandas, the shell, `%%writefile`, `expanduser()` expand it; `open()`, `pathlib` and NumPy don't, and write a folder named `~` in the current folder instead), so nh asks either way: the cell means the home folder, or makes a `~` folder by mistake |
| absolute | not under the project root once `.` and `..` are resolved (`/home/me/proj/notebooks/../data/x.csv` is inside; a leading `//`, which `posixpath.normpath` keeps, reads `/`) |
| relative | joined to the project root, the notebook's folder and the folder the cell moved to, then resolved as above: from `notebooks/`, `../data/x.csv` is inside and `../../x.csv` outside; from a notebook at the project root, `../x.csv` is outside |
| `file:///…` | as its path. Any other scheme (`s3://`, `https://`, `sqlite:///…`) is no local file: a network one is L012's |

- **Exempt** (decided; plan D6's shell ask exempts `/dev/*` and Claude's tmp dirs, and the cell rule matches it): `/dev` and below (`/dev/null`, `/dev/stdout`, `/dev/stderr`: nothing on disk changes), and the system temp folders `/tmp`, `/var/tmp`, `/private/tmp` and `/private/var/tmp` and below: scratch space every program shares, emptied on reboot, that holds no project or user files (Claude's tmp dirs are inside `/tmp`). A run-time temp path (`tempfile.mkdtemp()`, `tempfile.gettempdir()`) isn't read anyway. In a project inside `/tmp` (tests, evals), every other `/tmp` path is exempt too.
- Symlinks aren't followed: a link inside the project that points elsewhere counts as inside, and the root is compared as the gateway has it (`svc.project`, resolved when nh found the project).
- **Text** (each kind's paths in source order, once each; `P` and `Q` as L012's `H`: "`a`", "`a` and `b`", "`a`, `b` and `c`", or "`a`, `b`, `c` and N more"):

| Outside sites | Question clause | Message |
|---|---|---|
| writes only | "writes to `P`, outside the project" | "The cell writes to `P`, outside the project (`<where>`[ (+N more)])." |
| removals only | "removes `Q`, outside the project" | "The cell removes `Q`, outside the project (…)." |
| both | "writes to `P` and removes `Q`, outside the project" | as the first, with that clause |

- A path is named resolved: absolute and normalised (`../../x.csv` from `/home/me/proj/notebooks` reads `/home/me/x.csv`); a `~` path as written; a path with unknown parts as its folder and `/…` (`/data/exports/…`). E122, its question (`secrets.current().redact`) and every tool result are redacted (6.8), so a secret in a path (a token in a folder name) doesn't reach Claude. A path keeps the message and the question on one line and readable: a control character shows as its escape (`\n`, `\t`, `\x07`); a path holding `'` shows in double quotes with `\` and `"` escaped, as L009 shows such a word, since the question sits in E122's single quotes (`` `"~/Bob's data/x.csv"` ``); and a path holding a backtick goes in a double-backtick span (``` `` /x/a`b.csv `` ```).
- `<where>` as L012's: the call's dotted name with `()` for a call in the chain (`df.to_csv`, `plt.savefig`, `Path().write_text`, `shutil.copy`, `open`, a function the cell defines by its name: `export`), the command (`!cp`, `!tee`, `!echo >` for a redirection, `cp` in a shell cell), `%%writefile`, or the Python call that runs the shell (`os.system`). `(+N more)` counts the other outside sites.
- **Fix**: "Keep the cell's files inside the project (for example under `data/processed/` or `reports/`); ask the user before writing anywhere else." At `error`, E120's Next for L013 (after L009's and L012's, which win when they refuse too): "Don't call again yet: this project refuses cells that write outside it. Write the files inside the project instead (for example data/processed/ or reports/), or tell the user where the cell would write and let them change it themselves."
- E.g. `df.to_csv("~/exports/trips.csv")`: E122 with `- L013: The cell writes to `~/exports/trips.csv`, outside the project (`df.to_csv`).` and the question "This cell writes to `~/exports/trips.csv`, outside the project. Run it as it is?".

**The cell key** (`approvals.cell_key`)
- `pending_key("cell\n" + notebook + "\n" + target + "\n" + normalised code)`: the notebook's path relative to the project (`ref.rel_path`, so `notebook=None` and the active notebook's path are one key), and `target` = `add` for `nh_add_cell`, `edit:<uid>` for `nh_edit_cell` (the resolved code cell's uid, whichever id form the call used).
- Normalised code: line endings as `\n`, each line's trailing whitespace dropped, blank lines at the start and end dropped. Nothing else: indentation, inner lines, comments and every other character count. So a resent call that differs only by a trailing newline or trailing spaces is the same call; any other code change is a new question.
- Not in the key: `title`, `notes`, `intent`, `after_cell_id`, `base_sha`. "Send the same call again" holds even when the note is reworded or the cell lands elsewhere.
- The ledger keeps only the sha256 hex (6.1): never the code, the question or the rules.

**The gate** (`approvals.gate_cell(svc, turn, key, asks)`)
- Called by `add_cell` and `edit_cell` inside `svc.locks.hold`, after E120 and the E121 check, before the before-probe and the insert or update:
  - add: E110, E133, E121, lint (E120), **asks**, probe, insert, run;
  - edit: the cell and turn checks (E140, E145, E133, E113, E112, E117, E118, E111), E121, the user-change checks (E144, E141), lint (E120), **asks**, probe, update, run.
- With no asks nothing below runs. With asks:

| Situation (in this order) | Result |
|---|---|
| headless (`NH_HEADLESS=1`, `config.headless()`) | E122 with the headless Next; the ledger is neither read nor written, so nothing is recorded and nothing is granted, a yes record included |
| `use_grant(session, record, turn, "cell", key)`: this message is a yes ("go" only alone, 6.1) and the pending question is the previous message's, with this key; a writer's question only when its run reported in the asking message's reply before this message opened (C5d2, C5d3, "A yes typed during the run") | the call goes on; `use_grant` cleared the pending question, so the grant is used once |
| this message already asked this key | E122 with the same question again (no new pending). The main conversation's call while the asking message is still the record's turn calls `asked_directly` first: its Next has it ask the user itself, so a writer's question whose run hasn't reported becomes its own (C5d2, "A yes typed during the run") |
| this message already asked another question (a cell or, later, a re-run) | E122 "already waiting for the user's answer", with no question |
| this message's yes grants the pending question (`grant()` with that question's own kind and key, and `reported_runs()`) and this call has another key | E122 "held": the yes stays for the call it approved; nothing is recorded |
| otherwise | `set_pending("cell", key, turn, run_id=…)` (the writer's run, `TurnContext.run_id`; None for the main conversation), replacing an earlier message's pending, as 6.1's table drops or keeps it; then E122 with the question |

- `record` is `turn_record.read()` at that moment; `turn` is the call's turn in `TurnContext` (the canonical human turn, or the writer's run turn). `use_grant` applies 6.1's `grant()` table, so any other answer ("no", other text, "go on", "yes but …"), a yes Claude Code absorbs mid-turn at a tool boundary (never an answer, 6.1; one queued after the turn's last tool call is the next message: Known gaps), a yes two messages later, a yes with another key, or a yes to a writer's question typed before its run reported (C5d2) grants nothing. A no or other answer drops the old question; this call then asks its own.
- `set_pending` can't refuse in the last row: it refuses only a second question of the same message, which the rows above already caught under the same lock.
- **Held.** In the yes message a different asked-for call doesn't replace the approved question, so the user's yes isn't lost to a cell they never saw. Its Next says to send the approved call first; once that is written, E110 holds the rest of the message, and the other cell asks in a later message. A yes message that never sends the approved call drops it at the next message (6.1).
- **Clearing.** A successful `nh_add_cell` or `nh_edit_cell` (its run started: after `start_run` returns, still inside the lock) calls `clear_pending(session, turn)`: the cell this message wrote supersedes this message's question, so a later yes grants nothing. A write that fails to start (rolled back) clears nothing, so its question stands. A pending question of an earlier message is left to `grant()`.
- **Single use.** A granted call is this message's one cell: a second identical `nh_add_cell` gets E110, and a retry that still asks (an `nh_edit_cell` whose fix keeps the install) asks again, since the grant is spent. A grant whose write then fails to start (rollback) is spent too: nh asks again.
- **E110.** A granted call adds no third exception to E110 (§0, 6.0 c): E110 comes before lint, so when the message already has its cell, the call gets E110 and the grant is not used (the next message drops it).
- **E109 first.** An explain, plan or ask message is refused at the gate (6.2), before the tool, so it records no pending question.
- **Writers.** nh:cell-writer's calls run the same gate with their run's turn (`_writer_turn`), so no thread writes an asked-for cell without a yes. Its E122 records the pending question under that turn, the same key and its run (`run_id`), so the main conversation's identical call in the user's yes message is granted (as is an identical writer call there), once the run's report reached the asking message's reply before the yes message opened (C5d3), or the main conversation asked the question itself (`asked_directly`): a yes typed during the run grants nothing (C5d2, "A yes typed during the run" below). Its Next is `RETURN_TO_WORKFLOW` and the question goes in a detail line (below); the workflow carries it to the main conversation as the outcome `needs_approval` (C5d, "Ultracode: the writer's question" below).
- **Locking.** The grant check and use, `set_pending` and `clear_pending` all run inside `svc.locks.hold(notebook, turn)`. Two calls of one message are serialised by its turn lock: of two identical granted calls, one is written and the other gets E110 (an edit: E133 while the first one's run is going, E112 once it is ok), and no pending question is left behind. `test_approvals.py` proves it for `nh_add_cell` and `nh_edit_cell`: with the check moved before the lock, the second call asks again and leaves a pending question.
- **No record.** A question asked in a message with no turn record (the `/nh:init` message) is never granted: the next message has no `prev_turn_id`, so it asks there again.

**E122** (`policy/errors.py`)
- First line: "Not {verb}: this needs the user's yes first." Detail: one line per ask, `- L009: <message>`, `- L012: <message>`, `- L013: <message>`, in `_CHECKS` order.
- Next, by case:

| Case | Next (the question in single quotes) |
|---|---|
| asked, or asked again | `Ask the user, then stop: '<question>'. After a yes, send the same call again.` |
| already waiting | detail adds "- nh is already waiting for the user's answer to this message's first question; it asks one at a time."; Next: "Ask the user only nh's first question of this message, then stop; write nothing more until they answer." |
| held | detail adds "- The user's yes in this message is for the other cell nh asked about, and it covers only that exact call."; Next: "Send the call the user said yes to first, exactly as before (same tool, cell and code); propose this cell in your reply instead." |
| headless | `No one can answer here (NH_HEADLESS=1): write nothing, and tell the user this cell needs their yes in an interactive session: '<question>'` |
| nh:cell-writer (any case) | detail adds `- The main conversation asks the user: '<question>'` (or the waiting, held or headless line); Next: `RETURN_TO_WORKFLOW` |

- **The question** (`approvals.question(asks)`): "This cell " + the asks' clauses joined by "; it also " + ". Run it as it is?", e.g. "This cell installs `seaborn` into the kernel only, and the next env sync removes it (`uv add seaborn` keeps it). Run it as it is?", or with both rules "This cell installs `seaborn` into the kernel only, and the next env sync removes it (`uv add seaborn` keeps it); it also connects to `data.example.org` over the network. Run it as it is?". An ask without a clause (only a hand-built `Config` makes one) reads "trips nh's rule <id> (<message>)". A yes means "write and run this exact cell"; anything else drops it. The question is redacted with the installed Redactor (`secrets.current().redact`, 6.8) when built, and the whole refusal again by `NhError`, so no secret (a token in a `pip install git+https://…` URL) reaches it.
- E122 doesn't count as a lint reject, and it is in `CATALOGUE`, so 6.2's doc-row test requires its `errors.md` and `troubleshooting.md` rows.

**Events** (`.nh/log.jsonl`, never code or the question)

| Event | Fields |
|---|---|
| `cell_asked` | `session_id`, `turn_id`, `rules` (e.g. `["L009"]`), `outcome`: `asked`, `repeated`, `waiting`, `held` or `headless` |
| `cell_granted` | `session_id`, `turn_id`, `rules` |

**Spike V13: the gate under `claude -p`** (C5e; plan "Spikes" V13, its E122 part; the hook part ran in C0)
- **Setup.** Five cases, each in its own scratch copy of `dev/sandbox` (`nhctl env sync`, its own JupyterLab from `nhctl lab start`), the plugin from the repo (`--plugin-dir`), `--allowedTools "Skill,Read,mcp__plugin_nh_nh"` (the v0.1.1 acceptance runs' list without Workflow and TaskStop: no qa-cell run), the default permission mode and no permission prompt tool (as V13 (hook)). The cell asked for is `!uv pip install --offline --dry-run pandas`: L009 asks, and a granted run changes nothing. A second message in the same process goes through `spikes/v0.2/tools/driver.py` (stream-json input); a later one through `claude -p "<text>" --resume <session>`, a new process with a new gateway.
- Every run inherited the environment of the remote Claude Code session that ran the spike: `CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT=remote` (Claude Code keeps an inherited entrypoint, so the hooks and gateways saw `remote`, not a user's `sdk-cli`), its child-session and attended flags and its session id, so each first run passed its own `--session-id`.
- **Results** (all PASS; events from `.nh/log.jsonl`, `pending` from the ledger file where a snapshot was kept, the notebook from its `.ipynb` after JupyterLab's save):

| Case | Messages | Gateway | Notebook |
|---|---|---|---|
| A: next-message yes | 1: the cell; 2: "yes" (driver, after 1's result); 3: asks for the same cell again (`--resume`, no yes) | 1: E122 `asked`, `pending` 1's (no snapshot: the driver sent "yes" 0.7 s after 1's result; the grant in 2 needs it, and C's and E's snapshots after 1 hold 1's question); 2: the identical call, `cell_granted` then `cell_added`, `pending` null; 3: the same cell (same key; its `intent` and `notes` differ), E122 `asked` again, `pending` 3's | the cell written once, in 2 |
| B: no | 1; 2: "no"; 3: asks for the cell after all (`--resume`, no yes) | 2: no nh call, and the file still holds 1's question; 3: E122 `asked` (not `repeated` or `held`), `pending` 3's | nothing written |
| C: two processes | `claude -p "<1>"`, then `claude -p "yes" --resume <session>` | 1: E122 `asked`, `pending` 1's; 2: `cell_granted`, `cell_added`, `pending` null | the cell written once, in 2 |
| D: `NH_HEADLESS=1` (on the `claude` process) | 1; 2: "yes" (driver); 3: "yes" (`--resume`) | E122 `headless` in each; no ledger file is ever written | nothing written |
| E: a yes mid-turn | 1, with "yes" written to stdin as `nh_add_cell`'s tool_use appeared (driver `--n 1 --delay 0`), before nh asked; 2: "yes" (`--resume`) | 1: E122 `asked`, then `turn_absorbed` (answer yes, logged, not applied); the model's identical retry in 1: E122 `repeated`; 2: `cell_granted`, `cell_added` | the cell written once, in 2 |

- **Review probes** (C5e's review; the same setup, each once):

| Probe | What was sent | What happened |
|---|---|---|
| P2 | "yes" written to stdin right after message 1 | Claude Code merged it into message 1: one `turn_open` (`prompt_chars` 247 + 1 + 3), no answer; E122 `asked`; nothing written |
| P3 | "yes" written 0.5 s after message 1's E122 result, 3.5 s before the asking reply's text (driver `--n 1 --delay 0.6`) | no tool call followed, so it wasn't absorbed: Claude Code queued it (the transcript's `queue-operation`) and opened it as the next message once the reply ended (`answer` yes, `prev_turn_id` 1): `cell_granted`, `cell_added` once, `pending` null |
| environment | under `claude -p`: a probe MCP server and hooks with no inherited entrypoint, then a probe plugin and nh itself | hooks and the server see `CLAUDE_CODE_ENTRYPOINT=sdk-cli`, and the server's parent argv holds `-p`; the hook payload (`permission_mode` `default`) and the MCP `clientInfo` name no mode. With `CLAUDE_CODE_MCP_ALLOWLIST_ENV=1` (by Claude Code's code, also the `local-agent` entrypoint) a stdio server gets an allowlisted environment plus its own `env` only: `NH_HEADLESS=1` reached a server only through its config. A plugin whose `.mcp.json` `env` has `"NH_HEADLESS": "${NH_HEADLESS:-}"` got `1` when it was set (under the allowlist too) and `""` when not; so did nh's gateway once its `.mcp.json` had it (read from the gateway process's environment; `plugin:nh:nh` connected) |
| mixed processes | `NH_HEADLESS` changed between processes of one session, in `test_approvals.py` (each process a new gateway on one project, as `--resume` starts one) | headless, then interactive: m1 `headless` (nothing recorded), m2 "yes" `asked`, m3 "yes" `granted`. Interactive, headless, interactive: m1 `asked`, m2 "yes" `headless` (the file keeps m1's question), m3 "yes" `asked` (its `prev_turn_id` is m2) |

- **What it shows.**
  - A `-p` run without `NH_HEADLESS=1` is interactive to the gateway: the interactive E122, and a yes in the next message, scripted through stream-json or sent with `--resume`, grants the exact call once (a resent call that names no `notebook` only if the asking gateway's active notebook was harness.toml's: Known gaps). It fails closed all the same: nothing is written before that yes.
  - nh doesn't treat `-p` as headless by itself, though Claude Code marks a print-mode run (6.0 b): a print-mode run can still carry a person's next message (A, C), and refusing it would refuse that yes. Making `sdk-cli` headless by default would be a design change, left to the user.
  - `NH_HEADLESS=1` set on the `claude` process reaches the gateway (D), and then no yes grants anything, a resumed one included. Claude Code passes its whole environment to a stdio server by default; since C5e the nh server's own `env` in `.mcp.json` forwards `NH_HEADLESS` too (`${NH_HEADLESS:-}`: `""` when unset, which isn't headless), so it also arrives when Claude Code passes only an allowlisted environment. `NH_JUPYTER_URL` and `NH_JUPYTER_TOKEN` aren't forwarded that way (outside C5).
  - Mixing `NH_HEADLESS` across a session's processes fails closed both ways: a headless E122 records nothing, so after it the user's first interactive yes gets nh's question and only the next yes grants; a headless "yes" to an interactive question grants nothing, and the next interactive yes asks anew (its `prev_turn_id` is the headless message).
  - The grant is single-use (A 2: `pending` null after `cell_granted`), and no approval stands (A 3: the same cell asks again); after a no, the next gated call drops the old question and asks anew (B 3); the ledger carries the question across processes (C, E 2).
  - A yes Claude Code absorbs mid-turn, at a tool boundary, is no answer, also when it was typed before the question existed (E 1), and it leaves the question for the next message (E 2). A yes sent with the first message is part of it (P2). A yes queued after the asking turn's last tool call is the next message and grants (P3; Known gaps).
