  - A no is applied lazily: `grant()` runs when a gated call reads the ledger, so the file keeps the question until then (B 2); no later message can grant it (6.1's table: from the next message on, its `turn_id` is no longer `prev_turn_id`).
  - The model mostly followed E122's Next. In A, B and C, and in E's reply, it asked nh's question verbatim, then stopped; in the yes message it sent the identical call first. In E 1 it took the absorbed yes as an answer and sent the identical call once more in the same message (E122 `repeated`) before asking. Headless (D), it paraphrased the question, ended message 1 by asking whether to run the cell as written, and after each "yes" sent the call once more, refused each time; nothing was written.

**Ultracode: the writer's question** (C5d; plan D4 "Ultracode", the {main, writer} axis of the plan's approval matrix)
- nh:cell-writer can't ask the user (it works in the background, for the workflow). Its E122 already records the pending question for the main conversation (Writers, above); C5d carries the question and the exact call to the main conversation, which asks and, after a yes, sends the call itself, with no gateway code change; C5d2 makes the gate wait for that report ("A yes typed during the run" below).

| Step | Who | What |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | nh:cell-writer | its `nh_add_cell` or `nh_edit_cell` gets E122 with the line `- The main conversation asks the user: '<question>'` (`approvals.WRITER_QUESTION`): the gate recorded the pending question under the run's turn (the human message that launched the run), the call's key and the run's id (`run_id`, C5d2) |
| 2 | nh:cell-writer | returns at once (`agents/cell-writer.md`): status `needs_approval`, `result` the refusal verbatim, `call` the exact call it sent. It doesn't retry, rewrite or split the cell, never drops or moves what nh asked about (the URL, the path) to get past the question, and never asks the user |
| 3 | `qa-cell.js` | runs no QA round on an answer that ends on an E122 (QA never sees a cell nh didn't write); `report()` returns the outcome `needs_approval` with `approval: {question, tool, args}` |
| 4 | the main conversation, replying to the report (a notification: an alias of the asking message, whose hook marks the run done: `done_ts`, and `done_turn` the asking message, C5d3) | asks the user `approval.question` as it stands, then stops: no cell, no new run. Only the user's next message can be the yes (the reply turn's calls: below) |
| 5 | the main conversation, in the user's next message | a yes ("go" alone too): sends `approval.tool` with `approval.args` itself, before anything else; the gate grants it once (6.1's `grant()`: the pending question's turn is the yes message's `prev_turn_id`, and its run's report reached the asking message's reply (`done_turn`, C5d3) with its `done_ts` before the yes message's `ts`: the user saw the question), as that message's one cell (then E110 for an add, E112 for an edit). QA doesn't check it. Anything else drops it (`asks.md`) |

- **The reply turn** (step 4) is still the asking message, so a call there is gated with the writer's question pending in that same turn:

| The main conversation's call in the reply turn | Result |
|---|---|
| the approved call | E122 "repeated": the same question again, nothing recorded; a revision of the message's OK cell gets E112 first |
| another cell nh asks about | E122 "already waiting" (the writer's question was first) |
| another cell that asks nothing (the install, the URL or the path dropped) | written as the message's cell; it clears the writer's question (Clearing, above), so the user's later yes grants nothing and the approved call asks again. Nothing is written unasked (it fails closed), but the user gets a cell they didn't ask for in place of the question |

- **Leave to write.** The pinned `QA_REPORT` reminder says "write no new cell unless its writer wrote none", and after `needs_approval` the writer wrote none; that exception must not read as leave to write here (as for an explain turn, 6.2). `qa-workflow.md` says "Write nothing now", and `WORKFLOW_AGAIN_REASON` (`pre_tool.py`, not pinned), the denial of a second launch in the message, now adds "unless the report says needs_approval: then ask its question and stop" after "if its writer wrote no cell, write the cell yourself".
- **A report for an earlier message** (`QA_EARLIER`: the user wrote since the launch). The question still belongs to the asking message, and the message the user wrote during the run is the next one after it. Even a whole-message yes there ("ok", "yes", "go") grants nothing: the run reported after that message opened, so the user hadn't seen nh's question (C5d2, "A yes typed during the run" below). `qa-workflow.md` has the main conversation neither ask nor send the call in that reply (`QA_EARLIER`: "change no cell for it"): it says the cell isn't written because nh needs the user's yes, and what it would do. If the user then asks for the cell, the call sent in that later message finds the question two messages old, so 6.1 drops it and nh asks anew. `test_approvals.py` and `test_qa_workflow.py` pin both.

**The report** (`report()`; the comment above it lists the outcomes)
- Outcome order: `no_ask` and `writer_failed` (as before), then **`needs_approval`**, then `checked` or `not_checked` (a cell was written), then `refused` or `not_written`.
- QA never runs on a writer answer whose `result` holds an `nh: E122` line (any of E122's writer lines, with or without a usable `call`), whatever `status` and `wrote` the writer gives: the writer's last call wrote nothing, so QA would check an earlier version against nh's refusal.
- `needs_approval` needs both of these, from the writer answer that ended the run (the first write or a revision):

| Part | From | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| `question` | the writer's `result`, split into lines at LF (CR LF and a lone CR read as LF) | it holds a line `nh: E122` and a line `- The main conversation asks the user: '<question>'`; the last such line's text between the outer quotes, unchanged. The workflow never builds or rewords a question: the gateway words it (6.8 redacts it) and the main conversation's own E122 Next would carry the same words. Only LF ends a line: L013 escapes C0 control characters and DEL in a path, but not U+0085, U+2028 or U+2029, and JavaScript's `.` and multiline `$` stop at the last two, so each line is matched whole (`[^\n]*`, no `m` flag); L009's and L012's parts hold none of them |
| `tool` | the writer's `call.tool` | `nh_add_cell` or `nh_edit_cell` |
| `args` | the writer's `call` | `code` verbatim (a string with non-blank text, never trimmed: indentation counts in the key); then `cell_id` (an edit; required), `after_cell_id` (an add), `notebook`, `title`, `notes` (a list of strings, or one string) and `intent`, those the writer gave, trimmed. Any other field, and a field the tool doesn't take (`cell_id` on an add, `after_cell_id` on an edit, `base_sha`), is dropped, so `args` is a valid call of `tool` |

- Without both, the outcome falls through as before: `checked` or `not_checked` when a version was written, else `refused` when the writer said `refused` or `needs_approval`, else `not_written`. A note says why, by the writer's E122 line (E122 above; the JS matches the start of the waiting, held and headless lines, and the tests tie them to `approvals.WAITING_LINE`, `HELD_LINE` and `WRITER_HEADLESS`):

| The writer's answer | Note |
|---|---|
| nh's question, no usable `call` | "nh asked for the user's yes (E122), but the writer didn't return the exact call, so there is no call to send after a yes." |
| E122 with the already-waiting line | "nh was already waiting for the user's answer to an earlier question of this message, which the writer didn't return, so there is nothing to ask." |
| E122 with the held line | "nh keeps this message's yes for the other cell the user approved, so it didn't write the writer's cell." |
| E122 with the headless line (it holds nh's question) | "No one can answer here (NH_HEADLESS=1): the cell needs the user's yes in an interactive session." |
| status `needs_approval`, none of these lines | "The writer said nh asked for the user's yes, but its nh result holds no question from nh, so there is nothing to ask." |

- Each of these leaves the outcome without a call to send after a yes, so `qa-workflow.md` has the main conversation not ask nh's question (Known gaps: a question nobody relays). An E122 line stays in `result` only when the writer's last answer was the one that ended the run (a revision that asked leaves the checked version's `result`; its note says so).
- A writer answer with an E122 and `wrote` true (it wrote a version before the call nh asked about) adds "The cell the writer wrote before nh's E122 is in the notebook, not QA-checked; one undo removes it." on the first write; on a revision's fix, the existing "The last revision left the cell not ok; one undo restores the notebook to before this message." says it when its status isn't `ok`, and without `approval` "nh's E122 ended the writer's fix of revision n, so QA didn't check it." is added.
- `approval` is null for every other outcome. The rest of the report keeps its contract (§3.7): `result` drops the refusal's `Next:` and `Writer:` lines, so the question line stays in it.
- `WRITER_SCHEMA`'s `status` gains `needs_approval`, and the schema gains the optional `call` object (`tool`, `code`, `cell_id`, `after_cell_id`, `notebook`, `title`, `notes`, `intent`; `tool` and `code` required).

| The call nh asked about | The notebook | The report |
|---|---|---|
| the first write (`nh_add_cell`, or `nh_edit_cell` of the cell the launch named) | unchanged | `needs_approval`; no QA round, `qa.verdict` `unchecked`; a note: "nh asked for the user's yes before writing the writer's last call (E122), so QA checked nothing." |
| the writer's fix of its own cell after that cell's run failed (`nh_edit_cell`; say its fix reads the file from a URL) | the failed version stays (`wrote` true) | `needs_approval` with `approval.tool` `nh_edit_cell` on that cell; no QA round; the same note, then "The cell the writer wrote before nh's E122 is in the notebook, not QA-checked; one undo removes it." (`cell` names it; the writer's `changes` say how it failed) |
| a revision from QA findings | the version QA checked stays | `needs_approval`; `qa` as for that version (`final_version_checked` true, its verdict and open findings), `revisions` doesn't count it, `result` stays that version's, and the asking revision's `changes` (what it would change) join `changes`; a note: "nh asked for the user's yes before writing revision n (E122), so the cell is still the version QA checked." |
| the writer's fix of a revision whose run failed | that failed revision stays (counted) | `needs_approval`; `qa` unchecked for it (its findings in `earlier_findings`); a note: "nh asked for the user's yes before writing the writer's fix of revision n (E122), so QA didn't check it." |

**Main's retry is the writer's key** (`cell_key(notebook, target, code)`, above)

| Key part | The writer's call | The main conversation's call from `approval` |
|---|---|---|
| notebook | `ref.rel_path` of its `notebook`, or of the active notebook when it gave none | the same `notebook`, or none: the writer's call made that notebook the active one, and only a write tool changes it, which the main conversation doesn't call in between. That holds within one gateway: a new one (an `/mcp` reconnect, a `claude -p --resume` process) starts on harness.toml's notebook (Known gaps) |
| target | `add`; or `edit:<uid>` of the cell its `cell_id` names | the same tool and `cell_id`: any id form of the cell resolves to the same uid |
| code | as sent | `args.code`, unchanged |
| (not in the key) | `after_cell_id`, `title`, `notes`, `intent` | sent as given, so the cell lands where the writer put it; the main conversation writes its own `title`, `notes` or `intent` when one is missing (an add needs them) |

- The call shapes this covers, each tested in `test_approvals.py`, and end to end in `test_qa_workflow.py` (there the add has `after_cell_id` and `notebook`): an add with or without `after_cell_id` and `notebook`; an edit of the cell the launch named (an earlier message's cell); the writer's edit of its own just-added cell after a failed run; a revision's edit. An edit in the yes message is that message's one cell (its state has no claims, so no E112 or E113), sent with no `base_sha` (the writer sends none either): the cell is nh's, and E141 still stops it if the user changed the cell since.
- A writer whose `call` isn't what it sent (other code) makes the main conversation's call another key: in the yes message it is "held" (the yes stays for the approved key) and nothing is written. The gate fails closed; the user is asked again later.

**Which thread may send the approved call** (decided in C5d). The grant is keyed to the call, not the thread, as before C5d: 6.1's `grant()` and the hash-only pending question hold no thread. C5d2's `run_id` names the run whose report must reach the user before the yes, not who may send the call.
- In the yes message the main conversation's identical call is granted once, and so is the identical call of a writer in a run launched in that message: that run works for the yes message (its turn), and the cell is the one the user approved. C5d2 and C5d3 keep both: the asking run reported in the asking message's reply before the yes message opened, which is all their condition asks. `qa-workflow.md` has the main conversation send it itself: a new writer composes its code afresh, so its cell would rarely be the same. A different cell nh asks about gets "held" (the yes stays for the approved call, which the main conversation can still send after the run). A different cell that asks nothing (the URL or the path dropped) is written as the yes message's cell, and the approved call then gets E110: the user's yes is lost, though nothing is written unasked. INSTRUCTIONS rule 10 ("launch the nh:qa-cell workflow instead of writing") still applies under ultracode and pushes toward that relaunch; only `qa-workflow.md` ("When to start it") excludes the yes to a `needs_approval` question (Known gaps).
- A run of the asking message, or of any earlier one, never reaches the gate in the yes message: `_writer_turn` refuses it with E107 first (unchanged), so it neither uses nor drops the grant. The main conversation's call after it is granted only if the run reported in the asking message's reply before the yes message opened (C5d2, C5d3): a run whose writer still calls in the yes message hasn't reported, so that call asks anew ("A yes typed during the run", below).
- Why not main only: the pending question would have to record a thread, and the approved cell would be refused to a writer working for the very message that approved it, while either thread can send only the exact call.
- Two messages later, no thread is granted (6.1).

**A yes typed during the run** (decided in C5d2; closes C5d's Known gap of that name)
- The gap: the writer's E122 records the pending question under the asking message (p1) as soon as the writer asks, but the user sees the question only when the run's report reaches the main conversation. A whole-message yes ("ok", "yes", "go") typed during the run is the next human message (p2), so 6.0 b counted it as a yes to a question the user never saw: the exact call was granted in p2 and in the report's turn (an alias of p2), and only text guarded it (`QA_EARLIER`, `qa-workflow.md`). A yes means "write and run this exact cell": the user must have seen the question.
- The rule: a pending question that a writer's run recorded (`run_id`) is grantable only when that run's report reached the main conversation before the yes message opened, i.e. the run is marked done (`done_ts`, set by the notification hook's `turn_record.mark_done`) at a time earlier than the yes message's turn record `ts` (when the human message opened; aliases keep it), and in the asking message's reply: the hook keeps the human turn the notification aliased (`done_turn`, C5d3), which must be the pending question's turn, so no clock decides it alone (Clocks, below). The main conversation's own questions (`run_id` None) are unchanged, as is a writer's question the main conversation asks itself ("The main conversation asks it itself", below).

| The writer asked in p1; the user's next message p2 is a yes | The run's `done_ts` | The exact call in p2 or one of its aliases |
|---|---|---|
| typed after the report (the normal flow: the reply asked, the user answered) | before p2's `ts`, `done_turn` p1 | granted once |
| typed during the run, before the report | none when p2 opened; after p2's `ts` once the report arrives (its notification aliases p2: `QA_EARLIER`, `done_turn` p2) | not granted: E122 with nh's question, recorded as p2's (`run_id` None), so a yes in p3 grants it; no "held" |
| the run was stopped (TaskStop) or never reported | status `killed` (`done_turn` None), or none | not granted, as above |
| the same, but first the main conversation sent the identical call in p1 (E108 lets it once the run is stopped or past its hour) and got E122 "repeated" | status `killed`, or none | granted once: that E122's Next had the main conversation ask the user itself, so `asked_directly` made the question its own (`run_id` None) |
| a writer of a run launched in p2 sends the identical call | the first run's, before p2's `ts`, `done_turn` p1 | granted once ("Which thread may send the approved call", above) |

- `grant()` returns no grant and keeps the pending question as it is: this reply doesn't drop it, and 6.1's rules drop it at the next message as before. "Held" doesn't apply either: `gate_cell` checks `grant()` with the pending question's own kind and key and the same `reported`, so another asked-for call in p2 records its own question.
- `TurnLedger.use_grant` and `gate_cell`'s "held" check pass `reported_runs(layout, session, pending)` (6.1), which reads the runs only when the pending question has a `run_id`; `grant()` stays pure. A writer's E122 records `run_id=TurnContext.run_id`; the main conversation's records None.
- A stopped run: TaskStop marks a run done with status `killed` (§3.7, `post_tool.STOPPED`), though it sends no report, so `reported_runs` skips status `killed` (`turn_record.STOPPED`; a killed run's notification would carry no report either, even in the asking message's reply): only the notification hook's mark is a report. TaskStop's mark keeps no turn either (`done_turn` None, C5d3), and as the first mark it stays. This reading only fails closed.
- **The main conversation asks it itself** (`ledger.asked_directly`, 6.1; found in C5d2's review). Once the run is stopped, or past its hour with no report, E108 lets the main conversation's writes through in the asking message, and its identical call gets E122 "repeated" with `ASK_NEXT`: it asks the user itself, so the yes needn't wait for a report that will never come. `gate_cell` calls `asked_directly(session, turn)` for the main conversation's call (never a writer's) when the record's turn is still the asking message: the question's `run_id` becomes None, unless its run reported (then a later yes is granted as it is and nothing is written). That is safe as far as nh can see: a message typed during the main conversation's turn is absorbed at a tool boundary and never an answer (6.1), so the next human message comes after its reply's last tool call; one typed after that, while the reply is still being written, is queued as the next message and grants ("The reply's own race", below; Known gaps). A call that `canonical()` maps to the asking message through `earlier` after a later message opened changes nothing: that message may have been typed before the question was shown.
- **The reply's own race** (found in C5e's review, V13 probe P3). `done_turn` and `done_ts` show that the report reached the asking message's reply, not that the user read the question. A yes typed after the report arrived, while the reply that asks is still being written (after its last tool call), is queued by Claude Code as the next message, so it is granted as a yes typed after the reply is: the same race as the main conversation's own question (Known gaps). A yes typed during the run, before the report, still grants nothing.
- Fails closed: a run the runs file no longer lists (it keeps `MAX_RUNS`, 20), a `done_ts` that isn't a number, an unreadable record `ts` (0), a system clock stepped back between the report and the yes (both times are the hooks' `time.time()`), a report that reached no message's reply (an orphan: `done_turn` None), or a run marked done before C5d3 (no `done_turn`) grants nothing, and nh asks again.
- Clocks (closed in C5d3): the time check alone compared two wall-clock times written by two hook processes, so a clock stepped back between a yes typed during the run and the report (an NTP step, a manual change, a VM resume) could make `done_ts` read earlier than the yes message's `ts`. The notification hook now passes the open human turn to `mark_done` (`done_turn`), and `reported_runs` counts a run only when that is the pending question's turn: a report that reached the yes message's reply never counts, whatever the clocks say. That assumes Claude Code runs one prompt's UserPromptSubmit hook at a time (it submits a queued message at a tool boundary, V16; no spike measured two prompts' hooks overlapping). If the yes message's hook read the turn record first and wrote its own only after the notification's hook had read the old one, `done_turn` would be the asking message and only the time check would refuse that yes; every overlapped order is still no worse than C5d2 (a notification hook that writes last overwrites the yes message's record, so the yes message's calls get E102). The time check stays as a second condition. `test_approvals.py` sets a reported run's `done_ts` before the yes message's `ts`, as a clock step leaves it, and nothing is granted; and, for a report in the asking message's reply, after it: nothing is granted or "held".
- Hooks: C5d2 changed none (the notification hook marks the run done when the report arrives, `prompt_submit.notification`, and `opened()` stamps a human message's `ts`); C5d3 has that notification pass the open human turn to `mark_done` (None for an orphan), still Python 3.9 stdlib.
- `qa-workflow.md`'s "Report for an earlier message" drops its reason ("the message the user wrote meanwhile may count as a yes to a question they never saw"), which the gate now refuses, and keeps "Don't ask, and don't send the call in this reply" (`QA_EARLIER`: change no cell for it) and "If the user then asks for the cell, send the call in that message: nh asks its question then." If the main conversation sends the call in that reply anyway, nh asks (E122), and the user's yes to that question in the next message grants it.

**Model-facing text**
- `skills/notebook/reference/asks.md` (new): on E122, ask the question in chat, one question, then stop; after the user's yes (or "go" alone) in the next message send the exact same call again, before anything else (same tool, cell and code; title and notes may change); a no or anything else drops it; never rephrase the code between the question and the retry; a second ask in a message gets "already waiting"; a different asked-for call in the yes message gets "held"; the yes is for the next message and one call, still the message's one cell; `uv add` with Bash (in a conda project environment.yml and `nhctl env sync`) is the preferred way to install, an install in a cell the fallback the user can approve; re-running a cell with `nh_run` asks nothing, so ask first; headless (`NH_HEADLESS=1`) can't ask; nh:cell-writer returns E122 to the workflow.
- `skills/notebook/SKILL.md`: one pointer line under "Rules". The install bullet under "Never" keeps "Ask first; after a yes, run `uv add <pkg>`", names environment.yml and `nhctl env sync` for conda, and points an install in a cell at E122; "tidy" is rewrapped to one line, so the file stays at 150 lines.
- `reference/errors.md`: an E122 row; the L009 row of the E120 table says it applies only when `harness.toml` makes it an error (`"error"`, or `"hint"` under strict mode) and that by default nh asks first (E122). `reference/tools.md`: the same for its L009 bullet. The conda advice there and in the `ModuleNotFoundError` row is environment.yml and `nhctl env sync`, as in E120's Next.
- INSTRUCTIONS stay as they are: rule 9 already says "Ask before installing packages or writing outside the project." (6.2). The `nh_add_cell` description ("no package installs") is unchanged.
- C5b: `asks.md` names the network cell (a URL read from a host that isn't approved, a download, `requests`, `curl`) beside the install, says not to ask before the call (a yes nh didn't ask for grants nothing, so the user would be asked twice: send the call and let nh ask), that a yes approves this exact cell once and never the host, and how a host is approved for good: `/nh:init` adds its data URL's host to `.nh/state/approved_hosts.json`, a list the user can also edit; Claude never writes it and nh has no command for it. Its "Anything else" bullet adds the network fallback: ask the user to download the file into the project (for example `data/raw/`), then read it there; never fetch it with Bash, WebFetch or another cell on your own. `errors.md`: an L012 row in the E120 table (an error only when `harness.toml` makes it one, by default nh asks first, E122) and the E122 row names network cells. `tools.md`: an L012 bullet beside L009's. `SKILL.md` is unchanged (its E122 pointer line covers both; it stays at 150 lines). The init skill's step 3 says that when `data.approved_host` is set (an http(s), s3 or similar URL; never a database URL, `file://` or localhost), scaffold approved that host, and the report says so; `first-cell.md` says why the plain URL cell runs with no question.
- C5c: `asks.md` names the outside-write cell beside the install and the network cell (a file or folder the cell writes, creates or removes outside the project: `df.to_csv("~/…")`, `plt.savefig("/…")`, `open("../../x.txt", "w")`, `shutil.copy`, `!cp`, `>`, `%%writefile`), that its question names the paths, that a relative path starts from the notebook's folder (`../data/processed/` from `notebooks/` is inside), and to write inside the project unless the user named the place; its "Anything else" bullet adds the fallback: write inside the project (for example `data/processed/` or `reports/`) and tell the user where the file is. `errors.md`: an L013 row in the E120 table (an error only when `harness.toml` makes it one; by default nh asks first, E122), the E120 lead says "except for L009, L012 and L013", and the E122 row names outside writes. `tools.md`: an L013 bullet beside L012's ("Four rules differ"), and the default-ask sentence names outside writes. `SKILL.md` is unchanged: its "Never" bullet already says "write outside the project unasked" and its E122 pointer line covers the question (150 lines). INSTRUCTIONS stay as they are (rule 9: "Ask before installing packages or writing outside the project.").
- C5d (no INSTRUCTIONS or `SKILL.md` change: rule 10 and the skill's qa-workflow pointer line stand, and `SKILL.md` stays at 150 lines):
  - `agents/cell-writer.md`: on E122, stop at once and return it: status `needs_approval`, `result` the refusal verbatim, and `call` the exact call (`tool`, `code` character for character, and the `cell_id`, `after_cell_id`, `notebook`, `title`, `notes` and `intent` it passed); never retry it, change its code or write another cell instead, never drop or move what nh asked about (the URL, the path) to get past the question, never ask the user; if a later call got E122 "already waiting" anyway, return the first E122 and its call. Its "Never" list says "write outside the project unasked", as `SKILL.md` does, where it said "write files" (a cell that writes inside the project was never meant).
  - The writer and installs (decided in C5d): `cell-writer.md` keeps "Install packages" under "Never" with its `no_write` rule (an ask that needs a package that isn't installed: write nothing, name it). The writer can't run Bash, so it can't do the better install (`uv add`, `asks.md`); `no_write` hands the ask back to the main conversation, which can. Its E122 bullet names the network and writes outside the project, the asks a writer meets; a writer that writes an install anyway and gets E122 still returns it as `needs_approval` rather than dropping it. The gateway tests' writer cells are installs: the gate is the same for every ask rule.
  - `agents/cell-qa.md`: QA sees only a cell nh wrote and ran, never one waiting for the user's yes; a `fix` that installs a package, downloads or writes outside the project can't be written now (the writer installs nothing; nh asks the user about the rest, E122), so the cell stays as QA checked it: prefer a fix without one.
  - `reference/qa-workflow.md`: "When to start it" excludes the user's answer to a `needs_approval` question (send its call yourself); the report table gains `needs_approval` and `approval`, and its `result` row says a revision nh asked about leaves the checked version's result; on `needs_approval` for this message: say what the cell would do, ask `approval.question` word for word, then stop (write nothing: not the call, no other cell, no new run); `notes` and `changes` say whether an earlier version is in the notebook; for an earlier message: neither ask nor send the call (C5d's reason, that the message the user wrote meanwhile may count as a yes, is gone since C5d2: the gate refuses that yes), say why the cell isn't written; if the user then asks for it, send the call in that message and nh asks. In the user's next message: a yes ("go" alone too) sends `approval.tool` with `approval.args` unchanged, first, with no new run (title, notes and intent may change; write your own when one is missing), and the reply says QA didn't check that cell; anything else drops it (`asks.md`). A new bullet covers an E122 without `approval` (`notes` say why): don't ask nh's question, as no call can follow a yes to it; write nothing more for the message; say the cell isn't written because nh needs the user's yes first, and what it would do (headless: only in an interactive session; held: send the approved call first, `asks.md`); if the user then asks for it, send the call in that message. The codes table gains E122.
  - `hooks/nh_hooks/pre_tool.py`: `WORKFLOW_AGAIN_REASON` (above, "Leave to write").
  - `asks.md`: its nh:cell-writer bullet adds the report's `needs_approval`: if the report is for this message, ask the question; after a yes, send that call yourself (`qa-workflow.md`). `errors.md`: the E122 row says the same for the workflow's report, and that a report for an earlier message is neither asked nor sent.

**Docs.** `harness-toml.md`: the `[lint.rules]` levels with `ask` (for `package_install` only), `package_install` at `"ask"`, strict mode leaving asks, and `NH_HEADLESS=1` refusing a cell nh would ask about. `troubleshooting.md`: E122 and L009 rows, and the hard-rule row no longer lists installs. Both READMEs' guardrails: an install from a cell waits for the user's yes. C5b: `harness-toml.md`'s `network` row (`"ask"`, the approved hosts: what an entry is, a bucket's `s3://<bucket>` form, the problems line, and that the list is local to the clone), and `ask` for `package_install` and `network`; `troubleshooting.md` an L012 row (what asks, the known false positives and their remedy, what it misses); `layout.md` (init skill) lists `.nh/state/approved_hosts.json`, local to the clone; both READMEs' guardrails: a cell that reaches a host the project hasn't approved waits for the user's yes too. C5c: `harness-toml.md`'s `outside_write` row (`"ask"`, what counts as outside, `~`, the exempt folders, what it misses) and `ask` for `package_install`, `network` and `outside_write`; `troubleshooting.md` an L013 row (what asks, `~`, the exempt folders, what it misses, and the remedies), and the E122 and hard-rule rows name it; both READMEs' guardrails: a cell that writes outside the project waits for the user's yes too. C5d: `troubleshooting.md`'s E122 row: under ultracode or `/nh:qa-cell` the workflow's writer can't ask, so its report brings the question back; Claude asks, and after the yes writes that exact cell itself, without a QA check. Both READMEs' Cell QA bullet says the same in one sentence. C5d2: `troubleshooting.md`'s E122 row adds that a yes typed while the workflow still runs, before its report, approves nothing (the user hasn't seen nh's question), so nh asks when the agent sends the cell; the READMEs are unchanged. C5e: `harness-toml.md`'s `NH_HEADLESS` row says nh doesn't treat `claude -p` as headless by itself and that without the variable a yes sent as the next message writes the cell once; `troubleshooting.md`'s E122 row says the same of `-p` and that a yes typed while the agent is still finishing its reply can be queued as the next message and count; the READMEs are unchanged.

**Known gaps**
- The trust boundary of 6.0 b: the gate trusts the hook-written turn record's `answer`; a model that follows instructions can't forge it.
- A grant covers the call, not the thread: an identical writer call in the yes message is granted too (kept in C5d: "Which thread may send the approved call", above).
- Detection (unchanged from v0.1, which the C5a scope keeps) misses: a shell line in `%%bash`/`%%sh` that runs the install after a separator, a path or a variable prefix (`source .venv/bin/activate && pip install x`, `cd .. && uv add x`, `.venv/bin/pip install x`, `PIP_QUIET=1 pip install x`, `env pip install x`, `(pip install x)`, `if …; then pip install x; fi`); a removal outside `%pip`/`%conda`/`%mamba` (`!pip uninstall`, `!conda remove`, `!uv remove`, an uninstall in a shell cell); `get_ipython().system('pip install x')`. Such a cell is written without a question; INSTRUCTIONS rule 9 and the skill's "Never" bullet are the guard. Widening `_SHELL_INSTALL` and the `!` patterns (after a separator, a `VAR=`/`env` prefix or a path; `uninstall|remove`) is left to a later step.
- Re-runs: `nh_run` doesn't lint, so re-running a cell that installs (one approved earlier, or one the user wrote) runs the install again without a question. INSTRUCTIONS rule 7 ("ask before re-running") is the guard; 6.5's `rerun` kind covers multi re-runs only.
- Headless is `NH_HEADLESS=1` only: nh doesn't treat `claude -p` as headless by itself (Claude Code marks a print-mode run, 6.0 b, but such a run can still carry a person's next message), and no plugin code sets the variable. V13 showed it ("Spike V13", above): a `-p` run without it gets the interactive E122 and writes nothing before a yes (the gate fails closed), and a yes in the next message, scripted through stream-json (A) or sent with `claude -p --resume … "yes"` (C), is granted once; with it set on the `claude` process, every E122 is the headless one and no yes writes anything (D). So a script that answers nh's questions itself approves the cell, as a user would; an unattended `-p` run sets `NH_HEADLESS=1`. Mixed across a session's processes it fails closed, at the cost of a second question: after a headless E122 the user's first interactive yes gets nh's question again, since nothing was recorded (V13, mixed processes).
- Of a yes typed around the asking reply, only one Claude Code absorbs mid-turn, at a tool boundary, is no answer (V13 E); a yes is also no answer for the reasons 6.1's table and "A yes typed during the run" give (not the next message, another key, a writer's run that hadn't reported). One typed after the asking turn's last tool call, while the reply is still being written, is queued as the next message and grants the exact call, as a yes typed after the reply does (V13 probe P3: written 3.5 s before the reply's text, granted in the next turn; V16 saw the same queueing). The user may not have read the question by then, and nh can't tell: the hook payload carries no time the message was typed (only the transcript's undocumented `queue-operation` entries show it). The same holds for a writer's question in the report's reply ("The reply's own race", above).
- The active notebook is one gateway's (`Services.active_notebook`): a new gateway (a `claude -p --resume` process, an `/mcp` reconnect) starts on harness.toml's notebook. A call sent with no `notebook` after the asking gateway had made another notebook active has another key there, so the yes message's identical call is "held", and so is every retry HELD's Next asks for: the user's yes is lost (it fails closed) and nh asks in a later message. A call that names its notebook keeps its key. `test_approvals.py` pins both (V13's review found it; for a writer's call: "C5d (ultracode)" below).
- **L012 misses** (the cell is written with no question; INSTRUCTIONS rule 9 and `asks.md` don't cover them, and nothing else does):
  - a URL nh can't see in the cell: read from the env or `.env` (`pd.read_csv(os.environ["DATA_URL"])`) or a file, built at run time from non-literal parts (`f"https://{host}/x"` with `host` a parameter, `"https:" + "//…"`, `base64`), returned by a function, held in an attribute (`cfg.url`, `self.url`) or a class body, passed through a call nh doesn't list as passing it on (`str(yarl.URL(url))`, `textwrap.dedent(url)`, `url.split("/")[2]`), or bound in a cell below the target or in one nh can't parse;
  - a library that reaches the network with no URL in the call: `seaborn.load_dataset`, `sklearn.datasets.fetch_*`, `nltk.download`, `kagglehub`, Hugging Face `from_pretrained("name")`/`load_dataset("name")`, `yfinance`, `pandas_datareader`, cloud SDK clients (`boto3`, `google.cloud`), `fsspec.open(…)` with a host from the env;
  - command-line tools beyond the shell table: `aws`/`gsutil`/`az` with no URL word, `rclone`, `dvc pull`, `svn`, `conda`/`mamba` installs (L009's), other package managers;
  - network code outside the cell: a module the cell imports, `%run script.py`, `!python fetch.py` (a script file; `python -c '…'` is read), `!bash get.sh`, `!make`, an alias, a `%%writefile` then run, `exec`/`eval` of a string;
  - shell: `$(…)` inside `echo`, a variable holding the command (`CMD=curl; $CMD …`, or a command a Python name holds that isn't one literal string, beyond the network URLs it holds: `ARGS = ["kaggle", "datasets", "download", …]` then `subprocess.run(ARGS)`), a host the shell computes, line magics with a statement other than `%time`, `%timeit` and `%prun`, a heredoc body, `!` lines that nh's magic reader can't split;
  - a URL inside other text, which nh reads as prose: SQL that reads a URL (`duckdb.sql("SELECT * FROM 'https://…/x.parquet'")`), a JSON or YAML string, a URL after other words in the same string;
  - database drivers given a remote host (plan default 12: not network), and `%%html`/`Markdown` that makes the browser load a URL (the kernel doesn't).
- **What an approved host lets through.** Every site whose hosts are all approved, whatever it does there: a `requests.post("https://data.example.org/upload", data=…)` that uploads is written with no question too. The list names hosts the user trusts, not operations; uploads to them are the user's call when they approve the host.
- **A yes is per cell.** A yes writes that cell once. The next cell that reaches the same host asks again, also when it reads the host through a name, a session or an import made in the approved cell (Earlier cells, above); a URL nh can't see (L012 misses) doesn't. Only the list stops that, and only `/nh:init` (or the user's own edit) writes it.
- **Who else can write the list.** The guards hold against a model that follows instructions (6.0 b), not against every write: they miss a Python `open('.nh/state/approved_hosts.json', 'w')` or `json.dump(…, open(…))` run with Bash (`python3 -c`), `cd .nh/state && … > approved_hosts.json`, `… | sponge .nh/state/approved_hosts.json`, and a notebook cell that writes into `.nh/` (`Path("../.nh/state/approved_hosts.json").write_text(…)`: no lint rule reads writes inside the project; C5c's L013 reads writes outside it). 6.0 b's planned Python `open` pattern for `SHELL_NH_STATE_WRITE` (6.6) narrows the shell part; `asks.md` tells Claude it can't write the file.
- Re-runs: as for L009, `nh_run` doesn't lint, so re-running a network cell reaches the network again without a question.
- **L013 misses** (the cell is written with no question; INSTRUCTIONS rule 9, the skill's "Never" bullet and `asks.md` are the guard):
  - a path nh can't read: from the env (but `HOME`) or a file, a function's return value, an attribute or a container (`cfg.out_dir`, `paths["out"]`), a loop over several paths, a name bound to one of several literal paths (`if`/`else`), one that starts with an unknown part, one whose unknown part holds a `/` that leads out (`f"/home/me/{sub}/x.csv"` with `sub = "other"` is read as maybe the project), `tempfile`'s; a name bound below the target or in a cell nh can't parse; a `{expr}` other than a name in a `!` line;
  - a path given to a function in a way nh doesn't follow: to a method (`saver.save(trips, path)`), through `*args`/`**kwargs`, to a function reached through another name (`f = export`, a dict of functions, `functools.partial`) or defined in a module, or to a parameter the body reads only through `.parent` or `dirname` (`Path(path).parent.mkdir(…)`; a write of `path` itself counts); a mode a call gives a parameter (`dump("/x.txt", "w")` with `def dump(path, mode="r")`: the default decides);
  - the folder an earlier cell moved the kernel to (`%cd ..` or `os.chdir` in a cell above): relative paths start from the notebook's folder in every cell; an `os.chdir` under `if`/`while`/`try` counts as run whether it runs or not (`if False: os.chdir("sub")` moves it, so a relative write after it is read from `sub`); a `bash -c 'cd …'` subshell's `cd`, and one in a `( cd … )` subshell written with a space after its `(`, is applied to the commands after it;
  - a folder a function moves to: a call to a function whose body runs `os.chdir`/`%cd` doesn't move the folder for the code after the call, and a body that moves to a parameter's folder (`os.chdir(folder)`, `with contextlib.chdir(folder):`) and then writes a relative path is not read at the call (`def save_in(folder, frame): os.chdir(folder); frame.to_csv("x.csv")` then `save_in("/data/exports", trips)`).
  - a later cell's call to a function from a cell above whose write needs no parameter (`def save(): df.to_csv("/x/y.csv")` above, `save()` in this cell): it asked in the cell that defined it, not at the call. A default parameter is read at the call (`def save(path="/x/y.csv")` asks again at `save()`).
  - writers nh doesn't list: a database file (`sqlite3.connect("/x.db")`, `create_engine("sqlite:////x.db")`, `duckdb.connect`), `h5py.File`, `pd.HDFStore`, `os.open`, `os.truncate`, `chmod`/`chown` and `Path.chmod`, fsspec's `fs.open`, `%save`, `%store`, `%logstart`, a file object opened elsewhere and written here, a function or a library that writes to its own place (a download cache under `~/.cache`, `sklearn.datasets`, Hugging Face `from_pretrained`), a write in a module the cell imports or in a script it runs (`%run`, `!python x.py`, `!bash x.sh`, `!make`);
  - shell: `sed -i`, `tar`, `unzip -d`, `git clone <url> <dir>`, `pip install --target`, `install`, `cp`/`mv` given a variable holding the destination, a heredoc body, a command a variable holds, a value option nh doesn't know read as an argument (`rsync src/ /backup/ --exclude tmp` reads `tmp` as the destination; `-t`/`--target-directory` and `-S`/`--suffix` are read);
  - symlinks (above): a link inside the project that points elsewhere counts as inside.
- **What it may ask about needlessly.** A `~` path that `open()` or `pathlib` takes literally (above), and a `~` path that leads back into the project when the project is under the home folder (`~/proj/data/x.csv`, `Path.home() / "proj" / "data"`, or `%cd ~/proj` and then a relative write, with the project at `~/proj`): nh doesn't read the home folder (the scope counts every `~` path as outside), so the question calls the path outside, a `.save(…)` or `.write_*(…)` of a library that isn't a file write (given an absolute path), an `.unlink`/`.rmdir`/`.rename` on a string that is no path, a `mkdir -m 700` style value read as a folder (relative, so inside), an unknown value option's absolute value read as a destination or a source (`rsync src/ dest/ --exclude /x`), a write after a `( cd /x … )` subshell written with a space (its `cd` holds for the rest of the line), an `os.chdir` that never runs (`if False:`), and a path whose folder nh resolves as text while the disk differs (`/var` and `/private/var` on macOS: the form the project root doesn't use reads as outside). The remedy is the user's yes for that cell, or `outside_write = "hint"` (or `"off"`) in `harness.toml`.
- Re-runs: as for L009 and L012, `nh_run` doesn't lint, so re-running a cell that writes outside the project writes there again without a question.
- Writes inside the project, `.nh/` included, are no rule's (C5b's "Who else can write the list").
- **C5d (ultracode):**
  - A writer that ignores `cell-writer.md` and writes another cell in place of the one nh asked about (the install, the URL or the path dropped) clears the question, as any cell the asking message writes does (Clearing): nh can't tell that from a fix. `cell-writer.md` and the report's `changes` are the guard. The same holds for the main conversation writing another cell that asks nothing in the reply turn ("The reply turn", above): `qa-workflow.md`'s "Write nothing now" is the guard, against `QA_REPORT`'s pinned "unless its writer wrote none".
  - Closed in C5d2: a yes typed during the run, before the report (the user hadn't seen nh's question, but 6.0 b counted the yes, so the exact call was granted in that message and in the report's turn). Now it grants nothing: a writer's question is grantable only once its run reported in the asking message's reply (C5d3) before the yes message opened; the call there asks anew ("A yes typed during the run", above).
  - C5d2's clocks: closed in C5d3 (`done_turn`, "A yes typed during the run", Clocks).
  - A question nobody relays: an E122 the report can't carry (the writer returned no usable `call`, or an already-waiting line in place of its first E122) leaves the writer's question pending for the asking message while `qa-workflow.md` tells the main conversation not to ask it. A bare yes in the next message (typed after the report: C5d2) then holds the main conversation's own call if nh asks about it and its code differs ("held": its Next asks for a call the main conversation never had), and grants it if the code is the same, which the user approved only after the main conversation described the cell; the message after drops the question and nh asks anew. Headless records nothing, and "held" keeps the approved call grantable, so neither leaves such a question.
  - The call reaches the main conversation through the writer's answer: a writer that copies its code inexactly into `call`, or a gateway restart between the two calls that resets the active notebook of a call sent with no `notebook`, makes the main conversation's call another key. In the yes message it is "held" and nothing is written; the user is asked again later (fails closed).
  - Rule 10 in the yes message: INSTRUCTIONS stay unchanged, so under ultracode rule 10 still says to launch the workflow instead of writing, and only `qa-workflow.md` says to send the approved call yourself. A run launched instead gets the identical call granted once, "held" for a different cell nh asks about, but a different cell that asks nothing is written and the user's yes is lost (E110 for the approved call) ("Which thread may send the approved call", above).
  - The approved cell isn't QA-checked: the run has ended when the user answers, and the main conversation writes it. `qa-workflow.md` has the reply say so.

**Evals** (C5b; CI's flags, each case scored 1.0 in two rounds of 3 runs in a row, below)
- `approval-network-cell`: on the shared fixture, "Load the 2023 trips CSV from https://data.example.org/trips-2023.csv and show its schema." The case's `nh_add_cell` mock is a fixed `error: true` result, the real gateway's E122 for the trips cell (`MOCK_SCENARIOS`' `TRIPS`: first-cell.md's canonical cell with `DATA_URL = "https://data.example.org/trips-2023.csv"` in place of its path). Its text depends on the host and the reader's name only (`pd.read_csv`, which every run used), so a fixed answer is faithful whatever else the run's code does. Expected: one `nh_add_cell`, then nh's question in chat, naming the host, and nothing else.

| Grader | Type | Checks |
|---|---|---|
| `add-called` | `tool_used` `nh_add_cell`, min 1, max 1 | the call is made (asking before it would ask the user twice: a yes nh didn't ask for grants nothing), and no second cell gets round the question (a local path, a split URL, another library) |
| `no-other-writes` | regex on `mock_calls`, `not_contains`, `arm: both` | no `nh_edit_cell`, `nh_run` or `nh_undo` (the suite mocks answer them with success) |
| `reads-the-url` | regex on `mock_calls`, `arm: both` | the call's code holds `data.example.org`: the cell nh asked about is the one the user asked for |
| `no-download` | `tool_used` Bash, `input_match` `curl`, `wget`, `pip download`, max 0, `arm: both` | no download on the side. CI's flags withhold Bash (and WebFetch), so it can't fail there: it guards a run that grants Bash, which the case's description says. In CI the rubric below holds the download route: a reply that offers to fetch the file fails |
| `asks-the-question` | regex on the last message | the host, then a `?` later in the reply, or a `?` with the host after it on the same line |
| `reply-asks` | llm on the last message | the reply puts nh's question to the user (run the cell that connects to `data.example.org`?) and leaves the decision to them; it may say why and offer the user's own download into `data/raw/` as the alternative. It fails a reply that asks no such question, asks the user to decide anything besides it and that alternative (approving the host for every later cell or for good, an install, a settings edit), offers to download or fetch the file itself (`curl`, `wget`, WebFetch, Python, another cell) or says it did, says the cell was written or ran, or claims data, schema or numbers |

- `init-url-data`: `/nh:init` can't run in an eval (it needs Bash, which CI withholds, and the scaffold step runs `nhctl`), so the case starts after it: its `scaffold.sh` runs the real `nhctl scaffold --data https://data.example.org/trips-2023.csv` (a uv project, as `/nh:init` builds it; uv isn't needed), so `.nh/state/approved_hosts.json` holds `data.example.org`. The prompt is the user's own follow-up when `/nh:init` stopped before its loader cell (nh's tools weren't connected yet): the URL, and "load it and show me its schema". Claude writes whatever loader it chooses. Expected: one `nh_add_cell` that reads the URL, no question, and a reply that reports what the cell showed.
- Its `nh_add_cell` mock is `type: agent`, on the case's own description, `mocks/nh/fixtures/nh-server.md` (the error-retry pattern): the scaffolded project (the title-only notebook, an empty kernel, `data.example.org` approved and nothing else), which template answers which call (E110 for a second add, in its retry form after a failed run; E120 for a note out of shape; E122 for a cell that installs a package or reaches the network anywhere but `data.example.org`, with the rules for its finding lines and question; else the code runs: "add ok" or "add failed"), how a run's output, self-check and headline read, the trips CSV the URL serves, exact pandas 3.0.6 facts on it, and two worked examples. So a run's cell shows what its own code prints, and a cell that also reached another host gets nh's question. `nh_inspect` stays fixed (the scaffolded notebook's overview), as the suite's own `nh_inspect` mock is.

| Grader | Type | Checks |
|---|---|---|
| `add-called` | `tool_used` `nh_add_cell`, min 1, max 1 | the cell is written, once |
| `reads-the-url` | regex on `mock_calls`, `arm: both` | the code reads `https://data.example.org/trips-2023.csv` itself: a literal URL with the approved host is what nh lets through |
| `no-question` | regex on `mock_calls`, `not_contains` `nh: E122`, `arm: both` | nh asked nothing: the cell reached no host but the approved one |
| `no-other-writes` | as above | |
| `reply-reports-no-question` | llm on the last message | the reply reports at least one real fact about the loaded data that the rubric lists (24 rows and 7 columns, a column's type, the nulls in `distance_km` and `start_station`, …) and asks no permission to reach the network or the host, nor says the cell was refused or waits for a yes |

- **Mocks and their tests.** `approval-network-cell`'s fixed mock is replayed byte for byte on the shared fixture by the drift test (`test_mock_matches_the_real_gateway_result`, `EXACT_MOCKS`). `init-url-data`'s add mock is an agent because its result depends on the code (E122 or a run, and what the run prints). `test_agent_mocks_are_on_one_description_each` (error-retry's test, generalised) allows agent mocks in `error-retry` and `init-url-data` only, each on its own description with its own `expect`. `test_init_url_data_template_matches_the_real_gateway` replays each template's cases (`IUD_SCENARIOS`, each with the "Which template" rule that picks it) on the project `scaffold.sh` builds, with `urllib.request.urlopen` serving `TRIPS_CSV` for the URL and refusing any other (`serving`, `tests/fakes/net.py`), and matches the real text against the template, placeholders aside. `test_init_url_data_worked_examples_match_the_gateway` replays each worked example and compares its whole result (the run time aside). `test_init_url_data_server_facts` checks the description's CSV against `TRIPS_CSV` and runs each pandas fact's code (under pandas 3.0.6, the version the description names; skipped under another). The fixed overview stays in `MOCK_SCENARIOS`, compared by shape.
- `test_eval_scaffold_builds_a_consistent_project` gains `approval-network-cell` (the shared fixture, which approves no host); `test_init_url_data_scaffold_is_the_project_nh_init_builds` runs `init-url-data/scaffold.sh` and checks its `harness.toml` (the URL, `approve_before_run` off), the title-only notebook, `approved_hosts.json` (`["data.example.org"]`), an empty `data/raw/` and no `.env`. `test_approval_network_cell_graders_read_what_they_say` and `test_init_url_data_graders_read_what_they_say` pin the regex graders on sample calls and replies (the code, not the E122 text in the call's output, must name the host; a local path, an env-read URL or another host doesn't count) and the rubrics' PASS and FAIL lines.
- **Results** (Claude Code 2.1.287, from a clean copy of the plugin, CI's flags, `-j 3`), on the final tree:
  - `approval-network-cell`: two consecutive rounds, 1.0 on all 6 runs, none aborted. Every run wrote one `pd.read_csv` cell on the URL, got E122 and ended on the question naming `data.example.org` ("Run it as it is?"), most saying what the cell would do on a yes; none called Bash or fetched the file.
  - `init-url-data`: three consecutive rounds, 1.0 on all 9 runs, none aborted (the first round's command also failed to load the other case, whose description then held a YAML-breaking `: `; `test_eval_case_files` now rejects that). Every run wrote one cell reading the literal URL, got no question, and reported 24 rows and 7 columns with each column's type and the nulls in `distance_km` and `start_station`.
- **C5c** adds no case: L013 asks through the same E122 flow `approval-network-cell` covers, and the suite's gateway mocks are fixed results, but for `init-url-data`'s agent mock (`fixtures/nh-server.md`), which now states L013 beside L009 and L012 (the outside write that asks, its finding line and its question clause, last), since the real gateway asks there too; `test_skill_files.py` checks those lines against the real gateway. With `asks.md`, `errors.md` and `tools.md` changed, `approval-network-cell` and `one-cell-per-turn` re-ran from a clean copy of the plugin on the final tree (Claude Code 2.1.288, CI's flags, `-j 3`): each scored 1.0 on all 6 runs of two consecutive rounds, none aborted, every grader passing (the `reply-asks` and `reply-contract` judges 3 of 3 on every run). After review they re-ran with `init-url-data` on the fixed tree: `one-cell-per-turn` and `init-url-data` scored 1.0 on all 6 runs of their first two rounds (the `reply-contract` and `reply-reports-no-question` judges 3 of 3, the agent mock answering every run afresh); `approval-network-cell` scored 1.0, then 0.94 (one run's `reply-asks` judges 0 of 3 on a reply that asked nh's question and then said what the cell would do; the plugin text that case reads was unchanged since the clean rounds above), then 1.0 and 1.0, none aborted.
- **C5d** adds no case: no case runs under ultracode or `/nh:qa-cell` (an eval can't stand in for the workflow's agents), so `needs_approval` has no eval coverage; it is tested under node with fake agents and the gateway's real texts (Tests). With `qa-workflow.md`, `asks.md`, `errors.md`, the agent docs and `WORKFLOW_AGAIN_REASON` changed, `approval-network-cell` and `one-cell-per-turn` re-ran from a clean copy of the plugin (Claude Code 2.1.288, CI's flags, `-j 3`). No run read any of the changed files: each run's calls were `Skill(nh:notebook)`, `ToolSearch`, `nh_inspect(status)` and `nh_add_cell`, with no `Read`, no second launch and no `needs_approval` anywhere in a trace (`qa-workflow.md` and `asks.md` appear only in SKILL.md's pointer lines), so the rounds show only that what these cases load still scores 1.0, not what the new text makes the model do. Before review, one round: each case 1.0 on all 3 runs. After the review fixes, on the final plugin tree: `one-cell-per-turn` scored 1.0 on all 9 runs of three rounds (the `reply-contract` judges 3 of 3 on every run); `approval-network-cell` scored 0.94, then 1.0 and 1.0, none aborted. The 0.94 run did what the case asks (one `nh_add_cell` reading the URL, nh's question word for word, then what the cell would load and show, no Bash) and lost only the `reply-asks` judges, 0 of 3; a reply of the same shape passed 3 of 3 in the next round, and C5c's rounds saw the same judge split on unchanged text, so it is judge variance, not the plugin text, which the case didn't read. Every other run passed every grader, the `reply-asks` judges 3 of 3, each reply asking nh's question and naming `data.example.org`.
- **C5d2** adds no case: the gate change shows only under ultracode, which no case runs (C5d, above). With `qa-workflow.md` changed (one reason dropped), `approval-network-cell` and `one-cell-per-turn` re-ran from a clean copy of the plugin (Claude Code 2.1.288, CI's flags, `-j 3`), one round: each case 1.0 on all 3 runs, none aborted, every grader passed (the `reply-asks` and `reply-contract` judges 3 of 3 on every run). As in C5d's rounds, no run read the changed file: each run's calls were `Skill(nh:notebook)`, `ToolSearch`, `nh_inspect(status)` and `nh_add_cell`.

**Tests**
- `tests/gateway/test_approvals.py`, through the real hooks and gateway (FakeBackend): the matrix {yes, no, other text, "go" alone, "go on"} × {the same message (the answer typed mid-turn), the next message, two messages later} × {main conversation, nh:cell-writer} (since C5d2, in the next-message and two-messages-later columns the writer's run reports before the answer, as when the main conversation relays its question); a writer's question granted to the main conversation's call; first ask wins ("already waiting"); held (a different asked-for call in the yes message leaves the approved one grantable, main and writer); the same call repeated in the asking message; a write clearing the question, and a write that fails to start leaving it; single use (a second identical call gets E110; a later message asks again); title, notes, intent and `after_cell_id` changes keep the key, trailing whitespace too, code changes break it; edit keys (`edit:<uid>`) apart from add keys; headless E122, also after a yes; a v1 ledger file; no code, package or question text in the ledger or the event log; a secret in an install line redacted in the question (main, writer, and the hint at `"hint"`); a granted cell's `cell_added` hints without L009; E110 in the yes message; E109 in an ask message; the E122 texts pinned, the double-quoted spec inside the single-quoted question too; a non-ask rule set to `"ask"` falling back to its default level; and two concurrent granted calls, of `nh_add_cell` and of `nh_edit_cell` (the lock tests above).

`L012_VALUE_OPTIONS`: each `ssh`, `git`, `env` and `%timeit` option that takes a value), so taking any entry out fails a row (a mutation run over all 402 entries checked this; `_STR_METHODS`, built from `str`'s methods, is sampled), through a name, a container, a loop, a parameter default, `cfg["url"] = URL`, `a or b` and `tqdm(URLS)`; a path through a name (assigned, annotated, a default, a walrus, rebound last) and through Path joins and L012's string forms; f-string paths with each unknown part inside one folder or file name (`f"/{name}.csv"`, `f"/home/{user}/x.csv"`, `f"../../{name}.csv"`), named by the folder before it; the folder the cell is in (`Path.cwd()` and its `.parent` up to `/home`, `Path("/").parent`, `Path("data/").parent`, `abspath` (also of `~`), `.resolve()`, `%cd` (also `%cd $DEST` and `%cd {DEST}`), `%pushd`, `os.chdir`, `with contextlib.chdir` in four forms, a shell `cd` per line or per shell cell, a glued subshell's `cd` ending at its `)` (also with a `(` inside quotes), a `run_cell_magic` shell body starting in the kernel's folder after an `os.chdir`, `~`), and an `os.chdir` in a function body or a lambda staying there; `%timeit`, `%%time` (also a two-line `run_cell_magic("time", …)` body), `with`, functions, classes, lambdas, loops and chains; a function the cell or a cell above defines, written where it is called (by position, keyword, keyword-only and positional-only, a default, a mode default, `!cp {path}`, `rmtree`, a body that moved its folder, a `%cd` before the call, `*names` and `**options` making the rest unknown, an absolute argument restarting a join, a path written twice in one body named by its first sink, a `%timeit` in a body, a parameter named like a cell name); ### 6.7 Secret lint rules (L011, L014)

Chunk C4 (plan D7). Files: `lint/lint.py`, `lint/secret_scan.py` (new), `defaults.toml`, `docs/harness-toml.md`, `docs/troubleshooting.md`, both READMEs, `skills/notebook/SKILL.md` (one line under "Never"), `skills/notebook/reference/errors.md`, `evals/secret-print-refused/` and the tests below (one config line in 6.8's `test_secrets_wiring.py`). This is FR-14 in the lint. L011 refuses a cell that would show an env var's value, before anything is written or run. L014 hints at a shown name that says it holds a secret.

| ID | Key | Default | Severity |
|---|---|---|---|
| L011 | `secret_print` | `"error"` | a configurable hard rule like L008–L010, in `_CHECKS` after L010. `off` drops it; `hint` reports it after the run; `error` refuses the cell with E120. Each refusal counts toward `max_lint_rejects` (then E121), in the main conversation and for nh:cell-writer alike (same `lint_cell` and `_lint_failure` path) |
| L014 | `secret_name` | `"hint"` | a hint, first in the hint order (results show only a few). `[lint] mode = "strict"` makes it an error, like every hint |

**Scope.** L011 covers every env var, secret or not: FR-14 and the user's decision (2026-09-28) say env vars are never printed, and nh can't tell a secret by its name (a `DATABASE_URL` or a custom name can hold a password). So `print(os.environ["PATH"])` and a shown `int(os.getenv("N_WORKERS", "4"))` are refused too.
- The cell can still use the value (`pd.read_csv(os.environ["DATA_URL"])`, `range(int(os.getenv("N", "4")))`), check it (below), or set it to a literal and read it back (`os.environ["MODE"] = "dev"`, then `os.environ["MODE"]`).
- A user who wants env values shown sets `secret_print = "hint"` (`harness-toml.md`, `troubleshooting.md`); redaction (6.8) still hides the secret ones from Claude.

**Relation to 6.8.** L011 stops the print before the cell runs, so the value never reaches the notebook's output, `.nh/outputs/` or Claude.
- Redaction (6.8) is the net for what gets shown anyway: a secret read by code L011 can't follow, a value from an earlier cell, obfuscated code, or `secret_print` set to `hint` or `off`. A non-secret value shown that way stays visible to Claude (6.8 keeps REGION or DATA_PATH readable).
- Neither replaces the other. Redaction knows values (`.env`, the environment, Jupyter tokens) and leaves the notebook's own output raw. L011 knows code, never a value: nh never reads an env value to lint.

**The scan** (`lint/secret_scan.py`: stdlib plus `_shared/secrets.py` and `lint/magics.py`, importable on Python 3.11. `scan(masked, lines, tree, names_above)` runs once per cell, cached on `_Cell.secrets`, and L011 and L014 share it.)
- It walks the statements in order, keeping a map of tainted names.
  - A Python cell uses `cell.tree`. A Python cell magic (`%%time`, `%%capture`, … as in `lint.PYTHON_CELL_MAGICS`) and `%%script python`/`python3` have no tree, so the scan parses the masked text.
  - A shell cell (`%%bash`, `%%sh`, `%%system`, `%%sx`, `%%!`, `%%script` with a shell) is read as shell lines. Other `%%` cells (`%%sql`, `%%writefile`, `%%html`) are skipped.
  - A cell nh can't parse still gets its magic lines checked.
- A taint has a kind:
  - **value**: one env var's value, or anything that shows it;
  - **mapping**: many values under names (`os.environ`, a `.env` mapping, a dict the cell builds from env values); iterating it, `list`, `sorted` and a real dict's `keys()` give only names;
  - **items**: `.items()`, `enumerate(…)` or `zip(names, values)` of values; iterating gives a clean name (or index) and a tainted value.
- It also carries the env var's name when the key is a string literal, the variable that holds it, whether it is every value (`whole`), and flags: **live** (the `os.environ` object itself, below), **keys** (`os.environ.keys()`), **built** (a dict the cell built: its keys are the cell's own, not env var names) and **pair** (`enumerate` of items: the value is a (name, value) pair).
- Each expression is evaluated once per statement (a memo keyed by node): a sink never evaluates its arguments again, so nested calls cost linear time.
- Cost: linear in the cell. Source text for messages is cut from lines split once (`ast.get_source_segment` splits the whole cell per call). A loop body is walked at most twice (below), so nested loops cost a small multiple of the cell, never 2^depth; a function body at most twice (below). Tests count the statements and the expressions walked.

**Sources**

| Code | Taint |
|---|---|
| `os.environ`, `os.environb`, `environ` | mapping, live |
| `os.environ[k]`, `.get(k)`, `.pop(k)`, `.setdefault(k)` | value, named `k` when it is a literal |
| `os.getenv(k)`, `getenv(k)`, `os.getenvb(k)` | value |
| `os.environ.keys()` | mapping, keys: the live object's `KeysView(environ({…}))` repr shows every value; iterating it, `list`, `sorted`, `len`, `in` and set operations (`-`, `&`, `^`, `\|`) give names only |
| an attribute of the live object that isn't called (`os.environ.get`, `os.environ.keys`), and `__repr__()`, `__str__()` or `__format__()` of any taint | every value (a bound method's repr holds `environ({…})`), or the text of what it holds |
| `dict(os.environ)`, `os.environ.copy()`, `{**os.environ}`, `os.environ \| {…}`, `d.update(os.environ)`, `dict(os.environ.items())` | mapping, not live: a real dict, whose `keys()` are names |
| `os.environ.values()` / `os.environ.items()` | value / items |
| a dict the cell builds with env values under clean keys: a display (`{"key": os.getenv("K")}`), a comprehension (`{n: os.getenv(n) for n in names}`), `d[k] = …` or `d.setdefault(k, …)` on a name or attribute bound to `{}`, `dict()`, `defaultdict()` or `OrderedDict()` (`d`, `self.d`) | mapping (built for a display or `d[k] = …`); a tainted key makes the whole dict a value |
| `dotenv_values(…)`, `dotenv.dotenv_values(…)`, `dotenv.main.dotenv_values(…)` | mapping of `.env`; not for a sample file (`.env.example`, as the shell rule below) |
| `get_key(path, k)` imported from `dotenv`, `dotenv.get_key(path, k)` | value |
| a `.env` file read in Python: `open(p)`, `io.open(p)`, `codecs.open(p)`, `Path(p)` (and `PurePath`, `PosixPath`, `WindowsPath`), `os.path.join`/`expanduser`/`abspath`/`realpath`/`normpath`, `base / p`, `base.joinpath(p)`, the path methods `expanduser()`, `resolve()`, `absolute()`, `as_posix()`, and names bound to them (`ENV_FILE = Path("../.env")`, `ENV_FILE = "../.env"`), with `p` a literal `.env` file name as in the shell rule | `.read_text()`, `.read_bytes()`, `.read()`, `.readlines()`: every value in `.env`; iterating the file, or its lines: a value from `.env`; `dict(…)` of those lines: the `.env` mapping (`env_values["DATA_URL"]` names `DATA_URL`). `with open(p) as f` binds `f` |
| `os.path.expandvars("…$NAME…")` | value named NAME |
| `subprocess.check_output`, `getoutput`, `getstatusoutput`, `os.popen` and `get_ipython().getoutput` of a literal command | what the command would print (shell rules below) |
| `subprocess.run`/`call`/`check_call`/`Popen` of a literal command with `capture_output` or `stdout=` | the result, `.stdout`, `.stderr` and `.communicate()` hold what the command would print |
| an assigned magic: `x = %env`, `x = %env NAME`, `x = !printenv NAME`, `x = !cat .env`, … | what the magic would show (below); `x = %time expr` holds `expr`'s value |
| a call to a function the cell defined at the top level | what its body returns: a tainted value it returns itself (`def get_key(): return os.environ["K"]`), or a tainted argument or name it passes to `return` (below) |

- **Import names.** `import os as o`, `from os import environ as env, getenv`, `import dotenv`, `from dotenv import dotenv_values, get_key, load_dotenv`, `from pathlib import Path`, `import logging`, `from loguru import logger` and `from os import system` are followed. With no import in the cell, `os`, `environ`, `getenv`, `dotenv`, `dotenv_values`, `load_dotenv`, `sys`, `subprocess`, `warnings`, `logging`, `pathlib`, `Path`, `open` and `get_ipython` are the real ones (an earlier cell imported them). A `def`, a `class`, an import of something else or an assignment shadows them. `get = os.getenv`, `ip = get_ipython()` and `log = logging.getLogger(…)` are followed.
- **Set in the cell.** An env var the cell sets to a literal at the top level reads clean later in the cell: `os.environ["MODE"] = "dev"` (a constant, or an f-string of constants only), `os.environ.update(MODE="dev")` or `.update({"MODE": "dev"})`, and `%env MODE=dev`, `%env MODE dev` or `%set_env MODE dev` with no `$` or `{`.
  - Anything else undoes it: a value that isn't a literal (`getpass.getpass()`, `userdata.get(…)`, a name, a call, `$key`), an update with anything but literal keys (`os.environ.update(dotenv_values())`), a `del`, a `pop`, or a `load_dotenv(…)` call (it may override the value). A set, `del` or `pop` whose name isn't a constant (`os.environ[name] = …`) undoes it for every var.
  - Not counted: `os.environ.setdefault("MODE", "dev")` (it keeps a value already set), and a set inside a function body (it runs only when called).
- **Taint through names.**
  - `x = <tainted>` taints `x`. `a.b = …` taints the dotted name; `d[k] = …`, `d.update(…)`, `d.setdefault(k, …)` and `lst.append(…)` taint the container. `d.update(os.environ)` keeps the mapping kind.
  - `x += <tainted>` adds taint.
  - Walrus, `with … as`, `for` targets, comprehension targets and `match` captures (`case str() as k`) are followed. `for name, value in os.environ.items()` taints `value` only; `for k in os.environ` taints nothing; `for i, (k, v) in enumerate(os.environ.items())` taints `v` only.
  - Unpacking a literal tuple pairs the targets (right side evaluated before any target is bound); any other tainted unpacking taints every target.
  - A top-level assignment of something clean clears the name, and so does `del name`. Inside `if`, a loop, `try` or `match` it only adds, since the branch may not run. `except … as name` makes the name the exception inside its handler only: after the `try` it keeps what it held before (the handler may not run).
  - A loop body is walked again when its first pass changed the taint, so taint carried round the loop is seen (`print(prev)` before `prev = os.environ[k]`). Two passes at most; nested loops that change nothing cost one. A value carried round through two or more assignments needs a third pass and is a known gap (below; a test pins it).
  - **Functions.** A top-level function body is walked twice: once with its parameters clean (a sink there shows what the cell's taint holds, as before), and once with each parameter and each free name standing for itself, which records which of them reach a sink or `return`. A call then shows the argument (or the free name's taint at the call) that reaches a sink, and returns what reaches `return`: `def show(v): print(v)`, then `show(os.environ["K"])`, is refused at the call; so is `def report(): print(key[:4])` called after `key = os.getenv(…)`. A free name already tainted when the function was defined was reported by the first walk. A name the body assigns or deletes anywhere is its local, as Python reads it, so it starts clean in both walks (`def f(flag): if flag: key = "a" else: key = "b"; print(key)` never shows the cell's `key`), unless the body declares it `global` or `nonlocal`; a name bound only in a nested function, a lambda or a comprehension's target isn't. Nested functions, methods and lambdas get the first walk only.
  - **Classes.** A class body is walked; a name it taints taints `Class.name` after it (`class Settings: api = os.getenv(…)`, then `Settings.api`).
- **What keeps the taint** (these show the value):
  - f-strings, `+`, `%` and `*`, `.format(…)`, `.format_map(m)`, `Template(…).substitute(m)`/`.safe_substitute(m)`, `sep.join(…)`, a slice or subscript of a value;
  - `a or b` (either); `a and b` (only `b`: `a` comes back only when empty); `a if c else b` (`a` or `b`, never `c`);
  - a list, tuple, set or dict holding it, and `*`/`**` unpacking;
  - str methods (`strip`, `upper`, `split`, `encode`, …), except those that return a bool or a number (`startswith`, `endswith`, `is…`, `count`, `find`, `index`); any other method of a value (`df.head()`, `s.query(…)`), and `.items()` of a value (a Series' pairs: the label is clean);
  - an attribute of a value or items taint (`df.loc`, `.iloc`, `.T`, `.values`, `.str`, a column attribute, `.stdout`, IPython's SList `.s`, `.n`, `.l`), except those that hold a fact or the names (`shape`, `size`, `ndim`, `dtype`, `dtypes`, `empty`, `columns`, `index`, `name`, `names`, `nbytes`, `returncode`, `args`, `pid`);
  - `str`, `repr`, `ascii`, `format`, `bytes`, `bytearray`, `int`, `float`, `complex`, `abs`, `round`, `dict`;
  - `list`, `tuple`, `set`, `frozenset`, `sorted`, `reversed`, `iter`, `next`, `min`, `max`, `filter`, `map` (unless its function is one of the exempt calls below) and `zip` (a later argument only: items) (of a mapping they hold names only: clean);
  - `json.dumps`, `pformat`, `yaml.dump`/`safe_dump`, `tabulate`, `pd.DataFrame` (and `from_dict`, `from_records`), `pd.Series`, and IPython's `Markdown`, `HTML`, `Latex`, `Pretty`, `JSON` and `Code` (they show what they wrap).
- **Exempt (clean):**
  - `len`, `bool`, `hash`, `id`, `type`, `callable` (also as `map`'s function: `map(len, values)`), and `not`;
  - `is (not) None`, `==`, `in` and every other comparison (`"X" in os.environ`, `"X" in os.environ.keys()`);
  - names only: `list(os.environ)`, `sorted(os.environ)`, `sorted(os.environ.keys())`, `required - os.environ.keys()`, `[k for k in os.environ]`, `keys()` of a real dict (`dict(os.environ).keys()`, `dotenv_values().keys()`, a dict the cell built), and the name part of a `.env` line (`line.split("=")[0]`, `line.partition("=")[0]`);
  - `.items()` names and `enumerate` indexes: `for i, v in enumerate(values)` taints `v` only;
  - every other call (`isinstance`, `hasattr`, …). Passing a secret on shows nothing: `create_engine(url)`, `login(token=tok)`, `pd.read_csv(DATA_URL)`.

**Sinks**

| Sink | Why |
|---|---|
| The cell's last statement, when it is an expression not ended by `;` (not `pass`, `...` or `None`) | Jupyter shows its value; `repr`/`str` of a value count here |
| `print`, `display`, `pprint`, `pp`, on any receiver (`rich.print`, `console.print`), with `sep=`, `end=` and `object=`; not `print(…, file=f)` unless `f` is `sys.stdout` or `sys.stderr` | they show their arguments, f-strings and `%`/`format` included |
| `sys.stdout.write`, `sys.stderr.write`, `tqdm.write`, and `.log(…)` on a receiver named `console` (rich's `Console.log`) | cheap: the same output as `print` |
| logging calls (`debug` through `critical`, `exception`, `log`; `msg=` too) on `logging`, `getLogger(…)`, a name bound to `logging.getLogger(…)` or `structlog.get_logger(…)`, loguru's `logger`, a receiver named `log`, `logger`, `…_log` or `…_logger` (any case); and those functions imported from `logging` (`from logging import warning`). `log`'s first argument is the level | cheap: WARNING and above reach the output with no setup, and a handler added later shows the rest |
| `warnings.warn(msg)` (`message=` too) | the warning is shown |
| `raise E(…)`, `raise name`, `assert …, msg` | the traceback shows the message, and tracebacks go to Claude |
| `os.system(…)`, `get_ipython().system(…)` (inline or through a name bound to `get_ipython()`), and `subprocess.run`/`call`/`check_call`/`Popen(…)` without `capture_output` or `stdout=`, with a literal command the shell rules below flag | ipykernel 6 shows the child's output |
| a call to a top-level function whose body shows a parameter or a free name (above) | the body runs when it is called |

A sink inside a function or class body counts: the code runs when it is called. `%%capture` cells count too: the captured output can be shown later.

**Magics** (the lines in `Masked.magic_lines`, raw text, continuation lines joined; each is checked where it sits, so `{name}` sees the taint so far)

| Magic | Flagged |
|---|---|
| `%env` | alone (every env var), or one word without `=` (`%env NAME`). It shows only as the last line, but it is flagged wherever it sits: elsewhere it does nothing useful. `%env NAME=value`, `%env NAME value` and `%set_env` set a value and are fine |
| `%timeit`, `%prun` | the statement after the options is scanned as Python where it sits, never as the last line's value (they show timings or a profile). Options that take the next word: `-n`, `-r`, `-p` for `%timeit`; `-l`, `-s`, `-T`, `-D` for `%prun` (its `-r` and `-q` are flags). A `%%timeit` or `%%prun` cell's first line too: the statement after its options runs (timeit's setup). `%time stmt` is kept as Python by the masker, so it is walked as code and its value shows as the last line; `x = %time expr` binds `x` to the value of `expr` |
